## Conclusion / 结论
===Original===

It is possible to portray a great social movement of the past with accuracy and in detail if one can spare to the task the time necessary for research and further bring to it a certain power of coordination by which a great mass of detail can be integrated and made one whole.
Such a task is rarely accomplished, but it does not exceed the powers of history.
With regard to the future it is otherwise. No one can say even in its largest aspect or upon its chief structural line what that future will be. He can only present the main tendencies of his time: he can only determine the equation of the curve and presume that that equation will apply more or less to its next developments.
So far as I can judge, those societies which broke with the continuity of Christian civilisation in the sixteenth century﻿—which means, roughly, North Germany and Great Britain﻿—tend at present to the reestablishment of a servile status. It will be diversified by local accident, modified by local character, hidden under many forms. But it will come.
That the mere capitalist anarchy cannot endure is patent to all men. That only a very few possible solutions to it exist should be equally patent to all. For my part, as I have said in these pages, I do not believe there are more than two: a reaction towards well-divided property, or the reestablishment of servitude. I cannot believe that theoretical collectivism, now so plainly failing, will ever inform a real and living society.
But my conviction that the reestablishment of the servile status in industrial society is actually upon us does not lead me to any meagre and mechanical prophecy of what the future of Europe shall be. The force of which I have been speaking is not the only force in the field. There is a complex knot of forces underlying any nation once Christian; a smouldering of the old fires.
Moreover, one can point to European societies which will most certainly reject any such solution of our capitalist problem, just as the same societies have either rejected, or lived suspicious of, capitalism itself, and have rejected or lived suspicious of that industrial organisation which till lately identified itself with “progress” and national well-being.

===Chinese===

只要能为这项工作挤出研究所需的时间，并具备一定的统筹能力，能把大量细节整合为一个整体，人们是有可能准确而详尽地描绘过去的一场伟大社会运动的。
这样的任务很少被完成，但它并不超出历史学的能力。
至于未来，则另当别论。没有人能够说出未来将会怎样，哪怕只就其最大的方面或其主要结构线索而言也不可能。他只能呈现他那个时代的主要趋势：只能确定曲线的方程，并假定这一方程大体上适用于曲线的下一段延展。
依我的判断，那些在十六世纪断绝了基督教文明之连续性的社会——大略地说，即北德意志和不列颠——目前正趋于重建一种奴役身份。它将因各地的偶然因素而多样化，因各地的地方性格而有所修正，隐藏在种种形式之下。但它终将到来。
纯粹的资本主义无政府状态不能延续，这一点人人可见。它的出路只有寥寥几种可能，这一点同样应当人人可见。就我而言，正如我在本书中所说，我相信出路只有两条：或者转向分散良好的财产制，或者重建奴役制。我不相信如今已如此明显走向失败的理论上的集体主义，能够赋予一个真实而有生命的社会以形态。
但是，我坚信工业社会中奴役身份的重建确实正在向我们走来，这并不导致我对欧洲的未来作出贫乏而机械的预言。我一直谈论的那种力量并非场上的唯一力量。在任何一个曾经信奉基督教的民族之下，都有一团复杂的力的纽结；旧日的火焰仍在闷烧。

===Original===

These societies are in the main the same as those which, in that great storm of the sixteenth century﻿—the capital episode in the story of Christendom﻿—held fast to tradition and saved the continuity of morals. Chief among them should be noted today the French and the Irish.
I would record it as an impression (and no more) that the servile state, strong as the tide is making for it in Prussia and in England today, will be modified, checked, perhaps defeated in war, certainly halted in its attempt to establish itself completely, by the strong reaction which these freer societies upon its flank will perpetually exercise.
Ireland has decided for a free peasantry, and our generation has seen the solid foundation of that institution laid. In France the many experiments which elsewhere have successfully introduced the servile state have been contemptuously rejected by the populace, and (most significant!) a recent attempt to register and to “insure” the artisans as a separate category of citizens has broken down in the face of an universal and a virile contempt.
That this second factor in the development of the future, the presence of free societies, will destroy the tendency to the servile state elsewhere I do not affirm, but I believe that it will modify that tendency, certainly by example and perhaps by direct attack. And as I am upon the whole hopeful that the Faith will recover its intimate and guiding place in the heart of Europe, so I believe that this sinking back into our original paganism (for the tendency to the servile state is nothing less) will in due time be halted and reversed.
*Videat Deus*.

===Chinese===

这些社会大体上就是那些在十六世纪那场大风暴——基督教世界史上的关键一幕——中坚守传统、保全了道德之连续性的社会。其中首要的，今天当数法兰西和爱尔兰。
我要把下面这层意思记录下来（仅此而已）：尽管如今普鲁士和英格兰正形成推向奴役国家的强大潮流，但凭借其侧翼这些更自由的社会将不断施加的强大反作用，奴役国家将被修正、被遏制，或许会在战争中败北，其完全确立自身的企图则必将受挫。
爱尔兰已经选择了自由的农民阶级，我们这一代人已经看到这一制度奠定了坚实的基础。在法兰西，那些在别处成功地引入了奴役国家的种种试验，遭到了民众的鄙弃；而且（最具深意的是！）近来一项把手工业者作为一个特殊公民类别加以登记和“保险”的企图，就在一种普遍而阳刚的轻蔑面前碰了壁。
我并不断言，未来发展的这第二个因素——自由社会的存在——会摧毁其他地方的奴役国家趋势；但我相信它会修正这一趋势，肯定通过榜样，或许还会通过直接的打击。而且，正如我总体上抱有希望，相信信仰终将重新赢回它在欧洲心中的那个亲密的、指导性的位置，我也相信，这种向我们的原始异教的后退（因为奴役国家的趋势无非就是这个）终有一天会被遏止并扭转。
*Videat Deus*（愿上帝明鉴）。

===Original===

To

**E. S. P. Haynes**

===Chinese===

献给

**E. S. P. 海因斯（E. S. P. Haynes）**

## I Definitions / 第一章 定义
===Original===

What “wealth” is and why necessary to man﻿—How produced﻿—The meaning of the words “capital,” “proletariat,” “property,” “means of production”﻿—The definition of the “capitalist state”﻿—The definition of the “**Servile State**”﻿—What it is and what it is not﻿—The reestablishment of status in the place of contract﻿—That servitude is not a question of degree but of kind﻿—Summary of these definitions.
Man, like every other organism, can only live by the transformation of his environment to his own use. He must transform his environment from a condition where it is less to a condition where it is more subservient to his needs.
That special, conscious, and intelligent transformation of his environment which is peculiar to the peculiar intelligence and creative faculty of man we call the “production of wealth.”
“Wealth” is matter which has been consciously and intelligently transformed from a condition in which it is less to a condition in which it is more serviceable to a human need.
Without wealth man cannot exist. The production of it is a necessity to him, and though it proceeds from the more to the less necessary, and even to those forms of production which we call luxuries, yet in any given human society there is a certain *kind* and a certain *amount* of wealth without which human life cannot be lived: as, for instance, in England today, certain forms of cooked and elaborately prepared food, clothing, warmth, and habitation.
Therefore, to control the production of wealth is to control human life itself. To refuse man the opportunity for the production of wealth is to refuse him the opportunity for life; and, in general, the way in which the production of wealth is by law permitted is the only way in which the citizens can legally exist.
Wealth can only be produced by the application of human energy, mental and physical, to the forces of nature around us, and to the material which those forces inform.
This human energy so applicable to the material world and its forces we will call “labour.” As for that material and those natural forces, we will call them, for the sake of shortness, by the narrow, but conventionally accepted, term “land.”
It would seem, therefore, that all problems connected with the production of wealth, and all discussion thereupon, involve but two principal original factors, to wit, labour and land.

===Chinese===

“财富”是什么、为何为人所必需——财富如何生产——“资本”“无产阶级”“财产”“生产资料”诸词的含义——“资本主义国家”的定义——“**奴役国家（Servile State）**”的定义——它是什么、不是什么——以身份重建取代契约——奴役不是程度问题而是种类问题——以上定义的总结。
人如同其他一切生物一样，只有把他的环境改造得合乎己用才能生存。他必须把环境从较少满足其需要的状态改造为较多满足其需要的状态。
这种对他所处环境的特殊的、自觉的、有智识的改造，乃是人所特有的智力和创造能力之所为，我们称之为“财富的生产”。
“财富”是经过自觉而明智的改造、从不甚合于人类需要的状态变为更合于人类需要的状态的物质。
没有财富，人就无法生存。财富的生产对人来说是必需的；虽然它由必需程度较高的东西逐步扩展到必需程度较低的东西、甚至扩展到我们称之为奢侈品的那些生产，但在任何一个给定的人类社会中，总有一定的*种类*和一定的*数量*的财富，没有它们人类生活便无从谈起：例如在今天的英国，某些形式的经烹饪精心制作的食物、衣物、取暖和居所。
因此，控制财富的生产，就是控制人类生活本身。拒绝给人以生产财富的机会，就是拒绝给他生活的机会；而且一般说来，法律所允许的财富生产方式，就是公民能够赖以合法存在的唯一方式。
财富只有靠人的能量——脑力的和体力的——施加于我们周围的自然力以及这些力量所贯注的物质，才能生产出来。
这种可以施加于物质世界及其力量的能量，我们称之为“劳动”。至于那种物质和那些自然力，为简便起见，我们用那个虽褊狭但约定俗成的词来称呼它们：“土地”。
因此，与财富的生产有关的一切问题及其讨论，看来都只涉及两个主要的基本因素，即劳动与土地。

===Original===

But it so happens that the conscious, artificial, and intelligent action of man upon nature, corresponding to his peculiar character compared with other created beings, introduces a third factor of the utmost importance.
Man proceeds to create wealth by ingenious methods of varying and often increasing complexity, and aids himself by the construction of “implements.” These soon become in each new department of the production as truly necessary to that production as labour and land. Further, any process of production takes a certain time; during that time the producer must be fed, and clothed, and housed, and the rest of it. There must therefore be an “accumulation of wealth” created in the past, and reserved with the object of maintaining labour during its effort to produce for the future.
Whether it be the making of an instrument or tool, or the setting aside of a store of provisions, labour applied to land for either purpose is not producing wealth for immediate consumption. It is setting aside and reserving somewhat, and that *somewhat* is always necessary in varying proportions according to the simplicity or complexity of the economic society to the production of wealth.
To such wealth reserved and set aside for the purposes of future production, and not for immediate consumption, whether it be in the form of instruments and tools, or in the form of stores for the maintenance of labour during the process of production, we give the name of “capital.”
There are thus three factors in the production of all human wealth, which we may conventionally term land, capital, and labour.
When we talk of the “means of production” we signify land and capital combined. Thus, when we say that a man is “dispossessed of the means of production,” or cannot produce wealth save by the leave of another who “possesses the means of production,” we mean that he is the master only of his labour and has no control, in any useful amount, over either capital, or land, or both combined.
A man politically free, that is, one who enjoys the right before the law to exercise his energies when he pleases (or not at all if he does not so please), but not possessed by legal right of control over any useful amount of the means of production, we call “proletarian,” and any considerable class composed of such men we call a “proletariat.”

===Chinese===

然而事情恰恰在于：人对自然所施加的那种自觉的、人为的、有智识的作用——对应于他与其他受造物相比的特殊本性——引入了一个极端重要的第三因素。
人靠种种巧妙的方法创造财富，这些方法不断变化且往往日益复杂，并借助“工具”的制造来帮助自己。这些工具很快在每一个新的生产部门中变得同劳动和土地一样为生产所必需。此外，任何生产过程都需要一定时间；在这段时间里，生产者必须吃饭、穿衣、住房等等。因此，必须有一种在过去创造出来、被储备起来以便在为未来进行生产的努力期间维持劳动的“财富积累”。
无论是制造一件器具或工具，还是储存一批给养，为这两种目的之任一而施加于土地的劳动，都不是在生产供直接消费的财富。它是在储存和保留某种东西，而那*某种东西*，依经济社会的简单或复杂程度按不同比例，永远为财富的生产所必需。
对这种为未来的生产而非直接消费的目的而被储备、被保留的财富——无论它采取工具器具的形式，还是采取在生产过程中维持劳动的给养储备的形式——我们给以“资本”之名。
因此，在一切人类财富的生产中有三个因素，我们可以约定俗成地称之为土地、资本和劳动。
当我们谈到“生产资料”时，我们指的是土地与资本的结合。因此，当我们说一个人“被剥夺了生产资料”，或者说他除非经“占有生产资料”的他人允许便不能生产财富时，我们的意思是：他只支配自己的劳动，而对资本、对土地或对两者的结合，都不能支配任何有用的数量。
一个在政治上自由的人——即享有在法律面前随意行使自己精力的权利（如果不愿意，也可以完全不行使）——却不依法律权利占有任何有用数量的生产资料，我们称之为“无产者”；由这样的人组成的任何可观的阶级，我们称之为“无产阶级”。

===Original===

“Property” is a term used for that arrangement in society whereby the control of land and of wealth made from land, including therefore all the means of production, is vested in some person or corporation. Thus we may say of a building, including the land upon which it stands, that it is the “property” of such and such a citizen, or family, or college, or of the state, meaning that those who “own” such property are guaranteed by the laws in the right to use it or withhold it from use. “Private property” signifies such wealth (including the means of production) as may, by the arrangements of society, be in the control of persons or corporations *other* than the political bodies of which these persons or corporations are in another aspect members. What distinguishes private property is not that the possessor thereof is less than the state, or is only a part of the state (for were that so we should talk of municipal property as private property), but rather that the owner may exercise his control over it to his own advantage, and not as a trustee for society, nor in the hierarchy of political institutions. Thus Mr. Jones is a citizen of Manchester, but he does not own his private property as a citizen of Manchester, he owns it as Mr. Jones, whereas, if the house next to his own be owned by the Manchester municipality, they own it only because they are a political body standing for the whole community of the town. Mr. Jones might move to Glasgow and still own his property in Manchester, but the municipality of Manchester can only own its property in connection with the corporate political life of the town.
An ideal society in which the means of production should be in the hands of the political officers of the community we call “collectivist,” or more generally “socialist.”

===Chinese===

“财产”是用来指称社会中这样一种安排的术语：土地以及由土地制成的财富——从而也包括一切生产资料——的控制权归属于某个人或某个法人团体。于是，对一座建筑连同它所路身的土地，我们可以说它是某公民、某家族、某学院或国家的“财产”，意思是那些“拥有”此财产的人受到法律的保障，有权使用它或不让它被使用。“私有财产”指的是这样的财富（包括生产资料）：按照社会的安排，它处于这些人或这些法人团体的控制之下，而不是处于政治团体——这些人和团体在另一层意义上也是政治团体的成员——的控制之下。私有财产区别于其他财产的地方，不在于其占有者小于国家或只是国家的一部分（若如此，我们就该把市政财产也称为私有财产了），而在于所有者可以为自己的利益行使对它的控制权，而不是作为社会的受托人、也不是在政治机构的层级序列之中行使这种控制。因此，琼斯先生是曼彻斯特的公民，但他并不是作为曼彻斯特的公民而拥有自己的私有财产，而是作为琼斯先生而拥有它；反之，如果他隔壁的房子归曼彻斯特市政当局所有，市政当局之拥有它，仅仅因为它是代表全镇共同体的一个政治团体。琼斯先生可以搬到格拉斯哥去，却仍拥有他在曼彻斯特的财产；而曼彻斯特市政当局只能在同该镇的政治共同体生活相联系的情况下拥有其财产。
一个把生产资料置于共同体政治官员手中的理想社会，我们称之为“集体主义的”，或更一般地称之为“社会主义的”。

===Original===

1
A society in which private property in land and capital, that is, the ownership and therefore the control of the means of production, is confined to some number of free citizens not large enough to determine the social mass of the state, while the rest have not such property and are therefore proletarian, we call “capitalist”; and the method by which wealth is produced in such a society can only be the application of labour, the determining mass of which must necessarily be proletarian, to land and capital, in such fashion that, of the total wealth produced, the proletariat which labours shall only receive a portion.
The two marks, then, defining the “capitalist state” are: (1) That the citizens thereof are politically free: i.e. can use or withhold at will their possessions or their labour, but are also (2) divided into capitalist and proletarian in such proportions that the state as a whole is not characterised by the institution of ownership among free citizens, but by the restriction of ownership to a section markedly less than the whole, or even to a small minority. Such a capitalist state is essentially divided into two classes of free citizens, the one capitalist or owning, the other propertyless or proletarian.
My last definition concerns the servile state itself, and since the idea is both somewhat novel and also the subject of this book, I will not only establish but expand its definition.
The definition of the servile state is as follows:﻿—

> That arrangement of society in which so considerable a number of the families and individuals are constrained by positive law to labour for the advantage of other families and individuals as to stamp the whole community with the mark of such labour we call **The Servile State**.

===Chinese===

1
在这样的社会中，土地和资本上的私有财产——即生产资料的所有权及其控制权——局限于一部分自由公民，其人数之少不足以决定这个国家的社会主体，其余的人则没有这种财产、因而是无产的：这样的社会我们称之为“资本主义的”；在这样的社会中财富的生产方式只能是：把劳动——其决定性的主体必然是无产的——施加于土地和资本，而且在所生产的全部财富中，从事劳动的无产阶级只能得到其中的一部分。
因此，界定“资本主义国家”的两个标志是：(一) 其公民在政治上是自由的，即可以随意使用或扣留自己的财产或劳动；但同时又 (二) 按这样的比例分为资本家和无产者，以至于整个国家的特征不是自由公民之间的所有制，而是所有权局限于一个明显小于全体的部分、甚至一个小少数。这样一个资本主义国家本质上分为两个自由公民阶级：一方是资本家即占有者，另一方是无产者。
我的最后一个定义关乎奴役国家本身；由于这个观念既颇为新颖又是本书的主题，我不仅要确立它的定义，还要加以扩展。
奴役国家的定义如下：——

> 社会的这样一种安排，其中相当多数的家庭和个人被实在法所强迫，为其他家庭和个人的利益而劳动，以致整个共同体都带有这种劳动的烙印，我们称之为“**奴役国家**”。

===Original===

Note first certain negative limitations in the above which must be clearly seized if we are not to lose clear thinking in a fog of metaphor and rhetoric.
That society is not servile in which men are intelligently constrained to labour by enthusiasm, by a religious tenet, or indirectly from fear of destitution, or directly from love of gain, or from the common sense which teaches them that by their labour they may increase their well-being.
A clear boundary exists between the servile and the non-servile condition of labour, and the conditions upon either side of that boundary utterly differ one from another, Where there is *compulsion* applicable by *positive law* to men of a certain *status*, and such compulsion enforced in the last resort by the powers at the disposal of the state, there is the institution of *slavery*; and if that institution be sufficiently expanded the whole state may be said to repose upon a servile basis, and is a servile state.
Where such formal, legal status is absent the conditions are not servile; and the difference between servitude and freedom, appreciable in a thousand details of actual life, is most glaring in this: that the free man can refuse his labour and use that refusal as an instrument wherewith to *bargain*; while the slave has no such instrument or power to bargain at all, but is dependent for his well-being upon the custom of society, backed by the regulation of such of its laws as may protect and guarantee the slave.
Next, let it be observed that the state is not servile because the mere institution of slavery is to be discovered somewhere within its confines. The state is only servile when so considerable a body of forced labour is affected by the compulsion of positive law as to give a character to the whole community.
Similarly, that state is not servile in which *all* citizens are liable to submit their energies to the compulsion of positive law, and must labour at the discretion of state officials. By loose metaphor and for rhetorical purposes men who dislike collectivism (for instance) or the discipline of a regiment will talk of the “servile” conditions of such organisations. But for the purposes of strict definition and clear thinking it is essential to remember that a servile condition only exists by contrast with a free condition.

===Chinese===

首先请注意上述定义中的一些否定性限制，若不把它们牢牢抓住，我们的清晰思考就会迷失在隐喻和修辞的雾霭中。
如果一个社会中的人是因为热情、因为某种宗教信条、或者因为对匮乏的恐惧而间接受到约束去劳动，或者因为逐利之心、或者因为常识——常识告诉他们劳动可以增进自己的福祉——而劳动，那么这个社会就不是奴役的。
奴役劳动与非奴役劳动之间存在一条清晰的界线，界线两侧的状况彼此全然不同。凡存在可以由*实在法*施加于某种*身份*的人的*强制*，且这种强制最终靠国家所掌握的权力来维持的地方，就存在*奴隶制*这一制度；如果这一制度扩展得足够广泛，就可以说整个国家奠基于奴役之上，它就是一个奴役国家。
凡缺乏这种正式的、法律的身份的地方，状况就不是奴役的；奴役与自由的差别——在实际生活的千百个细节中都可以察觉——最显著地表现在这一点上：自由人可以拒绝劳动，并把这种拒绝用作*讨价还价*的工具；而奴隶完全没有这样的工具或讨价还价的能力，他的福祉依赖于社会的习俗以及社会法律中对奴隶的保护与保障条款。
其次应当看到，一个国家并不会仅仅因为在它的范围内某处可以发现奴隶制这一制度，它就是奴役的。只有当相当规模的强制劳动受到实在法的强制，以致给整个共同体打上烙印时，这个国家才是奴役的。
同样，如果一个国家中*所有*公民都可能要服从实在法的强制、必须听凭国家官员的裁断去劳动，它也不是奴役的。出于散漫的隐喻和修辞的目的，不喜欢集体主义（比如说）或不喜欢军团纪律的人，会谈论这类组织的“奴役”状况。但为了严格定义和清晰思考，必须记住：奴役状况只有在与自由状况相对照时才存在。

===Original===

The servile condition is present in society only when there is also present the free citizen for whose benefit the slave works under the compulsion of positive law.
Again, it should be noted that this word “servile” in no way connotes the worst, nor even necessarily a bad, arrangement of society. This point is so clear that it should hardly delay us; but a confusion between the rhetorical and the precise use of the word servile I have discovered to embarrass public discussion of the matter so much that I must once more emphasise what should be self-evident.
The discussion as to whether the institution of slavery be a good or a bad one, or be relatively better or worse than other alternative institutions, has nothing whatever to do with the exact definition of that institution. Thus monarchy consists in throwing the responsibility for the direction of society upon an individual. One can imagine some Roman of the first century praising the new imperial power, but through a muddleheaded tradition against “kings” swearing that he would never tolerate a “monarchy.” Such a fellow would have been a very futile critic of public affairs under Trajan, but no more futile than a man who swears that nothing shall make him a “slave,” though well prepared to accept laws that compel him to labour without his consent, under the force of public law, and upon terms dictated by others.
Many would argue that a man so compelled to labour, guaranteed against insecurity and against insufficiency of food, housing and clothing, promised subsistence for his old age, and a similar set of advantages for his posterity, would be a great deal better off than a free man lacking all these things. But the argument does not affect the definition attaching to the word servile. A devout Christian of blameless life drifting upon an ice-flow in the Arctic night, without food or any prospect of succour, is not so comfortably circumstanced as the Khedive of Egypt; but it would be folly in establishing the definition of the words “Christian” and “Muhammadan” to bring this contrast into account.
We must then, throughout this inquiry, keep strictly to the economic aspect of the case. Only when that is established and when the modern tendency to the reestablishment of slavery is clear, are we free to discuss the advantages and disadvantages of the revolution through which we are passing.

===Chinese===

奴役状况只有当社会中同时也存在自由公民——奴隶正是为他们的利益而在实在法的强制下劳动——时才存在。
再者，应当注意，“奴役的”这个词绝不意味着最坏的、甚至不必然意味着坏的社会安排。这一点如此明白，本不该耽搁我们；但我发现，对“奴役”一词的修辞用法与精确用法之间的混淆，使公众对这个问题的讨论如此困惑，以致我必须再次强调本属自明之理。
奴隶制是好是坏、或比其他备选制度相对更好还是更坏的争论，与这一制度的严格定义毫无关系。譬如，君主制在于把指导社会的责任托付于一个个人。可以想象某个一世纪的罗马人赞美新生的帝国权力，却又由于反对“国王”的糊涂传统而发誓绝不容忍“君主制”。这样的人在图拉真治下会是个非常无用的时政批评家；但他的无用，并不超过那种发誓绝不做“奴隶”、却随时准备接受强迫他未经自己同意、凭公共法律的力量、按他人规定的条件去劳动的法律的人。
许多人会争辩说：一个这样被强制劳动、却获得免于困厄和衣食住匮乏的保障、被许诺老年有生路、子孙享有同样一套好处的人，处境要比一个缺乏这一切的自由人好得多。但这种争论并不影响“奴役”一词所附着的定义。一个品行无瑕的虔诚信奉基督教的人，在北极之夜漂流于浮冰之上，没有食物、没有得救的希望，其处境不如埃及赫迪夫舒适；但在确立“基督徒”和“穆斯林”这两个词的定义时，把这种对比考虑进去将是愚蠢的。
因此，在整个这项考察中，我们必须严格坚持问题的经济方面。只有在这一确立之后，只有当重建奴隶制的现代趋势已经清楚之后，我们才有余裕去讨论我们正在经历的那场变革的利弊。

===Original===

It must further be grasped that the essential mark of the servile institution does not depend upon the ownership of the slave by a particular master. That the institution of slavery tends to that form under the various forces composing human nature and human society is probable enough. That if or when slavery were reestablished in England a particular man would in time be found the slave not of capitalism in general but of, say, the Shell Oil Trust in particular, is a very likely development; and we know that in societies where the institution was of immemorial antiquity such direct possession of the slave by the free man or corporation of free men had come to be the rule. But my point is that such a mark is not essential to the character of slavery. As an initial phase in the institution of slavery, or even as a permanent phase marking society for an indefinite time, it is perfectly easy to conceive of a whole class rendered servile by positive law, and compelled by such law to labour for the advantage of another non-servile free class, without any direct act of possession permitted to one man over the person of another.
The final contrast thus established between slave and free might be maintained by the state guaranteeing to the *unfree*, security in their subsistence, to the *free*, security in their property and profits, rent and interest. What would mark the slave in such a society would be his belonging to that set or status which was compelled by no matter what definition to labour, and was thus cut off from the other set or status not compelled to labour, but free to labour or not as it willed.
Again, the servile state would certainly exist even though a man, being only compelled to labour during a portion of his time, were free to bargain and even to accumulate in his “free” time. The old lawyers used to distinguish between a serf “in gross” and a serf “regardant.” A serf “in gross” was one who was a serf at all times and places, and not in respect to a particular lord. A serf “regardant” was a serf only in his bondage to serve a particular lord. He was free as against other men. And one might perfectly well have slaves who were only slaves “regardant” to a particular type of employment during particular hours.

===Chinese===

还必须进一步把握：奴役制度的本质标志并不取决于奴隶归属于某个特定的主人。在构成人性和人类社会的各种力量的作用下，奴隶制趋向于那种形式，这很可能是实情。如果奴隶制在英格兰重建，一个人到头来将不只是笼统的资本主义的奴隶，而是比如说具体沦为壳牌石油托拉斯的奴隶，这是一个很可能的发展；我们也知道，在这一制度源远流长的那些社会里，自由人或自由人团体对奴隶的这种直接占有已经成为惯例。但我要指出的是，这一标志并非奴隶制本性的实质所在。作为奴隶制的一个初始阶段，甚至作为在一个不定时期内标记社会的持久阶段，完全可以设想这样一个整体阶级：它被实在法贬为奴役、被这种法律强制为另一个非奴役的自由阶级的利益而劳动，却不容许任何人对另一人的人身有任何直接的占有行为。
奴隶与自由人之间的这种最终对照，可以这样来维持：国家向*不自由者*保障其生计的安全，向*自由者*保障其财产和利润、地租和利息的安全。在这样一个社会里，标记着一个人是奴隶的，是他属于那个无论依何种定义都被强制劳动的身份，从而与另一个不被强制劳动、可随意劳动或不劳动的身份相隔绝。
再者，即使一个人只在一部分时间里被强制劳动，而在其“自由”时间里可以讨价还价、甚至可以积累，奴役国家也肯定存在。旧时的法学家习惯区分“全然农奴”（serf in gross）与“隶属农奴”（serf regardant）。“全然农奴”在任何时候、任何地方都是农奴，不因某个特定领主之故。“隶属农奴”只是在对某个特定领主的依附上才是农奴；对其他人而言他是自由的。同样完全可以有这样的奴隶：他们只在特定时段、就特定类型的职业而言是“隶属”的奴隶。

===Original===

But they would be slaves none the less, and if their hours were many and their class numerous, the state which they supported would be a servile state.
Lastly, let it be remembered that the servile condition remains as truly an institution of the state when it attaches permanently and irrevocably at any one time to a particular set of human beings as when it attaches to a particular class throughout their lives. Thus the laws of paganism permitted the slave to be enfranchised by his master: it further permitted children or prisoners to be sold into slavery. The servile institution, though perpetually changing in the elements of its composition, was still an unchanging factor in the state. Similarly, though the state should only subject to slavery those who had less than a certain income, while leaving men free by inheritance or otherwise to pass out of, and by loss to pass into, the slave class, that slave class, though fluctuating as to its composition, would still permanently exist.
Thus, if the modern industrial state shall make a law by which servile conditions shall not attach to those capable of earning more than a certain sum by their own labour, but shall attach to those who earn less than this sum; or if the modern industrial state defines manual labour in a particular fashion, renders it compulsory during a fixed time for those who undertake it, but leaves them free to turn later to other occupations if they choose, undoubtedly such distinctions, though they attach to conditions and not to individuals, establish the servile institution.
Some considerable number must be manual workers by definition, and while they were so defined would be slaves. Here again the composition of the servile class would fluctuate, but the class would be permanent and large enough to stamp all society. I need not insist upon the practical effect: that such a class, once established, tends to be fixed in the great majority of those which make it up, and that the individuals entering or leaving it tend to become few compared to the whole mass.
There is one last point to be considered in this definition.
It is this:﻿—
Since, in the nature of things, a free society must enforce a contract (a free society consisting in nothing else but the enforcement of free contracts), how far can that be called a servile condition which is the result of contract nominally or really free?

===Chinese===

但他们依然是奴隶；而如果他们的劳役时数很多、其人数庞大，那么他们所支撑的国家就是一个奴役国家。
最后，请记住：奴役状况每当在某一时点一成不变地、不可撤销地附着于某一群特定的人，就同它终生附着于某个特定阶级一样，仍然真正是国家的一种制度。因此，异教的法律容许主人解放奴隶，也容许把儿童或囚徒卖为奴隶：奴役制度虽然在构成上不断变动，却始终是国家中一个不变的因素。同样，即使国家只把收入低于某一水准的人贬为奴隶，同时听凭人们凭继承或其他方式脱离奴隶阶级、因破产而坠入奴隶阶级，这个奴隶阶级虽然构成上波动不定，却仍将永久存在。
因此，如果现代工业国家制定一条法律：奴役状况不适用于那些凭自身劳动能挣得超过某个数额的人，而适用于挣得少于这个数额的人；或者，现代工业国家以某种特定方式定义体力劳动，使承担它在固定时期内成为强制，但期满后听凭他们自由转往其他职业——那么，这些区分虽然附着于状况而非个人，毫无疑问确立了奴役制度。
总有相当多的人按定义必须是体力劳动者，而只要他们如此被定义，他们就是奴隶。在这里，奴役阶级的构成同样会波动，但这个阶级将永久存在，而且大到足以给整个社会打上烙印。其实际效果无须我强调：这样一个阶级一经确立，就趋于把其中的绝大多数固定下来，进出这个阶级的个人与整个群体相比趋于寥寥。
在这个定义中还有最后一点需要考虑。
就是这一点：——
既然按事物的本性，一个自由社会必须强制执行契约（自由社会无非就在于对自由契约的执行），那么一种名义上或实际上自由的契约所产生的状况，在多大程度上能被称为奴役的呢？

===Original===

In other words, is not a contract to labour, however freely entered into, servile of its nature when enforced by the state?
For instance, I have no food or clothing, nor do I possess the means of production whereby I can produce any wealth in exchange for such. I am so circumstanced that an owner of the means of production will not allow me access to those means unless I sign a contract to serve him for a week at a wage of bare subsistence. Does the state in enforcing that contract make me for that week a slave?
Obviously not. For the institution of slavery presupposes a certain attitude of mind in the free man and in the slave, a habit of living in either, and the stamp of both those habits upon society. No such effects are produced by a contract enforceable by the length of one week. The duration of human life is such, and the prospect of posterity, that the fulfilling of such a contract in no way wounds the senses of liberty and of choice.
What of a month, a year, ten years, a lifetime? Suppose an extreme case, and a destitute man to sign a contract binding him and all his children who were minors to work for a bare subsistence until his own death, or the attainment of majority of the children, whichever event might happen latest; would the state in forcing that contract be making the man a slave?
As undoubtedly as it would not be making him a slave in the first case, it would be making him a slave in the second.
One can only say to ancient sophistical difficulties of this kind, that the sense of men establishes for itself the true limits of any object, as of freedom. What freedom is, or is not, in so far as mere measure of time is concerned (though of course much else than time enters in), human habit determines; but the enforcing of a contract of service certainly or probably leaving a choice after its expiration is consonant with freedom. The enforcement of a contract probably binding one’s whole life is not consonant with freedom. One binding to service a man’s natural heirs is intolerable to freedom.
Consider another converse point. A man binds himself to work for life and his children after him so far as the law may permit him to bind them in a particular society, but that not for a bare subsistence, but for so large a wage that he will be wealthy in a few years, and his posterity, when the contract is completed, wealthier still.

===Chinese===

换言之，一个劳动契约，无论缔结得多么自由，当它由国家强制执行时，难道在本性上不是奴役的吗？
举例来说：我没有食物和衣服，也不占有生产资料，因而无法生产任何财富去换取它们。我的处境是：一个生产资料的占有者不容我使用那些资料，除非我签署一份以仅够糊口的工资为他服役一周的契约。国家在强制执行这份契约时，是否就把那一周的我变成了奴隶？
显然不是。因为奴隶制的前提是自由人和奴隶双方都具有某种心态、某种生活习惯，以及这两种习惯给社会打上的烙印。一份以一周为期的可执行契约产生不了这种效果。人生的长度如此，前途如此，履行这样一份契约丝毫无损于自由感和选择感。
那么一个月呢？一年、十年、一辈子呢？设想一个极端的情形：一个赤贫的人签署一份契约，约束他自己和他所有未成年的子女，以仅够糊口的代价劳动到他去世或子女成年——以后发生者为准；国家在强制执行这份契约时，是否把这个男人变成了奴隶？
正如在第一种情形下国家无疑没有使他成为奴隶，在第二种情形下国家无疑会使他成为奴隶。
对这类古老的诡辩式难题，只能说：人的常识会为任何事物——也为自由——自行确立真正的界限。就单纯的时间尺度而言（当然，起作用的远不止时间），自由是什么、不是什么，由人的习惯决定；但强制执行一份在期满后肯定或很可能留有选择的服役契约，与自由相容；强制执行一份很可能束缚一个人整个一生的契约，与自由不相容；而把一个人的亲生继承者也束缚于服役的契约，则是自由所不能容忍的。
再考虑一个相反的问题。一个人按特定社会法律所容许的最大限度，约束自己终身劳役、其子女随其后继续服役，但不是为仅够糊口的报酬，而是为如此丰厚的一份工资，以至于几年之内他将变得富有，而在契约履行完毕时，他的后代将更加富有。

===Original===

Does the state in forcing such a contract make the fortunate employee a slave? No. For it is in the essence of slavery that subsistence or little more than subsistence should be guaranteed to the slave. Slavery exists in order that the *free* should benefit by its existence, and connotes a condition in which the men subjected to it may demand secure existence, but little more.
If anyone were to draw an exact line, and to say that a life-contract enforceable by law was slavery at so many shillings a week, but ceased to be slavery after that margin, his effort would be folly. None the less, there is a standard of subsistence in any one society, the guarantee of which (or little more) under an obligation to labour by compulsion is slavery, while the guarantee of very much more is not slavery.
This verbal jugglery might be continued. It is a type of verbal difficulty apparent in every inquiry open to the professional disputant, but of no effect upon the mind of the honest inquirer whose business is not dialectic but truth.
It is always possible by establishing a cross-section in a set of definitions to pose the unanswerable difficulty of degree, but that will never affect the realities of discussion. We know, for instance, what is meant by torture when it exists in a code of laws, and when it is forbidden. No imaginary difficulties of degree between pulling a man’s hair and scalping him, between warming him and burning him alive, will disturb a reformer whose business it is to expunge torture from some penal code.
In the same way we know what is and what is not compulsory labour, what is and what is not the servile condition. Its test is, I repeat, the withdrawal from a man of his free choice to labour or not to labour, here or there, for such and such an object; and the compelling of him by positive law to labour for the advantage of others who do not fall under the same compulsion.
Where you have *that*, you have slavery: with all the manifold, spiritual, and political results of that ancient institution.
Where you have slavery affecting a class of such considerable size as to mark and determine the character of the state, there you have the servile state.

===Chinese===

国家在强制执行这样的契约时，是否使这个幸运的雇工成了奴隶？不是。因为奴隶制的本质在于：奴隶得到保障的是生计或仅略高于生计的东西。奴隶制的存在是为了让*自由人*从它的存在中获益，它意味着受它支配的人可以要求得到有保障的生存，但也仅此而已。
如果有人要划出一条精确的界线，说一份可由法律强制执行的终身契约，在每周若干先令以下是奴隶制，超过这个界限就不再是奴隶制，那他的努力是徒劳的。尽管如此，在任何一个社会中都存在一个生存标准：在强制劳动的义务下保障这一标准（或仅略高于它）就是奴隶制，而保障远高于它的东西则不是奴隶制。
这种文字游戏可以继续玩下去。这是一切向职业辩士敞开的探究中都会出现的文字难题，但对诚实的探究者的心智毫无影响——他的事业不是辩证法，而是真理。
在一套定义中取一个横截面，从而提出“程度”这个无法回答的难题，这永远是可能的；但它永远不会影响讨论的实质。譬如，当酷刑存在于一部法典之中、当它被禁止时，我们都知道酷刑指的是什么。揪一个人的头发与剥他的头皮之间、给他取暖与把他活活烧死之间的种种想象的程度难题，绝不会困扰一个以从某部刑法典中删去酷刑为业的改革者。
同样，我们知道什么是强制劳动、什么不是，什么是奴役状况、什么不是。我再重复一遍，它的检验标准是：剥夺一个人是否劳动、在此处还是彼处劳动、为此目的还是彼目的劳动的自由选择；并以实在法强制他为不承受同样强制的其他人的利益而劳动。
哪里有*这个*，哪里就有奴隶制，连同那一古老制度的一切纷繁的、精神的和政治的后果。
哪里有这样一种波及一个其规模足以标记并决定国家性格的阶级的奴隶制，哪里就有奴役国家。

===Original===

---
To sum up, then:﻿—The **Servile State** is that in which we find so considerable a body of families and individuals distinguished from *free citizens* by the mark of compulsory labour as to stamp a general character upon society, and all the chief characters, good or evil, attaching to the institution of slavery will be found permeating such a state, whether the slaves be directly and personally attached to their masters, only indirectly attached through the medium of the state, or attached in a third manner through their subservience to corporations or to particular industries. The slave so compelled to labour will be one dispossessed of the means of production, and compelled by law to labour for the advantage of all or any who are possessed thereof. And the distinguishing mark of the slave proceeds from the special action upon him of a positive law which first separates one body of men, the less-free, from another, the more free, in the function of contract within the general body of the community.
Now, from a purely servile conception of production and of the arrangement of society we Europeans sprang. The immemorial past of Europe is a servile past. During some centuries which the Church raised, permeated, and constructed, Europe was gradually released or divorced from this immemorial and fundamental conception of slavery; to that conception, to that institution, our industrial or capitalist society is now upon its return. We are reestablishing the slave.
---
Before proceeding to the proof of this, I shall, in the next few pages, digress to sketch very briefly the process whereby the old pagan slavery was transformed into a free society some centuries ago. I shall then outline the further process whereby the new non-servile society was wrecked at the Reformation in certain areas of Europe, and particularly in England. There was gradually produced in its stead the transitory phase of society (now nearing its end) called generally “capitalism” or the “capitalist state.”
Such a digression, being purely historical, is not logically necessary to a consideration of our subject, but it is of great value to the reader, because the knowledge of how, in reality and in the concrete, things have moved better enables us to understand the logical process whereby they tend towards a particular goal in the future.

===Chinese===

---
总结如下：——**奴役国家**是这样的国家：其中相当多数的家庭和个人以强制劳动为标志而区别于*自由公民*，以致给整个社会打上了普遍的性格特征；而附着于奴隶制制度的一切主要特征——无论善恶——都将发现已渗透于这样一个国家之中，无论奴隶是直接地、人身地隶属于主人，还是仅通过国家这一中介间接地隶属于主人，还是以第三种方式，通过服从于法人团体或特定产业而隶属于主人。这样被强制劳动的奴隶，是一个被剥夺了生产资料、却被法律强制为一切或任何占有生产资料者的利益而劳动的人。奴隶的标志性特征，来自实在法对他的特殊作用：这种法律首先在共同体的总体之内，把一批人即较不自由者，与另一批人即较自由者，在契约职能上区分开来。
而我们欧洲人正是从一种纯粹奴役的生产观念和社会安排中诞生的。欧洲源远流长的过去是一个奴役的过去。在教会所提升、渗透和建造的那几个世纪里，欧洲逐渐摆脱——或者说离弃——了这一源远流长的、根本性的奴隶制观念；而我们的工业社会即资本主义社会，如今正在重返那一观念、那一制度。我们正在重建奴隶。
---
在着手证明这一点之前，我将在下面几页里稍作离题，非常简略地勾勒几个世纪前古老的异教奴隶制如何转变为一个自由社会的过程。然后我将概述这一新的非奴役社会如何在宗教改革时期在欧洲某些地区、尤其是在英格兰遭到毁灭，并由此逐渐产生出一个通常称为“资本主义”或“资本主义国家”的过渡性社会阶段（它如今正接近尾声）。
这样一段离题纯属历史性的，对于考察我们的主题并非逻辑上所必需；但它对读者大有价值，因为了解事物在现实中、在具体进程中实际上如何演变，能帮助我们更好地理解它们在未来趋向某个特定目标的逻辑过程。

===Original===

One could prove the tendency towards the servile state in England today to a man who knew nothing of the past of Europe; but that tendency will seem to him far more reasonably probable, far more a matter of experience and less a matter of mere deduction, when he knows what our society once was, and how it changed into what we know today.

===Chinese===

对于一个对欧洲的过去一无所知的人，也可以证明当今英格兰趋向奴役国家的趋势；但当这样的人知道了我们的社会曾经是什么、它如何变成我们今天所知道的样子之后，这一趋势在他看来就会显得远为合理可信得多，远为出于经验、而较少出于单纯的推论。

## IV How the Distributive State Failed / 第四章 分散所有制的国家如何失败了
===Original===

This failure original in England﻿—The story of the decline from distributive property to capitalism﻿—The economic revolution of the sixteenth century﻿—The confiscation of monastic land﻿—What might have happened had the state retained it﻿—As a fact that land is captured by an oligarchy﻿—England is capitalist *before* the advent of the industrial revolution﻿—Therefore modern industry, proceeding from England, has grown in a capitalist mould.
With the close of the middle ages the societies of Western Christendom and England among the rest were economically free.
Property was an institution native to the state and enjoyed by the great mass of its citizens. Cooperative institutions, voluntary regulations of labour, restricted the completely independent use of property by its owners only in order to keep that institution intact and to prevent the absorption of small property by great.
This excellent state of affairs which we had reached after many centuries of Christian development, and in which the old institution of slavery had been finally eliminated from Christendom, did not everywhere survive. In England in particular it was ruined. The seeds of the disaster were sown in the sixteenth century. Its first apparent effects came to light in the seventeenth. During the eighteenth century England came to be finally, though insecurely, established upon a proletarian basis, that is, it had already become a society of rich men possessed of the means of production on the one hand, and a majority dispossessed of those means upon the other. With the nineteenth century the evil plant had come to its maturity, and England had become before the close of that period a purely capitalist state, the type and model of capitalism for the whole world: with the means of production tightly held by a very small group of citizens, and the whole determining mass of the nation dispossessed of capital and land, and dispossessed, therefore, in all cases of security, and in many of sufficiency as well. The mass of Englishmen, still possessed of political, lacked more and more the elements of economic, freedom, and were in a worse posture than free citizens have ever found themselves before in the history of Europe.
By what steps did so enormous a catastrophe fall upon us?

===Chinese===

这一失败源于英格兰——从分散的所有制衰落到资本主义的经过——十六世纪的经济革命——教团土地的没收——假如国家保住了它本会怎样——事实上土地落入寡头之手——英格兰在工业革命到来*之前*就已经是资本主义的——因此发源于英格兰的现代工业是在资本主义的模型中生长起来的。
中世纪结束时，西欧基督教世界各社会、包括英格兰在内，在经济上都是自由的。
财产是本国固有的制度，为绝大多数公民所享有。合作的机构、自愿的劳动规章，只是为保持这一制度完整、防止大财产吞并小财产，才限制所有者对财产的完全独立使用。
我们经过许多个世纪基督教的发展而达到的这种美好状态——在这一状态中，古老的奴隶制制度已最终从基督教世界根除——并没有在所有地方延续下去。在英格兰，它尤其被毁灭了。灾难的种子播撒于十六世纪。它最初的显著后果显露于十七世纪。到了十八世纪，英格兰最终地——虽然不稳固地——建立在无产阶级的基础之上，也就是说，它已经变成这样一个社会：一方面是占有生产资料的富人，另一方面是被剥夺了生产资料的多数人。到十九世纪，这株恶株已经长成，英格兰在那个时期结束之前变成了一个纯粹的资本主义国家，成了全世界资本主义的典型和样板：生产资料紧握在一小撮公民手中，而民族的决定性主体被剥夺了资本和土地，因而在一切情形中被剥夺了安全，在许多情形中还被剥夺了温饱。英格兰的大众虽然仍保有政治自由，却越来越多地丧失经济自由的要素，处境比欧洲历史上任何自由公民曾经陷于的都要糟糕。
如此巨大的灾难是通过哪些步骤落到我们头上的呢？

===Original===

The first step in the process consisted in the mishandling of a great economic revolution which marked the sixteenth century. The lands and the accumulated wealth of the monasteries were taken out of, the hands of their old possessors with the intention of vesting them in the Crown﻿—but they passed, as a fact, not into the hands of the Crown, but into the hands of an already wealthy section of the community who, after the change was complete, became in the succeeding hundred years the governing power of England.
This is what happened:﻿—
The England of the early sixteenth century, the England over which Henry VIII inherited his powerful Crown in youth, though it was an England in which the great mass of men owned the land they tilled and the houses in which they dwelt, and the implements with which they worked, was yet an England in which these goods, though widely distributed, were distributed unequally.
Then, as now, the soil and its fixtures were the basis of all wealth, but the proportion between the value of the soil and its fixtures and the value of other means of production (implements, stores of clothing and of subsistence, etc.) was different from what it is now. The land and the fixtures upon it formed a very much larger fraction of the totality of the means of production than they do today. They represent today not one-half the total means of production of this country, and though they are the necessary foundation for all wealth production, yet our great machines, our stores of food and clothing, our coal and oil, our ships and the rest of it, come to more than the true value of the land and of the fixtures upon the land: they come to more than the arable soil and the pasture, the constructional value of the houses, wharves and docks, and so forth. In the early sixteenth century the land and the fixtures upon it came, upon the contrary, to very much more than all other forms of wealth combined.
Now this form of wealth was here, more than in any other Western European country, already in the hands of a wealthy land-owning class at the end of the Middle Ages.
It is impossible to give exact statistics, because none were gathered, and we can only make general statements based upon inference and research.

===Chinese===

这一过程的第一步，在于对标志着十六世纪的那场伟大经济革命的处置失当。修院的地产和积累的财富从其旧占有者手中被夺走，本意是要归入王室——但事实上它们没有落入王室之手，而是落入了共同体中已经富有的那一部分人手中；变革完成之后，这部分人在此后的一百年里成了英格兰的统治势力。
事情是这样的：——
十六世纪初的英格兰——青年亨利八世继承其强大王冠的那个英格兰——虽然是一个绝大多数人拥有自己耕种的土地、居住的房屋和使用的工具的英格兰，却也是一个这些财货虽然分布广泛、却分布不均的英格兰。
那时如同现在一样，土地及其附着物是一切财富的基础，但土地及其附着物的价值与其他生产资料（工具、衣物和食粮的储备等等）的价值之间的比例，与今日不同。土地及其附着物在生产资料总量中占的份额，比今天大得多。今天它们还不到本国生产资料总量的一半；虽然它们是一切财富生产的必要基础，但我们的巨大机器、我们的衣食储备、我们的煤炭和石油、我们的船舶等等，合计起来已超过土地及地上附着物的真正价值：超过了耕地和牧场、房屋码头船坞的建筑价值等等。而在十六世纪初，土地及其附着物恰恰相反，远远超过其他一切形态财富的总和。
而这种形态的财富，在这里比在西欧任何其他国家都更甚地，在中世纪结束时已经握在一个富有的土地占有阶级手中。
不可能给出精确的统计，因为当时根本没有统计，我们只能依据推断和研究作出概括的陈述。

===Original===

But, roughly speaking, we may say that of the total value of the land and its fixtures, probably rather more than a quarter, though less than a third, was in the hands of this wealthy class.
The England of that day was mainly agricultural, and consisted of more than four, but less than six million people, and in every agricultural community you would have the lord, as he was legally called (the squire, as he was already conversationally termed), in possession of more demesne land than in any other country. On the average you found him, I say, owning in this absolute fashion rather more than a quarter, perhaps a third of the land of the village: in the towns the distribution was more even. Sometimes it was a private individual who was in this position, sometimes a corporation, but in every village you would have found this demesne land absolutely owned by the political head of the village, occupying a considerable proportion of its acreage. The rest, though distributed as property among the less fortunate of the population, and carrying with it houses and implements from which they could not be dispossessed, paid certain dues to the lord, and, what was more, the lord exercised local justice. This class of wealthy landowners had been also for now one hundred years the justices upon whom local administration depended.
There was no reason why this state of affairs should not gradually have led to the rise of the peasant and the decay of the lord. That is what happened in France, and it might perfectly well have happened here. A peasantry eager to purchase might have gradually extended their holdings at the expense of the demesne land, and to the distribution of property, which was already fairly complete, there might have been added another excellent element, namely, the more equal possession of that property. But any such process of gradual buying by the small man from the great, such as would seem natural to the temper of us European people, and such as has since taken place nearly everywhere in countries which were left free to act upon their popular instincts, was interrupted in this country by an artificial revolution of the most violent kind. This artificial revolution consisted in the seizing of the monastic lands by the Crown.
It is important to grasp clearly the nature of this operation, for the whole economic future of England was to flow from it.

===Chinese===

但大致可以说：在土地及其附着物的总价值中，可能略多于四分之一、但少于三分之一，握在这个富有的阶级手中。
那时的英格兰以农业为主，人口在四百万到六百万之间；在每一个农业共同体里，你都会看到领主——这是法律上的称呼——（乡绅，这是当时口语里已经流行的叫法）占有比任何其他国家都更多的领主自营地。平均而言，你会发现他以这种绝对的方式拥有村庄土地的四分之一强、或许三分之一：城镇里的分配则较为均匀。有时处于这个位置的是一个私人，有时是一个法人团体；但在每一个村庄，你都会发现这部分领主自营地绝对地归村庄的政治首脑所有，占其面积的相当比例。其余的土地虽然作为财产分配给人口中较不幸的人们，并附带不容剥夺的房屋和工具，却要向领主缴纳一定的贡献；更重要的是，领主行使着地方司法权。这个富有的土地占有者阶级，至今一百年来还一直是地方行政所依托的治安法官。
这种状态本来完全可以逐渐导致农民的兴起和领主的衰落，没有任何理由不如此。法国发生的就是这样的事，它也完全可能在这里发生。一个急于购买的农民阶级本可以逐渐牺牲领主自营地来扩大自己的地产；本来可以在已经相当完全的财产分配之上，再加上另一个极好的因素，即对财产更平等的占有。但是，小农向大领主逐渐购买的这种过程——这种在我们欧洲人的禀性看来自然而然、后来在几乎所有听凭民众本能自由行事的国家里都发生了的过程——在这个国家被一场最暴烈的人为革命打断了。这场人为革命就是王室对修院土地的攫夺。
牢牢把握这场运作的性质是重要的，因为英格兰整个经济的未来都将由此流出。

===Original===

Of the *demesne* lands, and the power of local administration which they carried with them (a very important feature, as we shall see later), rather more than a quarter were in the hands of the Church; the Church was therefore the “lord” of something over 25 percent, say 28 percent, or perhaps nearly 30 percent, of English agricultural communities, and the overseers of a like proportion of all English agricultural produce. The Church was further the absolute owner in practice of something like 30 percent of the demesne land in the villages, and the receiver of something like 30 percent, of the customary dues, etc., paid by the smaller owners to the greater. All this economic power lay until 1535 in the hands of cathedral chapters, communities of monks and nuns, educational establishments conducted by the clergy, and so forth.
When the monastic lands were confiscated by Henry VIII, not the whole of this vast economic influence was suddenly extinguished. The secular clergy remained endowed, and most of the educational establishments, though looted, retained some revenue; but though the whole 30 percent did not suffer confiscation, something well over 20 percent did, and the revolution effected by this vast operation was by far the most complete, the most sudden, and the most momentous of any that has taken place in the economic history of any European people.
It was at first *intended* to retain this great mass of the means of production in the hands of the Crown: that must be clearly remembered by any student of the fortunes of England, and by all who marvel at the contrast between the old England and the new.
Had that intention been firmly maintained, the English state and its government would have been the most powerful in Europe.
The executive (which in those days meant the *King*) would have had a greater opportunity for crushing the resistance of the wealthy, for backing its political power with economic power, and for ordering the social life of its subjects than any other executive in Christendom.
Had Henry VIII and his successors kept the land thus confiscated, the power of the French Monarchy, at which we are astonished, would have been nothing to the power of the English.
The King of England would have had in his own hands an instrument of control of the most absolute sort.

===Chinese===

在*自营地*及其附带的地方行政权（正如我们后文将看到的，这是一个非常重要的特征）中，略多于四分之一掌握在教会手中；因此，教会是英格兰农业共同体中百分之二十五强——比方说百分之二十八、或将近百分之三十——的“领主”，是英格兰全部农产品的同一比例的监督者。教会实际上还是村庄自营地中大约百分之三十的绝对所有者，是较小的所有者向较大的所有者缴纳的习惯贡献中大约百分之三十的收受者。这一切经济权力直到 1535 年都掌握在大教堂圣职团、男女修士团体、由教士经办的教育机构等等手中。
当修院土地被亨利八世没收时，这一庞大的经济影响并非整个突然熄灭。在俗教士的圣俸保留了下来，大多数教育机构虽遭洗劫也还保有部分收入；虽然并未全部百分之三十都遭没收，但百分之二十出头确实被没收了；这场庞大运作所完成的革命，是欧洲任何民族经济史上所发生的革命中最彻底、最突然、最重大的。
起初*本意*是要把这大笔生产资料保留在王室手中：任何研究英格兰命运的学生、一切对旧英格兰与新英格兰之对比感到惊异的人，都必须清楚记住这一点。
倘若这一意图被坚定地维持下去，英格兰国家及其政府本会是全欧洲最强大的。
行政当局（在那个年代就是*国王*）本会有比基督教世界任何其他行政当局都更好的机会去粉碎富人的抵抗、以经济力量为政治权力后盾、安排臣民的社会生活。
倘若亨利八世及其继承者保住了这样没收来的土地，今人望之惊叹的法国王权之强大，与英格兰的王权相比将不值一提。
英格兰国王本将把一件最绝对的控制工具握在自己手中。

===Original===

He would presumably have used it, as a strong central government always does, for the weakening of the wealthier classes, and to the indirect advantage of the mass of the people. At any rate, it would have been a very different England indeed from the England we know, if the King had held fast to his own after the dissolution of the monasteries.
Now it is precisely here that the capital point in this great revolution appears. *The King failed to keep the lands he had seized.* That class of large landowners which already existed and controlled, as I have said, anything from a quarter to a third of the agricultural values of England, were too strong for the monarchy. They insisted upon land being granted to themselves, sometimes freely, sometimes for ridiculously small sums; and they were strong enough in Parliament, and through the local administrative power they had, to see that their demands were satisfied. Nothing that the Crown let go ever went back to the Crown, and year after year more and more of what had once been the monastic land became the absolute possession of the large landowners.
Observe the effect of this. All over England men who already held in virtually absolute property from one-quarter to one-third of the soil and the ploughs and the barns of a village, became possessed in a very few years of a further great section of the means of production, which turned the scale wholly in their favour. They added to that third a new and extra fifth. They became at a blow the owners of *half* the land! In many centres of capital importance they had come to own *more* than half the land. They were in many districts not only the unquestioned superiors, but the economic masters of the rest of the community. They could buy to the greatest advantage. They were strictly *competitive*, getting every shilling of due and of rent where the old clerical landlords had been *customary*﻿—leaving much to the tenant. They began to fill the universities, the judiciary. The Crown less and less decided between great and small. More and more the great could decide in their own favour. They soon possessed by these operations the bulk of the means of production, and they immediately began the process of eating up the small independent men and gradually forming those great estates which, in the course of a few generations, became identical with the village itself.

===Chinese===

他大概会像一切强大的中央政府那样使用它：削弱较富的阶级，间接地造福于人民大众。无论如何，如果国王在解散修院之后守住自己的所得，那将会是一个与我们所知的英格兰截然不同的英格兰。
而恰恰在这里，这场大革命的关键之点出现了。*国王没能保住他攫夺的土地。*我已说过，那个既已存在、控制着英格兰四分之一到三分之一农业价值的大土地占有者阶级，对君主制来说过于强大了。他们坚持要求把土地授予自己，有时是白送，有时代价低得可笑；他们在议会中、并通过他们掌握的地方行政权，强大到足以确保自己的要求得到满足。王室放手的任何东西再也没有回到王室手中；年复一年，越来越多的昔日修院土地变成了大土地占有者的绝对财产。
请看这一效果。全英格兰，那些本来已经实际绝对地占有一个村庄四分之一到三分之一的土地、犁具和谷仓的人们，在短短几年内又占有了生产资料的又一大块，从而使天平彻底倒向他们一边。他们在那三分之一之上又添了新的整整五分之一。他们一举成为*一半*土地的所有者！在许多具有头等重要性的中心，他们占有的土地已经*超过*一半。在许多地区，他们不仅是无可置疑的上等人，而且是共同体其余人的经济主人。他们能以最优惠的条件购买。他们是严格*竞争性的*，一先令的贡献和地租都收足——而旧的教士地主是*按习惯*行事的，给佃户留下很多。他们开始填充大学和司法机构。王室越来越少地在大人物与小人物之间裁断，大人物越来越多地可以按自己的意愿裁断。他们很快通过这些运作占有了生产资料的主体，随即开始吞并小独立者的过程，并逐渐形成那些大庄园——几代人之后，这些庄园就与村庄本身合而为一了。

===Original===

All over England you may notice that the great squires’ houses date from this revolution or after it. The manorial house, the house of the local great man as it was in the Middle Ages, survives here and there to show of what immense effect this revolution was. The low-timbered place with its steadings and outbuildings, only a larger farmhouse among the other farmhouses, is turned after the Reformation and thenceforward into a palace. Save where great castles (which were only held of the Crown and not owned) made an exception, the pre-Reformation gentry lived as men richer than, but not the masters of, other farmers around them. *After* the Reformation there began to arise all over England those great “country houses” which rapidly became the typical centres of English agricultural life.
The process was in full swing before Henry died. Unfortunately for England, he left as his heir a sickly child, during the six years of whose reign, from 1547 to 1553, the loot went on at an appalling rate. When he died and Mary came to the throne it was nearly completed. A mass of new families had arisen, wealthy out of all proportion to anything which the older England had known, and bound by a common interest to the older families which had joined in the grab. Every single man who sat in Parliament for a country required his price for voting the dissolution of the monasteries; every single man received it. A list of the members of the Dissolution Parliament is enough to prove this, and, apart from their power in Parliament, this class had a hundred other ways of insisting on their will. The Howards (already of some lineage), the Cavendishes, the Cecils, the Russels, and fifty other new families thus rose upon the ruins of religion; and the process went steadily on until, about one hundred years after its inception, the whole face of England was changed.
In the place of a powerful Crown disposing of revenues far greater than that of any subject, you had a Crown at its wit’s end for money, and dominated by subjects some of whom were its equals in wealth, and who could, especially through the action of Parliament (which they now controlled), do much what they willed with Government.

===Chinese===

在全英格兰你都可以注意到，大乡绅的宅邸始建于这场革命或其后。庄园宅第——中世纪地方大人物的居所——如今这里那里还留存着一两处，足以显示这场革命的巨大影响。那座低矮木构、带有厢房杂舍、原本只是众多农舍中较大一座的宅子，在宗教改革之后从此变成了宫殿。除了大城堡（那只是持有于王室而非自有）构成例外之外，宗教改革前的士绅过着比周围农人富、却并非其主人的日子。宗教改革*之后*，全英格兰开始矗起那些巨大的“乡间宅邸”，它们迅速成为英格兰农业生活的典型中心。
这一进程在亨利死前已全面展开。英格兰的不幸在于，他留下的继承人是个病弱的孩童；在其在位的六年间（1547—1553），掠夺以骇人的速度继续着。到他死去、玛丽登基时，此事已近于完成。一大批新家族崛起了，其富有与旧英格兰所知的一切不成比例，并与参与掠夺的旧家族利益相连。每一个坐进议会代表乡村的人，都为投票解散修院索取他的价钱；每一个人都拿到了。解散议会成员的名单足以证明这一点；而除了议会中的权力，这个阶级还有一百种别的办法坚持自己的意志。霍华德家族（当时已有些门第）、卡文迪什家族、塞西尔家族、罗素家族，还有五十个新家族就这样在宗教的废墟上发迹；这一进程稳步推进，直到其发端约一百年后，英格兰的整个面貌为之改观。
取代支配着远非任何臣民可比的巨大岁入的强大王室，你有了一个为钱一筹莫展的王室，被臣民们所支配——其中一些臣民的财富与王室不相上下，而且能够——尤其是通过如今由他们控制的议会——对政府为所欲为。

===Original===

In other words, by the first third of the seventeenth century, by 1630﻿–﻿40, the economic revolution was finally accomplished, and the new economic reality thrusting itself upon the old traditions of England was a powerful oligarchy of large owners overshadowing an impoverished and dwindled monarchy.
Other causes had contributed to this deplorable result. The change in the value of money had hit the Crown very hard;2 the peculiar history of the Tudor family, their violent passions, their lack of resolution and of any continuous policy, to some extent the character of Charles I himself, and many another subsidiary cause may be quoted. But the great main fact upon which the whole thing is dependent is the fact that the monastic lands, at least a fifth of the wealth of the country, had been transferred to the great landowners, and that this transference had tipped the scale over entirely in their favour as against the peasantry.
The diminished and impoverished Crown could no longer stand. It fought against the new wealth, the struggle of the civil wars; it was utterly defeated; and when a final settlement was arrived at in 1660, you have all the realities of power in the hands of a small powerful class of wealthy men, the King still surrounded by the forms and traditions of his old power, but in practice a salaried puppet. And in that economic world which underlies all political appearances, the great dominating note was that a few wealthy families had got hold of the bulk of the means of production in England, while the same families exercised all local administrative power and were moreover the judges, the higher education, the Church, and the generals. They quite overshadowed what was left of central government in this country.
Take, as a starting-point for what followed, the date 1700. By that time more than half of the English were dispossessed of capital and of land. Not one man in two, even if you reckon the very small owners, inhabited a house of which he was the secure possessor, or tilled land from which he could not be turned off.
Such a proportion may seem to us today a wonderfully free arrangement, and certainly if nearly one-half of our population were possessed of the means of production, we should be in a very different situation from that in which we find ourselves.

===Chinese===

换句话说，到十七世纪头三十年末，到 1630—1640 年，经济革命最终完成；强加于英格兰旧传统之上的新的经济现实，是一个遮蔽着贫困衰微的君主制的、大土地占有者的强大寡头集团。
其他原因也促成了这一可悲的结果。币值的变化使王室遭受重创[2]；都铎家族的特殊历史、他们的暴戾任性、他们缺乏决断和一贯的政策、查理一世本人性格的某种因素，以及许多别的次要原因都可以援引。但整件事所系的主要事实是：修院土地——至少占全国财富的五分之一——转移到了大土地占有者手中，而这一转移使天平彻底倒向他们、压倒了农民。
缩小而贫困的王室再也支撑不住了。它与新财富作战——那便是内战；它被彻底击败；而 1660 年最终协议达成时，一切实在的权力都已握在一小撮强有力的富有人士阶级手中，国王仍被旧权力的形式与传统簇拥着，实际上不过是一个领薪的傀儡。而在一切政治表象之下的那个经济世界里，主导的调子是：少数几个富有家族攫住了英格兰生产资料的主体，同时这些家族行使着一切地方行政权，而且还是法官、高等教育、教会和将军们。他们完全遮蔽了这个国家残存的中央政府。
把 1700 年当作下文的起点。到那时，超过半数的英格兰人已被剥夺了资本和土地。即使把极小的所有者都算上，也凑不足两个人中有一个住在自己稳当拥有的房子里，或耕种着不会被赶走的土地。
这样的比例在今天看来也许还算得上一种惊人的自由安排；当然，如果将近一半的人口占有生产资料，我们的处境将与今日的处境大不相同。

===Original===

But the point to seize is that, though the bad business was very far from completion in or about the year 1700, yet by that date England had already become **capitalist**. She had already permitted a vast section of her population to become *proletarian*, and it is this and *not* the so-called “Industrial Revolution,” a later thing, which accounts for the terrible social condition in which we find ourselves today.
How true this is what I still have to say in this section will prove.
In an England thus already cursed with a very large proletariat class, and in an England already directed by a dominating capitalist class, possessing the means of production, there came a great industrial development.
Had that industrial development come upon a people economically free, it would have taken a cooperative form. Coming as it did upon a people which had already largely lost its economic freedom, it took at its very origin a *capitalist* form, and this form it has retained, expanded, and perfected throughout two hundred years.
It was in England that the industrial system arose. It was in England that all its traditions and habits were formed; and because the England in which it arose was already a capitalist England, modern Industrialism, wherever you see it at work today, having spread from England, has proceeded upon the capitalist model.
It was in 1705 that the first practical steam-engine, Newcomen’s, was set to work. The life of a man elapsed before this invention was made, by Watt’s introduction of the condenser, into the great instrument of production which has transformed our industry﻿—but in those sixty years all the origins of the industrial system are to be discovered. It was just before Watt’s patent that Hargreaves’ spinning-jenny appeared. Thirty years earlier, Abraham Darby of Colebrook Dale, at the end of a long series of experiments which had covered more than a century, smelted iron-ore successfully with coke. Not twenty years later, King introduced the flying shuttle, the first great improvement in the hand-loom; and in general the period covered by such a life as that of Dr. Johnson, born just after Newcomen’s engine was first set working, and dying seventy-four years afterwards, when the industrial system was in full blast, covers that great transformation of England.

===Chinese===

但必须抓住的要点是：尽管这笔坏买卖到 1700 年前后还远未完成，但到那时英格兰已经变成了**资本主义的**。她已经容许她人口中的一大部变成*无产阶级*；正是这一点、而*不是*后来才发生的所谓“工业革命”，解释了我们今日陷于其中的可怕的社会状况。
这一点如何真实，本节我还要说的话将予以证明。
在这样一个已经背负着庞大无产阶级的英格兰，在这样一个已由占有生产资料的、占支配地位的资本主义阶级所导引的英格兰，一场巨大的工业发展到来了。
倘若那场工业发展降临在一个经济上自由的民族头上，它会采取合作的形式。而它实际上降临在一个已大量丧失经济自由的民族头上，于是在其起源之时就采取了*资本主义*的形式；两百年间它保留、扩张并完善着这一形式。
工业制度兴起于英格兰。它的一切传统和习惯都是在英格兰形成的；而因为它兴于其上的那个英格兰已经是资本主义的英格兰，现代工业主义——无论你今天在哪里看到它在运转——既然是从英格兰扩散出去的，便都循着资本主义的模型进行。
1705 年，第一台实用蒸汽机——纽科门的蒸汽机——投入使用。隔了一代人的寿命，瓦特引入冷凝器，才使这项发明变成改造了我们工业的伟大生产工具——而就在那六十年间，工业制度的一切起源都可以找到。就在瓦特专利前不久，哈格里夫斯的珍妮纺纱机问世。再早三十年，科尔布鲁克代尔的亚伯拉罕·达比在历经一个多世纪的一长串实验之后，用焦炭成功地冶炼了铁矿石。不到二十年后，凯伊引入飞梭——手织机的第一项重大改进；大体上，纽科门发动机首次运转后不久出生、七十四年后工业体系全面轰鸣时去世的约翰逊博士的一生，正好覆盖了英格兰的这场大变革。

===Original===

A man who, as a child, could remember the last years of Queen Anne, and who lived to the eve of the French Revolution, saw passing before his eyes the change which transformed English society and has led it to the expansion and peril in which we see it today.
What was the characteristic mark of that half-century and more? Why did the new inventions give us the form of society now known and hated under the name of “Industrial?” Why did the vast increase in the powers of production, in population and in accumulation of wealth, turn the mass of Englishmen into a poverty-stricken proletariat, cut off the rich from the rest of the nation, and develop to the full all the evils which we associate with the capitalist state?
To that question an answer almost as universal as it is unintelligent has been given. That answer is not only unintelligent but false, and it will be my business here to show how false it is. The answer so provided in innumerable textbooks, and taken almost as commonplace in our universities, is that the new methods of production﻿—the new machinery, the new implements﻿—fatally and of themselves developed a capitalist state in which a few should own the means of production and the mass should be proletarian. The new instruments, it is pointed out, were on so vastly greater a scale than the old, and were so much more expensive, that the small man could not afford them; while the rich man, who could afford them, ate up by his competition, and reduced from the position of a small owner to that of a wage-earner, his insufficiently equipped competitor who still attempted to struggle on with the older and cheaper tools. To this (we are told) the advantages of concentration were added in favour of the large owner against the small. Not only were the new instruments expensive almost in proportion to their efficiency, but, especially after the introduction of steam, they were efficient in proportion to their concentration in few places and under the direction of a few men.

===Chinese===

一个孩提时还记得安妮女王末年、活到法国大革命前夜的人，亲眼看见了这场变革从眼前走过——它改变了英国社会，并把它带到我们今天所见的扩张与危险之中。
那半个多世纪的特征标志是什么？为什么新发明给了我们如今以“工业的”之名而著称且为人痛恨的社会形式？为什么生产力的巨大增长、人口和财富积累的巨大增长，却把英格兰大众变成了赤贫的无产阶级，把富人与民族的其他部分隔断，并把我们与资本主义国家相连的一切弊病发展到极致？
对这个问题，人们给出过一个几乎同它的愚蠢一样普遍的答案。这个答案不仅愚蠢而且错误，我在此的任务就是说明它错在哪里。这个在无数教科书里提供、在我们的大学里几乎被当作常识的答案是：新的生产方法——新的机器、新的工具——致命地、自然而然地本身就会发展出一个资本主义国家，其中少数人占有生产资料，群众则是无产者。有人指出，新工具规模比旧的大得多、昂贵得多，小人物买不起；而买得起的富人，靠竞争吃掉了装备不足、仍试图用旧而廉价的工具苦苦支撑的对手，把小所有者贬成了挣工资的人。除此之外（我们被告知），集中的好处又添了一把力，有利于大所有者对负小所有者。新工具不仅其昂贵几乎与其效能成正比，而且——尤其在蒸汽引入之后——它们集中于少数地点、听命于少数人之手的程度越高，效能就越高。

===Original===

Under the effect of such false arguments as these we have been taught to believe that the horrors of the industrial system were a blind and necessary product of material and impersonal forces, and that wherever the steam engine, the power loom, the blast furnace and the rest were introduced, there fatally would soon appear a little group of owners exploiting a vast majority of the dispossessed.
It is astonishing that a statement so unhistorical should have gained so general a credence. Indeed, were the main truths of English history taught in our schools and universities today, were educated men familiar with the determining and major facts of the national past, such follies could never have taken root. The vast growth of the proletariat, the concentration of ownership into the hands of a few owners, and the exploitation by those owners of the mass of the community, had no fatal or necessary connection with the discovery of new and perpetually improving methods of production. The evil proceeded indirect historical sequence, proceeded patently and demonstrably, from the fact that England, the seed-plot of the industrial system, was *already* captured by a wealthy oligarchy *before* the series of great discoveries began.
Consider in what way the industrial system developed upon capitalist lines. Why were a few rich men put with such ease into possession of the new methods? Why was it normal and natural in their eyes and in that of contemporary society that those who produced the new wealth with the new machinery should be proletarian and dispossessed? Simply because the England upon which the new discoveries had come was *already* an England owned as to its soil and accumulations of wealth by a small minority: it was *already* an England in which perhaps half of the whole population was proletarian, and a medium for exploitation ready to hand.
When any one of the new industries was launched it had to be “capitalised;” that is, accumulated wealth from some source or other had to be found which would support labour in the process of production until that process should be complete.

===Chinese===

在诸如此类的错误论断的影响下，我们被教导相信：工业制度的种种惨状是物质性、非人格力量的盲目而必然的产物；凡引入蒸汽机、动力织机、高炉等等的地方，都注定很快会出现一小撮占有者剥削一大群被剥夺者。
如此缺乏历史根据的断言竟获得了如此普遍的相信，实在令人惊讶。说真的，如果今天我们的中学和大学讲授英国历史的主要真相，如果受过教育的人熟悉本国过去的那些决定性的主要事实，这样的蠢话就绝不可能扎根。无产阶级的庞大增长、所有权向少数占有者手中的集中、这些占有者对共同体的剥削，与新的、不断改进的生产方法的发现，没有任何命定的或必然的联系。这一祸害是在直接的历史连续中、明明白白地、可证明地，源于这样一个事实：作为工业制度苗圃的英格兰，在那一连串伟大发现开始*之前*，就已经被一个富有的寡头集团所攫取。
请想一想工业制度是沿着怎样的资本主义路线发展起来的。为什么少数富人如此轻易地占有了新方法？为什么在他们的眼中、在当时社会的眼中，用新机器生产新财富的人就应该是无产的、被剥夺的，这竟是正常而自然的？仅仅因为新发现所降临的那个英格兰，*已经*是一个土壤和财富积累归一小撮少数所有的英格兰：它*已经*是一个或许半数人口都是无产者的英格兰，是一个现成的供剥削的介质。
任何一项新工业开办时，都必须被“资本化”；也就是说，必须从某个来源找到积累的财富，以便在生产过程完成之前养活这一过程中的劳动。

===Original===

Someone must find the corn and the meat and the housing and the clothing by which should be supported, between the extraction of the raw material and the moment when the consumption of the finished article could begin, the human agents which dealt with that raw material and turned it into the finished product. Had property been well distributed, protected by cooperative guilds, fenced round and supported by custom and by the autonomy of great artisan corporations, those accumulations of wealth, necessary for the launching of each new method of production and for each new perfection of it, would have been discovered in the mass of small owners. *Their* corporations, *their* little parcels of wealth combined would have furnished the *capitalisation* required for the new processes, and men already owners would, as one invention succeeded another, have increased the total wealth of the community without disturbing the balance of distribution. There is no conceivable link in reason or in experience which binds the capitalisation of a new process with the idea of a few employing owners and a mass of employed non-owners working at a wage. Such great discoveries coming in a society like that of the thirteenth century would have blest and enriched mankind. Coming upon the diseased moral conditions of the eighteenth century in this country, they proved a curse.
To whom could the new industry turn for capitalisation? The small owner had already largely disappeared. The corporate life and mutual obligations which had supported him and confirmed him in his property had been broken to pieces by no “economic development,” but by the deliberate action of the rich. He was ignorant because his schools had been taken from him and the universities closed to him. He was the more ignorant because the common life which once nourished his social sense and the cooperative arrangements which had once been his defence had disappeared. When you sought an accumulation of corn, of clothing, of housing, of fuel as the indispensable preliminary to the launching of your new industry; when you looked round for someone who could find the accumulated wealth necessary for these considerable experiments, you had to turn to the class which had already monopolised the bulk of the means of production in England. The rich men alone could furnish you with those supplies.
Nor was this all.

===Chinese===

总得有人拿出谷物、肉食、住房、衣服，去养活那些从原材料的采掘直到成品开始消费之间、经手原材料并把它变成成品的人。倘若财产分配良好，受到合作行会的保护，被习惯和各大工匠团体的自治所环绕和支撑，那么为每项新生产方法的创办、为它的每一项改进所必需的那些财富积累，本来会在众多小所有者中间找到。*他们的*团体、*他们*的一小笔一小笔的财富合起来，本可以提供新工艺所需的*资本化*；而本已是所有者的人们，随着一项发明接一项发明，本可以增加共同体的总财富而不扰乱分配的平衡。在理性中或经验中，都不存在任何可以设想的纽带，把一个新工艺的资本化与“少数雇佣的所有者加一大群受雇的非所有者按工资干活”这个观念绑在一起。这样的伟大发现若是降临在像十三世纪那样的社会，本会赐福人类、使人类富足。它们降临在本国十八世纪那种病态的道德环境中，结果证明是一场诅咒。
新工业能向谁去寻求资本化呢？小所有者已经大量消失。曾支撑他、确认他的财产的共同体生活和互助义务，不是被什么“经济发展”，而是被富人的蓄意行动砸得粉碎。他无知，因为他的学校被夺走了，大学对他关闭了。他更加无知，因为曾滋养他社会意识的共同生活和曾是他屏障的合作安排都已消失。当你为创办新工业而寻找谷物、衣服、住房、燃料的积累这一不可或缺的前期条件时；当你环顾四周、寻找能拿出这些可观试验所必需的积累财富的人时，你只能转向那个已经垄断了英格兰大部分生产资料的阶级。只有富人们能向你提供这些供给。
而这还不是全部。

===Original===

The supplies once found and the adventure “capitalised,” that form of human energy which lay best to hand, which was indefinitely exploitable, weak, ignorant, and desperately necessitous, ready to produce for you upon almost any terms, and glad enough if you would only keep it alive, was the existing proletariat which the new plutocracy had created when, in cornering the wealth of the country after the Reformation, they had thrust out the mass of Englishmen from the possession of implements, of houses, and of land.
The rich class, adopting some new process of production for its private gain, worked it upon those lines of mere competition which its avarice had already established. Cooperative tradition was dead. Where would it find its cheapest labour? Obviously among the proletariat﻿—not among the remaining small owners. What class would increase under the new wealth? Obviously the proletariat again, without responsibilities, with nothing to leave to its progeny; and as they swelled the capitalist’s gain, they enabled him with increasing power to buy out the small owner and send him to swell by another tributary the proletarian mass.
It was upon this account that the Industrial Revolution, as it is called, took in its very origins the form which has made it an almost unmixed curse for the unhappy society in which it has flourished. The rich, already possessed of the accumulations by which that industrial change could alone be nourished, inherited all its succeeding accumulations of implements and all its increasing accumulations of subsistence. The factory system, starting upon a basis of capitalist and proletariat, grew in the mould which had determined its origins. With every new advance the capitalist looked for proletariat grist to feed the productive mill. Every circumstance of that society, the form in which the laws that governed ownership and profit were cast, the obligations of partners, the relations between “master” and “man,” directly made for the indefinite expansion of a subject, formless, wage-earning class controlled by a small body of owners, which body would tend to become smaller and richer still, and to be possessed of power ever greater and greater as the bad business unfolded.
The spread of economic oligarchy was everywhere, and not in industry alone.

===Chinese===

供给既已找到、事业已经“资本化”，哪种人的劳动力最现成、可以无限剥削、软弱、无知、赤贫到极点、几乎肯按任何条件为你生产、只要你能让他活命他就感激不尽呢？就是现存的无产阶级——那个新的财富寡头在宗教改革后攫夺全国财富时，把英格兰大众从工具、房屋和土地的所有权中赶出来所造成的无产阶级。
富有的阶级为私利采用某种新生产方法时，是按照其贪欲早已确立的纯粹竞争的路线来运作的。合作的传统死了。它到哪里去找最廉价的劳动？显然是在无产阶级中间，而不是在残存的小所有者中间。在新财富下哪个阶级会膨胀？显然还是无产阶级：无幸无挂，没有什么可以留给后代的东西；而随着他们膨胀资本家的收益，他们又使资本家得以以越来越大的权势买断小所有者，把他变成又一条支流去壮大无产的群众。
正是由于这个缘故，所谓的工业革命在其起源之时就带上了那种形式，这种形式使它对它在其上繁盛的那个不幸社会几乎成了纯粹的诅咒。富人既已占有唯一能滋养那场工业变革的积累，也就继承了它此后的一切工具积累和一切不断增长的生计积累。工厂制度从资本家与无产阶级的基础上起步，就在决定了其起源的模型中生长。每一次新的进步，资本家都在寻找无产者的谷料去喂生产的磨。那个社会的一切环境——关于所有权和利润的法律所采取的形式、合伙人的义务、“主人”与“工人”之间的关系——都直接导向一个受一小撮占有者控制的臣属的、无形的、挣工资的阶级的无限扩张；而这撮人将随着这笔坏交易的展开，趋向于变得更小、更富，掌握越来越大的权力。
经济寡头的扩张无处不在，并不仅仅在工业中。

===Original===

The great landlords destroyed deliberately and of set purpose and to their own advantage the common rights over common land. The small plutocracy with which they were knit up, and with whose mercantile elements they were now fused, directed everything to its own ends. That strong central government which should protect the community against the rapacity of a few had gone generations before. Capitalism triumphant wielded all the mechanism of legislation and of information too. It still holds them; and there is not an example of so-called “social reform” today which is not demonstrably (though often subconsciously) directed to the further entrenchment and confirmation of an industrial society in which it is taken for granted that a few shall own, that the vast majority shall live at a wage under them, and that all the bulk of Englishmen may hope for is the amelioration of their lot by regulations and by control from above﻿—but not by property; not by freedom.
We all feel﻿—and those few of us who have analysed the matter not only feel but know﻿—that the capitalist society thus gradually developed from its origins in the capture of the land four hundred years ago has reached its term. It is almost self-evident that it cannot continue in the form which now three generations have known, and it is equally self-evident that some solution must be found for the intolerable and increasing instability with which it has poisoned our lives. But before considering the solutions variously presented by various schools of thought, I shall in my next section show how and why the English capitalist industrial system is thus intolerably unstable and consequently presents an acute problem which must be solved under pain of social death.
---
It must be noted that modern industrialism has spread to many other centres from England. It bears everywhere the features stamped upon it by its origin in this country.

===Chinese===

大地主们蓄意地、处心积虑地、为自己的利益，摧毁了对公地的公共权利。与他们结成一体、如今又与其中的商业成分相融合的那个小财阀集团，把一切都导向自己的目的。那个本应保护共同体免受少数人掠夺的强有力的中央政府，几代人之前就已不复存在。得胜的资本主义还攫住了立法和舆论的一切机器。它至今仍然攥着它们；今天没有一个所谓的“社会改革”的例子不是——虽然往往是下意识地——明白无误地指向工业社会的进一步固守和确认，在这个社会中，人们理所当然地认为：少数人应当占有，绝大多数人应当在他们之下靠工资生活，英格兰人的大众所能指望的，只是靠自上而下的规章和管制来改善处境——而不是靠财产；不是靠自由。
我们都感觉到——而我们中间那些分析过这个问题的人不仅感觉到而且知道——这个从四百年前攫夺土地发端而逐渐发展起来的资本主义社会，已经到了尽头。它不可能再以这三代人已经认识的这种形式延续下去，这几乎是不言自明的；同样不言而喻的是，必须为它毒化了我们生活的那种难以忍受且与日俱增的不稳定找到某种解决办法。但在考察各派思想提出的各种解决方案之前，我将在下一章说明：英国的资本主义工业制度为何以及如何是如此令人难以忍受地不稳定，因而提出了一个必须解决、否则便面临社会死亡之痛的尖锐问题。
---
必须指出，现代工业制度从英格兰扩散到了许多别的中心。它在各地都带着它发源于这个国家时被赋予的那些特征。

## III How the Servile Institution Was for a Time Dissolved / 第三章 奴役制度如何一度瓦解
===Original===

The subconscious effect of the Faith in this matter﻿—The main elements of pagan economic society﻿—The *villa*﻿—The transformation of the agricultural slave into the Christian “serf”﻿—Next into the Christian “peasant”﻿—The corresponding erection throughout Christendom of the “**distributive state**”﻿—It is nearly complete at the close of the Middle Ages﻿—“It was not machinery that lost us our freedom, it was the loss of a free mind.”
The process by which slavery disappeared among Christian men, though very lengthy in its development (it covered close upon a thousand years), and though exceedingly complicated in its detail, may be easily and briefly grasped in its main lines.
Let it first be clearly understood that the vast revolution through which the European mind passed between the first and the fourth centuries (that revolution which is often termed the conversion of the world to Christianity, but which should for purposes of historical accuracy be called the growth of the Church) included no attack upon the servile institution.
No dogma of the Church pronounced slavery to be immoral, or the sale and purchase of men to be a sin, or the imposition of compulsory labour upon a Christian to be a contravention of any human right.
The emancipation of slaves was indeed regarded as a good work by the faithful: but so was it regarded by the pagan. It was, on the face of it, a service rendered to one’s fellowmen. The sale of Christians to pagan masters was abhorrent to the later empire of the barbarian invasions, not because slavery in itself was condemned, but because it was a sort of treason to civilisation to force men away from civilisation to barbarism. In general you will discover no pronouncement against slavery as an institution, nor any moral definition attacking it, throughout all those early Christian centuries during which it none the less effectively disappears.
The form of its disappearance is well worth noting. It begins with the establishment as the fundamental unit of production in Western Europe of those great landed estates, commonly lying in the hands of a single proprietor, and generally known as **villae**.

===Chinese===

信仰（the Faith）在此事上的潜意识影响——异教经济社会的主要要素——庄园（*villa*）——农业奴隶向基督教“农奴”的转变——继而转变为基督教“农民”——贯穿基督教世界的“**分散所有制国家（distributive state）**”的相应建立——在中世纪末期几近完成——“使我们丧失自由的并非机器，而是自由心灵的丧失。”
奴隶制（slavery）在基督徒中消失的过程，虽然其发展极为漫长（历经近千年之久），其细节也极其繁复，但其主要线索却可以简明易懂地予以把握。
首先应当明确理解：欧洲人的心灵在公元一世纪至四世纪之间所经历的那场巨大革命（常被称为世界归信基督教的革命，但出于历史精确性，理应称为教会［the Church］的成长），并未包含对奴役制度的任何抨击。
教会的任何信条都不曾宣称奴隶制是不道德的，或宣称人口买卖是一种罪恶，亦或宣称强迫基督徒服劳役是对任何人权的侵犯。
释放奴隶在信徒眼中的确被视为一件善行，然而在异教徒眼中同样如此。从表面上看，这是向同胞行善。在蛮族入侵时期的后帝国时代，将基督徒卖给异教主人的行为令人切齿痛恨，这并非因为奴隶制本身受到谴责，而是因为强迫人脱离文明沦落于野蛮乃是对文明的一种背叛。总的来说，在早期基督教的几个世纪里，尽管奴隶制在事实上切实消失了，但你却找不到任何反对作为一种制度的奴隶制的公开宣言，也找不到任何抨击它的道德界定。
它消亡的形式非常值得注意。它始于在西欧（Western Europe）确立起那些作为基本生产单位的大地产；这些大地产通常握在单一所有者手中，通称为**庄园（*villae*）**。

===Original===

There were, of course, many other forms of human agglomeration: small peasant farms owned in absolute proprietorship by their petty masters; groups of free men associated in what was called a *vicus*; manufactories in which groups of slaves were industrially organised to the profit of their master; and, governing the regions around them, the scheme of Roman towns.
But of all these the *villa* the dominating type; and as society passed from the high civilisation of the first four centuries into the simplicity of the Dark Ages, the *villa*, the unit of agricultural production, became more and more the model of all society.
Now the *villa* began as a considerable extent of land, containing, like a modern English estate, pasture, arable, water, wood and heath, or waste land. It was owned by a *dominus* or “lord” in absolute proprietorship, to sell, or leave by will, to do with it whatsoever he chose. It was cultivated for him by *slaves* to whom he owed nothing in return, and whom it was simply his interest to keep alive and to continue breeding in order that they might perpetuate his wealth.
