I concentrate particularly upon these slaves, the great majority of the human beings inhabiting the land, because, although there arose in the Dark Ages, when the Roman Empire was passing into the society of the Middle Ages, other social elements within the *villae*﻿—the freed men who owed the lord a modified service, and even occasionally independent citizens present through a contract terminable and freely entered into﻿—yet it is the *slave* who is the mark of all that society.
At its origin, then, the Roman *villa* was a piece of absolute property, the production of wealth upon which was due to the application of slave labour to the natural resources of the place; and that slave labour was as much the property of the lord as was the land itself.
The first modification which this arrangement showed in the new society which accompanied the growth and establishment of the Church in the Roman world, was a sort of customary rule which modified the old arbitrary position of the slave.
The slave was still a slave, but it was both more convenient in the decay of communications and public power, and more consonant with the social spirit of the time to make sure of that slave’s produce by asking him for no more than certain customary dues.

===Chinese===

当然，人类的集聚形态还有许多其他形式：由小业主绝对占有的小农场；由自由人结合而成、被称为村落（*vicus*）的群体；由奴隶群体按工场方式组织起来为主人的利益进行生产的手工作坊；以及统治其周边地区的罗马（Roman）城镇体系。
但在这一切之中，庄园（*villa*）是占据主导地位的形态；随着社会从最初四个世纪的高度文明过渡到黑暗时代（the Dark Ages）的朴素简陋，作为农业生产单位的庄园日益成为整个社会的范式。
庄园起初是一片相当广袤的土地，如同现代英格兰（English）的地产一样，包含牧场、耕地、水源、林地、荒原或废弃地。它归一位领主（*dominus*，即“主人”）绝对所有，任凭其出售、遗赠或随意处置。为他耕作的是奴隶，他对奴隶无须给予任何回报，使奴隶存活并繁衍后代纯粹是为了延续领主自身的财富利益。
我尤其着眼于这些奴隶——他们是栖居于土地上的绝大多数人——因为尽管在黑暗时代（即罗马帝国向中世纪社会过渡的时期），庄园内部也出现了其他社会成分——须向领主服一定劳役的获释奴隶，甚至偶有通过订立可终止的自由契约而存在的独立公民——然而奴隶才是整个社会的本质印记。
因此，罗马庄园在起源时是一块绝对所有的财产，其财富生产源于奴隶劳动对当地自然资源的投入；而这种奴隶劳动同土地本身一样，完全属于领主的财产。
随着教会在罗马世界的成长与确立，这种制度在新社会中所表现出的最初演变，是一种改变了奴隶以往专断受控处境的习惯法规则。
奴隶依然是奴隶，但在交通与公共权力衰落的背景下，加之与当时的社会精神相契合，通过仅向奴隶索取某些按惯例缴纳的贡赋来确保获取奴隶的产出，既更为便利，又更为合宜。

===Original===

The slave and his descendants became more or less rooted to one spot. Some were still bought and sold, but in decreasing numbers. As the generations passed a larger and a larger proportion lived where and as their fathers had lived, and the produce which they raised was fixed more and more at a certain amount, which the lord was content to receive and ask no more. The arrangement was made workable by leaving to the slave all the remaining produce of his own labour. There was a sort of implied bargain here, in the absence of public powers and in the decline of the old highly centralised and vigorous system which could always guarantee to the master the full product of the slave’s effort. The bargain implied was, that if the slave community of the *villa* would produce for the benefit of its lord not less than a certain customary amount of goods from the soil of the *villa*, the lord could count on their always exercising that effort by leaving to them all the surplus, which they could increase, if they willed, indefinitely.
By the ninth century, when this process had been gradually at work for a matter of some three hundred years, one fixed form of productive unit began to be apparent throughout Western Christendom.
The old absolutely owned estate had come to be divided into three portions. One of these was pasture and arable land, reserved privately to the lord, and called “domain”: that is, lord’s land. Another was in the occupation, and already almost in the possession (practically, though not legally), of those who had once been slaves. A third was common land over which both the lord and the slave exercised each their various rights, which rights were minutely remembered and held sacred by custom. For instance, in a certain village, if there was beech pasture for three hundred swine, the lord might put in but fifty: two hundred and fifty were the rights of the “village.”
Upon the first of these portions, domain, wealth was produced by the obedience of the slave for certain fixed hours of labour. He must come so many days a week, or upon such and such occasions (all fixed and customary), to till the land of the domain for his lord, and *all* the produce of this must be handed over to the lord﻿—though, of course, a daily wage in kind was allowed, for the labourer must live.

===Chinese===

奴隶及其后裔或多或少地固定在了一处土地上。有些人仍被买卖，但数量日渐稀少。随着世代更迭，越来越多的奴隶像其父辈那样居住在同一地点并遵循同样的劳作方式，而领主从他们身上所获得的产出，也日益由惯例固定下来。领主无法便利地随意变更条款。如果一个家庭每年向领主自营地提供若干蒲式耳的小麦、若干劳役、以及定量的禽肉与蛋类，那么维持传统的贡赋比扰乱庄园的经济秩序更加符合领主的利益。
于是，奴隶的劳动被划分为了两部分：一部分以传统的贡赋以及在领主自营地上的劳作形式交纳给主人；另一部分则保留给他自己，用于在分配给他的土地上维持自己家庭的生计。
这是迈出的第一大步。奴隶正走向成为农奴（serf）的道路。他尚未完全获得那一身份，但专断权力的边界已开始退缩。
若不破坏习惯性安排，奴隶便无法脱离庄园被单独出售。出卖奴隶意味着瓦解社区既定的社会秩序；在一个中央权威孱弱、地方稳定重于一切的时代，整个社会都反对任何此类破坏行为。
此外，教会促进了这种稳定性。教会并未将奴隶解放宣扬为一种抽象权利，但它赞赏那些公正仁慈对待依附者的人，并且倡导一种观念：耕作者对其炉灶与家园拥有道德上的权利。
因此，庄园的惯例逐渐凝固为法律，这并非依靠制定法令，而是依靠先例与传统的缓慢累积。

===Original===

Upon the second portion, “land in villenage,” which was nearly always the most of the arable and pasture land of the *villae*, the slaves worked by rules and customs which they gradually came to elaborate for themselves. They worked under an officer of their own, sometimes nominated, sometimes elected: nearly always, in practice, a man suitable to them and more or less of their choice; though this cooperative work upon the old slave-ground was controlled by the general customs of the village, common to lord and slave alike, and the principal officer over both kinds of land was the lord’s steward.
Of the wealth so produced by the slaves, a certain fixed portion (estimated originally in kind) was payable to the lord’s bailiff, and became the property of the lord.
Finally, on the third division of the land, the “waste,” the “wood,” the “heath,” and certain common pastures, wealth was produced as elsewhere by the labour of those who had once been the slaves, but divided in customary proportions between them and their master. Thus, such and such a water meadow would have grazing for so many oxen; the number was rigidly defined, and of that number so many would be the lord’s and so many the villagers’.
During the eighth, ninth and tenth centuries this system crystallised and became so natural in men’s eyes that the original servile character of the working folk upon the *villa* was forgotten.
The documents of the time are rare. These three centuries are the crucible of Europe, and record is drowned and burnt in them. Our study of their social conditions, especially in the latter part, are matter rather of inference than of direct evidence. But the sale and purchase of men, already exceptional at the beginning of this period, is almost unknown before the end of it.

===Chinese===

在第二部分土地上，即“农奴佃赋地（land in villenage）”——它几乎总是占庄园耕地和牧场的大部分——奴隶们按照他们自己逐渐拟定完善的规则与习俗进行耕作。领主的管事（bailiff）虽然依旧在场监督领主贡赋的缴纳与自营地的耕种，但对村庄的内部治理干涉得越来越少。
村庄正在自我组织起来。奴隶们正演变成为一个由习惯性佃农（customary tenants）构成的共同体。他们聚集在一起商讨轮作事宜，解决彼此之间的争端，分摊共同的劳役负担。简言之，他们在经济领域正在培育出自治能力。
一旦耕作者的义务被固定下来而不能被任意增加，同时其劳动的全部剩余皆归己所有，奴隶制的心理状态便不复存在了。这个人基本上是在为自己劳作。他深知，付出更多的辛劳将为自己的家庭带来更丰盈的福祉。
主人分得的份额是一种进贡，一种捐税；它或轻或重，但却是一条边界。一个按习惯法规则交出自己一半劳动并保留另一半劳动的人，与一个任凭主人拿走全部产出且随主人心意施舍残余的奴隶之间，其区别绝非程度之分，而是种类之别。
农奴此时已走在通往自由的半途之中。他对其自身的劳动拥有了财产权，并且对其所耕种的土地也萌生了最初的所有权。

===Original===

Apart from domestic slaves within the household, slavery in the old sense which pagan antiquity gave that institution had been transformed out of all knowledge, and when, with the eleventh century, the true Middle Ages begin to spring from the soil of the Dark Ages, and a new civilisation to arise, though the old word *servus* (the Latin for a slave) is still used for the man who works the soil, his status in the now increasing number of documents which we can consult is wholly changed; we can certainly no longer translate the word by the English word “slave;” we are compelled to translate it by a new word with very different connotations: the word “serf.”
The serf of the early Middle Ages, of the eleventh and early twelfth centuries, of the Crusades and the Norman Conquest, is already nearly a peasant. He is indeed bound in legal theory to the soil upon which he was born. In social practice, all that is required of him is that his family should till its quota of servile land, and that the dues to the lord shall not fail from absence of labour. That duty fulfilled, it is easy and common for members of the serf-class to enter the professions and the Church, or to go wild; to become men practically free in the growing industries of the towns. With every passing generation the ancient servile conception of the labourer’s status grows more and more dim, and the courts and the practice of society treat him more and more as a man strictly bound to certain dues and to certain periodical labour within his industrial unit, but in all other respects free.
As the civilisation of the Middle Ages develops, as wealth increases and the arts progressively flourish, this character of freedom becomes more marked. In spite of attempts in time of scarcity (as after a plague) to insist upon the old rights to compulsory labour, the habit of commuting these rights for money-payments and dues has grown too strong to be resisted.
If at the end of the fourteenth century, let us say, or at the beginning of the fifteenth, you had visited some squire upon his estate in France or in England, he would have told you of the whole of it, “These are my lands.” But the peasant (as he now was) would have said also of his holding, “This is my land.” He could not be evicted from it. The dues which he was customarily bound to pay were but a fraction of its total produce.

===Chinese===

除了宅第内的家奴之外，古代异教赋予奴隶制的那种旧有含义上的奴隶制，早已蜕变到完全面目全非的地步；到了十一世纪，当中世纪迸发出充沛的思想与物质生机之时，西欧的大地上已遍布着除了名义之外在各方面都享有自由的农业人口。
农奴已经变成了农民。他的保有地（holding）已成为世袭财产。他向领主缴纳某些固定的货币、实物或劳役租税；但这已不再是专断的要求。这些都记录在庄园法庭卷宗（manorial rolls）之中，而佃户亲自出庭的庄园法庭（manor court），则捍卫着庄园的习俗以抗衡领主的侵凌。
只要缴纳了按惯例的租金，农奴便不可被驱逐。若是他去世，其子嗣在缴纳一笔传统继承金（relief）后便可理所当然地继承产业。这块土地已变成了这个家族的土地，正如领主自营地变成了领主的土地一样。
就连在领主自营地上的劳役，也逐渐折算折变为货币支付。领主发现，比起向怨愤不平的农奴强索勉强的劳役，雇佣劳工或将自营地出租给佃农更为便利；而农民也热切渴望赎买自己时间的完全自由。
农奴由此演变为了中世纪晚期的公簿持有农（copyholder）或自由农民。这一转变如此潜移默化、循序渐进，以至于人们几乎没有觉察到这种变革。这里没有发生流血的革命，没有议会废除奴隶制的宏大宣言。奴隶制度不过是在天主教文明微妙而无所不在的熏陶下悄然消融了。

===Original===

He could not always sell it, but it was always inheritable from father to son; and, in general, at the close of this long process of a thousand years the slave had become a free man for all the ordinary purposes of society. He bought and sold. He saved as he willed, he invested, he built, he drained at his discretion, and if he improved the land it was to his own profit.
Meanwhile, side by side with this emancipation of mankind in the direct line of descent from the old chattel slaves of the Roman *villa* went, in the Middle Ages, a crowd of institutions which all similarly made for a distribution of property, and for the destruction of even the fossil remnants of a then forgotten servile state. Thus industry of every kind in the towns, in transport, in crafts, and in commerce, was organised in the form of *guilds*. And a guild was a society partly cooperative, but in the main composed of private owners of capital whose corporation was self-governing, and was designed to check competition between its members: to prevent the growth of one at the expense of the other. Above all, most jealously did the guild safeguard the division of property, so that there should be formed within its ranks no proletariat upon the one side, and no monopolising capitalist upon the other.
There was a period of apprenticeship at a man’s entry into a guild, during which he worked for a master; but in time he became a master in his turn. The existence of such corporations as the normal units of industrial production, of commercial effort, and of the means of transport, is proof enough of what the social spirit was which had also enfranchised the labourer upon the land. And while such institutions flourished side by side with the no longer servile village communities, freehold or absolute possession of the soil, as distinguished from the tenure of the serf under the lord, also increased.
These three forms under which labour was exercised﻿—the serf, secure in his position, and burdened only with regular dues, which were but a fraction of his produce; the freeholder, a man independent save for money dues, which were more of a tax than a rent; the guild, in which well-divided capital worked cooperatively for craft production, for transport and for commerce﻿—all three between them were making for a society which should be based upon the principle of property.

===Chinese===

他虽然并不总能出卖土地，但土地却始终可以由父传子、代代相承；大体而言，在这场长达千年的漫长历程终点，奴隶在人类日常生活的全部通常意义上已然蜕变为自由人。他拥有自己的房屋、自己的土地、自己的牲畜与农具，以及属于自己的家庭生活。他是一个自治共同体的成员。
不仅如此。在农业劳动者获得解放的同时，城镇中也发生着一场相应的运动，孕育出了中世纪的行会（gild）制度。
城镇中的手工业匠人与商人联合成为法人团体；这些团体得到了王室（the Crown）与地方领主的认可，并被授予特许状，赋予他们对自己行业的控制权。
行会规定了从事某一行业所依循的条件：劳动工时、应付工资、所造货物的品质，以及出售货物的价格。它防止了会员之间的恶性竞争，确保了每一位师傅工匠都能获得合宜的生计。
与此同时，通过学徒与帮工（apprenticeship and journeymen）制度，行会提供了一架敞开的阶梯，任何勤勉的少年都可以凭借它攀升为主持工场的师傅。资本作为一种统治和剥削劳动的独立力量并不存在。工匠的工具归工匠本人所有。

===Original===

All, or most﻿—the normal family﻿—should own. And on ownership the freedom of the state should repose.
The state, as the minds of men envisaged it at the close of this process, was an agglomeration of families of varying wealth, but by far the greater number owners of the means of production. It was an agglomeration in which the stability of this “distributive” system (as I have called it) was guaranteed by the existence of cooperative bodies, binding men of the same craft or of the same village together; guaranteeing the small proprietor against loss of his economic independence, while at the same time it guaranteed society against the growth of a proletariat. If liberty of purchase and of sale, of mortgage and of inheritance was restricted, it was restricted with the social object of preventing the growth of an economic oligarchy which could exploit the rest of the community. The restraints upon liberty were restraints designed for the preservation of liberty; and every action of medieval society, from the flower of the Middle Ages to the approach of their catastrophe, was directed towards the establishment of a state in which men should be economically free through the possession of capital and of land.
Save here and there in legal formulae, or in rare patches isolated and eccentric, the servile institution had totally disappeared; nor must it be imagined that anything in the nature of collectivism had replaced it. There was common land, but it was common land jealously guarded by men who were also personal proprietors of other land. Common property in the village was but one of the forms of property, and was used rather as the flywheel to preserve the regularity of the cooperative machine than as a type of holding in any way peculiarly sacred. The guilds had property in common, but that property was the property necessary to their cooperative life, their halls, their funds for relief, their religious endowments. As for the instruments of their trades, those instruments were owned by the individual members, *not* by the guild, save where they were of so expensive a kind as to necessitate a corporate control.
Such was the transformation which had come over European society in the course of ten Christian centuries.

===Chinese===

所有家庭，或绝大多数家庭——即通常的家庭——皆应拥有财产。而国家的自由应当奠基于所有权之上。
在这一进程的终点，人们心目中所构想的国家是一个个家庭的联合体，其中绝大多数家庭都拥有自己耕作的土地和使用的工具。合作精神无处不在：在村庄会议中，在城镇行会中，在宗教互助会中。它将人们维系在互助互责的网络之中，这并非靠国家的外在强制，而是通过自愿团体的有机凝聚力。
小财产所有者乃是公民的常态典型。社会的构建旨在保护他的财产所有权，防止他被更富有者的竞争所碾碎，或因高利贷而破产。高利贷（usury）遭到教会法的禁止并受到王国法庭的惩处。对生活必需品的投机倒把受到严厉压制。“公正价格（just price）”的理念主导着一切经济交易。
这是一个由所有者构成的社会，他们由彼此的义务纽结在一起，奠基于自由农民和自治工匠阶级的广阔基础之上。这便是历史上真实存在的“**分散所有制国家（distributive state）**”：并非书斋里的空谈理论，亦非乌托邦式的幻梦，而是我们的欧洲先祖切实建立并享有的鲜活现实。
除了在法律文书的只言片语中，或在罕见而孤立奇特的个别角落里，奴役制度已然彻底消失了；而且绝不可设想是有任何带有集体主义性质的东西取代了它。当时固然存在公地，但那是被同样作为其他土地私人所有者的人们所戒备守护的公地。村庄中的共有财产不过是财产的各种形式之一，其作用与其说是作为某种格外神圣的占有形式，不如说是作为维持合作机器规整运转的飞轮。行会拥有共有财产，但那些财产只是行会开展合作生活所必需的财产——行会会所、救济基金、宗教捐产。至于各行各业的工具，除因其造价极为昂贵而必须实施集体监管者外，那些工具皆归个别会员所有，而*不*归行会所有。
这便是在基督教的十个世纪历程中，欧洲社会所经历的翻天覆地的转变。

===Original===

Slavery had gone, and in its place had come that establishment of free possession which seemed so normal to men, and so consonant to a happy human life. No particular name was then found for it. Today, and now that it has disappeared, we must construct an awkward one, and say that the Middle Ages had instinctively conceived and brought into existence the **distributive state**.
That excellent consummation of human society passed, as we know, and was in certain provinces of Europe, but more particularly in Britain, destroyed.
For a society in which the determinant mass of families were owners of capital and of land; for one in which production was regulated by self-governing corporations of small owners; and for one in which the misery and insecurity of a proletariat was unknown, there came to be substituted the dreadful moral anarchy against which all moral effort is now turned, and which goes by the name of “capitalism.”
How did such a catastrophe come about? Why was it permitted, and upon what historical process did the evil batten? What turned an England economically free into the England which we know today, of which at least one-third is indigent, of which nineteen-twentieths are dispossessed of capital and of land, and of which the whole industry and national life is controlled upon its economic side by a few chance directors of millions, a few masters of unsocial and irresponsible monopolies?
The answer most usually given to this fundamental question in our history, and the one most readily accepted, is that this misfortune came about through a material process known as the “Industrial Revolution.” The use of expensive machinery, the concentration of industry and of its implements are imagined to have enslaved, in some blind way, apart from the human will, the action of English mankind.
The explanation is wholly false. No such material cause determined the degradation from which we suffer.
It was the deliberate action of men, evil will in a few and apathy of will among the many, which produced a catastrophe as human in its causes and inception as in its vile effect.
Capitalism was not the growth of the industrial movement, nor of chance material discoveries. A little acquaintance with history and a little straightforwardness in the teaching of it would be enough to prove that.

===Chinese===

奴隶制已经远去，取而代之的是自由占有制的牢固确立，这种制度在人们看来如此平常自然，如此契合人类幸福的生活。彼时并未为此特设专属名称。今日，既然它已然消逝，我们便不得不生造一个略显笨拙的词汇，称中世纪凭借本能构想并催生了“**分散所有制国家（distributive state）**”。
如我们所知，人类社会的这一完美成就最终逝去了，并在欧洲的某些省份，尤其是大不列颠（Britain），遭到了彻底摧毁。
一个绝大多数家庭皆为资本与土地所有者的社会，一个生产由小所有者自治团体所调控的社会，一个不知无产阶级的悲惨与朝不保夕为何物的社会，被一种可怕的道德无政府状态取而代之；如今人类的一切道德努力皆用于对抗这种状态，其名目便叫作“资本主义（capitalism）”。
这样一场灾难究竟是如何降临的？它为何被容许发生？这股恶势力是依凭何种历史进程滋生蔓延的？是什么把一个经济上自由的英格兰，变成了我们今日所目睹的英格兰——在这里，至少有三分之一的人口陷于贫困，二十分之十九的人口被剥夺了资本与土地，全国的工业与国民生活在经济层面上被少数侥幸掌控数百万资财的总监、少数反社会且不负责任的垄断寡头所操纵？
对我们历史上这一根本问题的最寻常回答，也是最容易为人所接受的解释，便是这场灾祸是源于一场被称为“工业革命（Industrial Revolution）”的物质进程。昂贵机器的采用、工业及其生产工具的集聚，被人们想象为脱离了人的意志、以某种盲目的方式奴役了英国人的行为。
这一解释完全是虚妄的。绝非任何此类物质原因决定了我们今日所遭受的堕落。
正是人们深思熟虑的行径——少数人的邪随意念与大众的意志冷漠——酿成了这场在起因和开端上与在其卑鄙后果上同样由人为造成的灾难。
资本主义并非工业运动的产物，亦非偶发物质发现的结果。对历史稍有涉猎，并在传授历史时稍具坦诚直率之心，便足以证明这一点。

===Original===

The industrial system was a growth proceeding from capitalism, not its cause. Capitalism was here in England before the industrial system came into being;﻿—before the use of coal and of the new expensive machinery, and of the concentration of the implements of production in the great towns. Had capitalism not been present before the Industrial Revolution, that revolution might have proved as beneficent to Englishmen as it has proved maleficent. But capitalism﻿—that is, the ownership by a few of the springs of life﻿—was present long before the great discoveries came. It warped the effect of these discoveries and new inventions, and it turned them from a good into an evil thing. It was not machinery that lost us our freedom; it was the loss of a free mind.

===Chinese===

工业制度乃是从资本主义中衍生出来的产物，而非其肇因。早在工业制度问世之前，资本主义便已存在于英格兰这片土地上——早于煤炭的使用，早于新型昂贵机器的采用，也早于生产工具在大城镇中的高度集中。倘若在工业革命之前未曾存在资本主义，那么这场革命本可向英国人证明其如今日所证明的有害那般造福于民。然而资本主义——亦即少数人对生存源泉的占有——早在那些伟大发现问世之前便已存在。它扭曲了这些发现与新发明所带来的影响，使其由善事化为了恶端。使我们丧失自由的并非机器，而是自由心灵的丧失。

## II Our Civilisation Was Originally Servile / 第二章 我们的文明起初是奴役性的
===Original===

The servile institution in pagan antiquity﻿—Its fundamental character﻿—A pagan society took it for granted﻿—The institution disturbed by the advent of the Christian Church.
In no matter what field of the European past we make our research, we find, from two thousand years ago upwards, one fundamental institution whereupon the whole of society reposes; that fundamental institution is slavery.
There is here no distinction between the highly civilised city-state of the Mediterranean, with its letters, its plastic art, and its code of laws, with all that makes a civilisation﻿—and this stretching back far beyond any surviving record﻿—there is here no distinction between that civilised body and the Northern and Western societies of the Celtic tribes, or of the little known hordes that wandered in the Germanies. *All* indifferently reposed upon slavery. It was a fundamental conception of society. It was everywhere present, nowhere disputed.
There *is* a distinction (or would appear to be) between Europeans and Asiatics in this matter. The religion and morals of the one so differed in their very origin from those of the other that every social institution was touched by the contrast﻿—and slavery among the rest.
But with that we need not concern ourselves. My point is that our European ancestry, those men from whom we are descended and whose blood runs with little admixture in our veins, took slavery for granted, made of it the economic pivot upon which the production of wealth should turn, and never doubted but that it was normal to all human society.
It is a matter of capital importance to seize this.
An arrangement of such a sort would not have endured without intermission (and indeed without question) for many centuries, nor have been found emerging fully grown from that vast space of unrecorded time during which barbarism and civilisation flourished side by side in Europe, had there not been something in it, good or evil, native to our blood.
There was no question in those ancient societies from which we spring of making subject races into slaves by the might of conquering races. All that is the guesswork of the universities. Not only is there no proof of it, rather all the existing proof is the other way. The Greek had a Greek slave, the Latin a Latin slave, the German a German slave, the Celt a Celtic slave.

===Chinese===

异教古代的奴役制度——其根本性质——异教社会视其为理所当然——基督教会（the Christian Church）的降临打破了这一制度。
无论我们在欧洲（Europe）过去的哪个领域进行探究，都会发现从两千年前向上溯源，整个社会都奠基于一个根本制度之上；那个根本制度就是奴隶制（slavery）。
在地中海高度文明的城邦国家——拥有文学、造型艺术、法典以及构成文明的一切，且其历史远超任何存世记载——与凯尔特部落（the Celtic tribes）或漫游于日耳曼地区的鲜为人知的游群等北部和西部社会之间，并无二致。*一切*社会皆毫无分别地奠基于奴隶制之上。它是社会的一种根本观念。它无处不在，无处见疑。
在此事上，欧洲人与亚洲人之间确实存在（或看似存在）区别。两者的宗教与道德从其发源之初就截然不同，以致每一种社会制度都带有这种对照的印记——奴隶制亦在其中。
但我们无须对此挂怀。我的论点是：我们的欧洲先祖——我们从其传续而来且其血脉纯正流淌于我们体内的那些人——视奴隶制为理所当然，将其作为财富生产所依凭的经济中枢，并且从不怀疑它对于整个人类社会而言皆是常态。
领会这一点是至关重要的。
倘若这种社会安排中没有某种——不论善恶——深植于我们血脉之中的东西，它就不可能在许多个世纪里毫无间断（实际上也毫无疑问）地延续下来，也不可能在那段野蛮与文明在欧洲并存的未载于史书的漫长岁月中，以完备成熟之姿呈现出来。
在孕育了我们的那些古代社会中，根本不存在征服民族凭借强力将臣服种族变为奴隶的问题。所有那些说法不过是大学学者们的臆测。不仅没有任何证据支持这一点，相反，所有存世证据都指向相反的方向。希腊人拥有希腊人奴隶，拉丁人拥有拉丁人奴隶，日耳曼人拥有日耳曼人奴隶，凯尔特人拥有凯尔特人奴隶。

===Original===

The theory that “superior races” invaded a land, either drove out the original inhabitants or reduced them to slavery, is one which has no argument either from our present knowledge of man’s mind or from recorded evidence. Indeed, the most striking feature of that servile basis upon which paganism reposed was the human equality recognised between master and slave. The master might kill the slave, but both were of one race and each was human to the other.
This spiritual value was not, as a further pernicious piece of guesswork would dream, a “growth” or a “progress.” The doctrine of human equality was inherent in the very stuff of antiquity, as it is inherent in those societies which have not lost tradition.
We may presume that the barbarian of the North would grasp the great truth with less facility than the civilised man of the Mediterranean, because barbarism everywhere shows a retrogression in intellectual power; but the proof that the servile institution was a social arrangement rather than a distinction of type is patent from the coincidence everywhere of emancipation with slavery. Pagan Europe not only thought the existence of slaves a natural necessity to society, but equally thought that upon giving a slave his freedom the enfranchised man would naturally step, though perhaps after the interval of some lineage, into the ranks of free society. Great poets and great artists, statesmen and soldiers were little troubled by the memory of a servile ancestry.
On the other hand, there was a perpetual recruitment of the servile institution, just as there was a perpetual emancipation from it, proceeding year after year; and the natural or normal method of recruitment is most clearly apparent to us in the simple and barbaric societies which the observation of contemporary civilised pagans enables us to judge.
It was poverty that made the slave.
Prisoners of war taken in set combat afforded one mode of recruitment, and there was also the raiding of men by pirates in the outer lands and the selling of them in the slave markets of the South. But at once the cause of the recruitment and the permanent support of the institution of slavery was the indigence of the man who sold himself into slavery, *or was born into it*; for it was a rule of pagan slavery that the slave bred the slave, and that even if one of the parents were free the offspring was a slave.

===Chinese===

认为“优等民族”入侵某地并将原住民驱逐或贬为奴隶的理论，无论根据我们当前对人类心灵的认知，还是根据存世文献的佐证，都毫无立足之地。事实上，异教文明赖以建立的奴役根基中最引人注目的特征，恰恰是主人与奴隶之间所承认的人格平等。主人可以处死奴隶，但两者属于同一民族，且在彼此眼中皆是人类同侪。
这种精神价值并不像另一批有害的臆测者所梦想的那样，是一种“发展”或“进步”。人类平等的信条本就深植于古代社会的肌理之中，正如它深植于那些尚未丧失传统纽带的社会之中一样。
我们可以推测，北方的蛮族在把握这一伟大的真理时不如地中海的文明人那般容易，因为各处的野蛮状态都表现出智力上的退化；但证明奴役制度是一种社会安排而非人种差别的明证，在于解放（emancipation）与奴隶制在各处的普遍并存。异教欧洲不仅认为奴隶的存在是社会的一种自然必需，而且同样认为，一旦赋予奴隶自由，被解放者便会自然而然地（尽管可能要相隔数代）跨入自由社会的行列。伟大的诗人、伟大的艺术家、政治家和军人，很少因背负奴隶血统的记忆而感到困扰。
另一方面，正如年复一年不断有奴隶获得解放一样，奴役制度也始终存在着源源不断的补充机制；而我们通过观察当代文明异教徒记录的朴素与蛮荒社会，可以最清晰地窥见其自然或常态的补充方式。
是贫困造就了奴隶。
在正规交战中俘获的战俘提供了一种补充途径，这在罗马征服高卢（Gaul）等大规模战争中尤为显著。但这并非主要方式。主要方式是债务，是饥荒，是对保护的渴望。一个人因无法偿还债务而将自己卖身为奴；一位父亲因无力抚养子女而将其出卖；在灾荒之年，人们成群结队地走向拥有粮食储备者的宅门，以出让自身的自由来换取生存的面包。

===Original===

The society of antiquity, therefore, was normally divided (as must at last be the society of any servile state) into clearly marked sections: there was upon the one hand the citizen who had a voice in the conduct of the state, who would often labour﻿—but labour of his own free will﻿—and who was normally possessed of property; upon the other hand, there was a mass dispossessed of the means of production and compelled by positive law to labour at command.
It is true that in the further developments of society the accumulation of private savings by a slave was tolerated and that slaves so favoured did sometimes purchase their freedom.
It is further true that in the confusion of the last generations of paganism there arose in some of the great cities a considerable class of men who, though free, were dispossessed of the means of production. But these last never existed in a sufficient proportion to stamp the whole state of society with a character drawn from their proletarian circumstance. To the end the pagan world remained a world of free proprietors possessed, in various degrees, of the land and of the capital whereby wealth may be produced, and applying to that land and capital for the purpose of producing wealth, *compulsory* labour.
Certain features in that original servile state from which we all spring should be carefully noted by way of conclusion.
First, though all nowadays contrast slavery with freedom to the advantage of the latter, yet men then accepted slavery freely as an alternative to indigence.
Secondly (and this is most important for our judgment of the servile institution as a whole, and of the chances of its return), in all those centuries we find no organised effort, nor (what is still more significant) do we find any *complaint of conscience* against the institution which condemned the bulk of human beings to forced labour.
Slaves may be found in the literary exercises of the time bewailing their lot and joking about it; some philosophers will complain that an ideal society should contain no slaves; others will excuse the establishment of slavery upon this plea or that, while granting that it offends the dignity of man. The greater part will argue of the state that it is necessarily servile. But no one, slave or free, dreams of abolishing or even of changing the thing. You have no martyrs for the case of “freedom” as against “slavery.”

===Chinese===

当然，奴隶制度在实践中表现为各种复杂的层次。在乡村庄园里，有从事繁重粗粝劳作的田野奴隶；在城市中，有受过高等教育的家庭教师、文书、管账人、医生乃至工场管理者，他们自身虽为奴隶，却往往享受着优渥的生活待遇，甚至享有某些财产处置的默契权利（peculium），受宠者有时还能借此购赎自身的自由。
此外，在异教晚期的混乱之中，某些大城市里确实出现了一个相当庞大的阶级：他们虽然是自由人，却被剥夺了生产资料。但这部分人从未达到能够用其无产境况为整个社会形态定性的比例。直至终局，异教世界始终是一个由自由所有者构成的世界：他们以不同程度占有可以生产财富的土地与资本，并为了生产财富的目的，对那些土地和资本施以*强制性*劳动。
在我们大家皆源于其中的那种最初的奴役国家中，有若干特征理应作为结论予以细心注意。
首先，尽管今日所有人都将奴隶制与自由相对照并倾向于推崇后者，但在那时，人们却是自愿接受奴隶制以作为免于匮乏的替代抉择。
其次（这对于我们对整个奴役制度以及对其卷土重来之几率的评判而言，是至关重要的），在所有那些世纪里，我们看不到任何有组织的对抗努力，甚至（这是更为意味深长的）找不到任何针对那项将绝大多数人类判处为强制劳动的制度的*良心抗议（complaint of conscience）*。
在那个时代的文学习作中，你可以看到奴隶们哀叹自己的命运或以此打趣；某些哲学家会抱怨理想社会中不应包含奴隶；其他一些人则会以这样或那样的理由为奴隶制的设立辩解，同时承认它有损人的尊严。绝大多数人会论证国家在本质上必然是奴役性的。但没有任何人——不论是奴隶还是自由人——梦想过废除乃至改变这一事物。你找不到任何为了“自由”对抗“奴役”而献身的殉道者。

===Original===

The so-called servile wars are the resistance on the part of escaped slaves to any attempt at recapture, but they are not accompanied by an accepted affirmation that servitude is an intolerable thing; nor is that note struck at all from the unknown beginnings to the Catholic endings of the pagan world. Slavery is irksome, undignified, woeful; but it is, to them, of the nature of things.
You may say, to be brief, that this arrangement of society was the very air which pagan antiquity breathed.
Its great works, its leisure and its domestic life, its humour, its reserves of power, all depend upon the fact that its society was that of the servile state.
Men were happy in that arrangement, or, at least, as happy as men ever are.
The attempt to escape by a personal effort, whether of thrift, of adventure, or of flattery to a master, from the servile condition had never even so much of driving power behind it as the attempt many show today to escape from the rank of wage-earners to those of employers. Servitude did not seem a hell into which a man would rather die than sink, or out of which at any sacrifice whatsoever a man would raise himself. It was a condition accepted by those who suffered it as much as by those who enjoyed it, and a perfectly necessary part of all that men did and thought.
You find no barbarian from some free place astonished at the institution of slavery; you find no slave pointing to a society in which slavery was unknown as towards a happier land. To our ancestors not only for those few centuries during which we have record of their actions, but apparently during an illimitable past, the division of society into those who must work under compulsion and those who would benefit by their labour was the very plan of the state apart from which they could hardly think of society as existing at all.
Let all this be clearly grasped. It is fundamental to an understanding of the problem before us. Slavery is no novel experience in the history of Europe; nor is one suffering an odd dream when one talks of slavery as acceptable to European men.

===Chinese===

所谓奴隶战争，乃是逃亡奴隶对任何重新抓捕企图的抵抗，但它们并未伴随某种普遍公认的“奴役是不可容忍之事”的宣言；从异教世界不为人知的源头直到其天主教结局，这种论调根本从未奏响过。奴隶制固然令人厌烦、有失体统、凄惨悲哀；但在他们看来，这属于事物的本性。
简而言之，你可以说这种社会安排乃是异教古代所呼吸的空气本身。
它的伟大工程、它的闲暇与家庭生活、它的幽默、它的力量储备，全部取决于其社会乃是一个奴役国家（the Servile State）这一事实。
人们在那种社会安排中过得很快乐，或者至少与人类以往能够体会到的一样快乐。
试图通过个人努力（不论是凭借节俭、冒险，还是向主人献媚）摆脱奴役处境的企图，其背后的推动力甚至远远比不上今日许多人试图从工资挣取者行列跃升为雇主阶层的劲头。奴役并不被视为一个人宁死也不愿坠入的地狱，亦非人不惜一切代价也必须挣脱的深渊。它是承受者与享用者双方共同接受的处境，是人类全部言行与思考中完全不可或缺的一部分。
你找不到任何来自自由区域的蛮族会对奴隶制感到惊愕；你也找不到任何奴隶会将一个不知奴隶制为何物的社会视为更幸福的乐土而心向往之。对我们的先祖而言，不仅在我们保有其行为记录的短短几个世纪里是如此，显然在无限久远的过去同样如此：社会划分为必须在强制下劳动的人与从其劳动中获益的人，乃是国家的基本蓝图，脱离了它，他们甚至几乎无法想象社会能够存在。
务请将这一切牢牢把握。这是理解横亘在我们面前的问题的基石。在欧洲历史上，奴隶制绝非新奇的经验；当有人谈及奴隶制可以为欧洲人所接受时，他也绝非在痴人说梦。

===Original===

Slavery was of the very stuff of Europe for thousands upon thousands of years, until Europe engaged upon that considerable moral experiment called “the Faith,” which many believe to be now accomplished and discarded, and in the failure of which it would seem that the old and primary institution of slavery must return.
For there came upon us Europeans after all those centuries, and centuries of a settled social order which was erected upon slavery as upon a sure foundation, the experiment called the Christian Church.
Among the byproducts of this experiment, very slowly emerging from the old pagan world, and not long completed before Christendom itself suffered a shipwreck, was the exceedingly gradual transformation of the servile state into something other: a society of owners. And how that something other did proceed from the pagan servile state I will next explain.

===Chinese===

成千上万年以来，奴隶制乃是欧洲肌理本身的一部分，直至欧洲投身于那场名为“信仰（the Faith）”的伟大道德试验——许多人认为这一试验如今已经告成并被抛弃，而在其失败之处，古老而原初的奴隶制度似乎必然会卷土重来。
因为历经了所有那些世纪、历经了建立在作为坚实基础的奴隶制之上的安定社会秩序的千百年之后，一场被称为基督教会的试验降临到了我们欧洲人身上。
作为从古老异教世界中极其缓慢孕育出来的这项试验的副产品之一，且在基督教世界自身遭遇沉船之前不久才告完成的，便是奴役国家极其渐进地转变为另一种形态：一个所有者的社会。而那另一种形态究竟是如何从异教奴役国家中衍生出来的，我将在下一章中予以阐释。

## Preface to Second Edition / 第二版序言
===Original===

With the issue of a second edition of this book the author may perhaps be excused for adding, by way of preface, a few words upon the thesis it maintains and the method through which that thesis is treated.
It appears the more necessary to do so because a careful comparison of the reviews and other expressions of opinion which it has received convinces the author that parts of his argument are liable to misconception. It would be a pity to correct such misconception by any changes in a completed book; a few words set down here by way of preface should be sufficient for the purpose.
First: I would point out that the argument contained in the book bears no relation to the common accusation levelled against socialists (that is, collectivists) that life in a socialist state would be so subject to regulation and order as to be unduly oppressive. With this common objection to the reform advocated by socialists I have nothing to do in this book, nor can it touch my subject at any point. This book does not discuss the socialist state. Indeed it is the very heart of my thesis that we are not, as a fact, approaching socialism at all, but a very different state of society; to wit, a society in which the capitalist class shall be even more powerful and far more secure than it is at present: a society in which the proletarian mass shall not suffer from particular regulations, oppressive or beneficent, but shall change their status, lose their present legal freedom, and be subject to compulsory labour.
Next, I would beg my readers to believe that I have not attempted to set up this thesis as a warning or as a piece of gloom. I say nowhere in the book that the reestablishment of slavery would be a bad thing as compared with our present insecurity, and no one has a right to read such an opinion into this book. Upon the contrary, I say clearly enough that I think the tendency towards the reestablishment of slavery is due to the very fact that the new conditions may be found more tolerable than those obtaining under capitalism. Which state of society might reasonably be preferred﻿—the reestablishment of slavery or the maintenance of capitalism﻿—would make an ample subject for another book: but that alternative does not concern this volume or the thesis therein maintained.

===Chinese===

随着本书第二版的刊行，作者或许可以借序言之机，就书中所持的论点以及论述该论点的方法略陈数语，以祈读者鉴谅。
这样做似乎尤有必要，因为仔细比照本书所收到的书评及其他意见表达，使作者确信：拙著论证的某些部分容易引起误解。若通过对一本已定稿的著作进行删改来修正此类误解，未免令人遗憾；借序言在此略陈数语，对于这一目的而言应当足够了。
第一：我谨指出，书中所包含的论证与针对社会主义者（即集体主义者）的常见指责毫无关联——那种指责称，社会主义国家中的生活将受到过多的规制与秩序约束，以致变得过度压抑。对于社会主义者所倡导的改革的这种常见异议，我在本书中未作任何探讨，它也绝不会在任何环节触及拙著的主题。本书并不讨论社会主义国家。事实上，拙著论点的精髓恰恰在于：我们在事实上根本没有走向社会主义，而是在走向一种迥然不同的社会形态；换言之，即走向一个资本家阶级比目前更加强大、远为稳固的社会：一个无产阶级大众所遭受的并非某些具体的压迫性或善意的规制，而是其身份发生转变、丧失当下的法律自由并被迫服从强制劳动的社会。
第二，我恳请读者相信，我绝非试图将这一论点作为某种警告或悲观论调来立论。我在全书中绝未断言奴隶制的重建相比于我们目前朝不保夕的不安定状态是一件坏事，任何人都无权将此类观点强加于本书。恰恰相反，我说得足够清楚：我认为走向奴隶制重建的趋势，正是由于人们可能会发现新状态比资本主义下的现状更容易忍受。哪种社会形态在理性上更可取——是重建奴隶制，还是维持资本主义——足以成为另一部著作的充实主题：但这种二选一的抉择并不关涉本书或其中所坚持的论点。

===Original===

Finally, I would beg such of my readers as are socialist by conviction not to misconceive my opinion upon what their movement is effecting. The most sincere and the best writer among the English socialists wrote of this book that the author had mistaken the “social reform” of the professional politicians for socialism, and that while this “social reform” might be tending towards the reestablishment of compulsory labour for the benefit of an owning class, yet socialism had no such intention or tendency.
Now I never made such an error. What I have said in this book is that the object of the socialist (a very simple and clear matter﻿—the putting of the means of production into the hands of politicians to hold in trust for the community) is not in practice being approached; that we are not, *as a matter of fact*, coming nearer towards the collective ownership of the means of production, but that we *are* rapidly coming nearer to the establishment of compulsory labour among an unfree majority of non-owners for the benefit of a free minority of owners. And I say that this tendency is due to the fact that the socialist ideal, in conflict with and yet informing the body of capitalism, produces a third thing very different from the socialist ideal﻿—to wit, the servile state. It is important to have this point clear, and perhaps a metaphor is needed. I will present one.
A traveller sincerely desirous of escaping from the cold climate of the mountains conceives the obvious plan of going south, where he will find lower and warmer land. With this project in his head he finds a river flowing in a southerly direction and he says, “If I travel upon this river I will reach my object the more readily.” One who has studied the nature of that mountainous region may say to him: “You are in error. The very evils from which you are trying to escape, the mountains, are so constructed that in a short while you will find them diverting the course of this river northward again. Indeed, if you will look at your compass you will see that the big bend has already begun.”
The traveller is the socialist. The south which he desires to reach is the collectivist state. The river is modern “organised reform.” The northern country where the mountain river will ultimately find a quiet bed is a society reposing upon compulsory labour.

===Chinese===

最后，我恳请那些笃信社会主义的读者，不要误解我对他们的运动正在产生之效果的看法。在英国社会主义者中最为真诚且最优秀的著述家评论本书时写道：作者把职业政客的“社会改革”误当成了社会主义；尽管这种“社会改革”可能正在趋于为占有者阶级的利益重建强制劳动，但社会主义绝无此类意图或趋势。
我从未犯过这样的错误。我在本书中所阐明的是：社会主义者的目标（这是一件非常简单明了的事情——即将生产资料置于政客之手，由其代共同体托管）在实践中并未被接近；*在事实层面上*，我们并未日益接近生产资料的集体所有制，但我们*确实*正在迅速接近在不占有财产的非自由多数人之中确立强制劳动，以谋取占有财产的自由少数人的利益。而且我认为，这种趋势是由于社会主义理想在与资本主义机体发生冲突却又贯注于其中的过程中，催生出了某种与社会主义理想大相径庭的第三种事物——即奴役国家（the Servile State）。澄清这一点是重要的，或许需要一个隐喻。我在此提供一个。
一位真诚渴望逃避山区寒冷气候的旅人，萌生了显而易见的向南进发的计划，在那儿他将找到地势更低、气候更温暖的土地。怀着这一打算，他发现了一条向南流淌的河流，便自言自语道：“若我顺着这条河漂流，就能更快达到我的目的。”一位研习过该山区地形的人可能会对他说：“你错了。你正竭力逃避的那些祸患本身——群山——其构造决定了不久之后它们就会迫使这条河流重新向北改道。事实上，如果你看一看罗盘，就会发现……”

===Original===

A man thus speaking to the traveller would not be denying either the sincerity of his desire to get southward or his belief that the river would lead him there; all he would be denying would be the fact that the river does lead him there.
There is only one discrepancy in this parallel, which is that the traveller in the metaphor could, upon being convinced of his error, leave the river and get south by land. That would correspond in the case of the socialist to a bold policy of confiscation, to a taking of the means of production from the hands of those who now own them and to a placing of them in the hands of politicians to be held in trust for the community.
I nowhere deny in my book that this is ideally possible: just as it is ideally possible that tomorrow all Englishmen shall take and preserve for twenty-four hours a vow of silence. What I say is that nothing like it or approaching it has ever been done or is now being done. I say further, what is of capital importance, that with every step taken along the existing lines of change in our industrial society we are making it more and more difficult to retrace such steps, to abandon the accepted method and to pursue the collectivist ideal. The path of confiscation, the only way by which socialists can reach their goal, gets more and more remote with every new and positive economic reform, undertaken, remember, with the aid and under the advice of socialists themselves.
These, then, are the three main points, I think, upon which there has been misconception and against which I hope I may warn the reader. To recapitulate:﻿—
1. The misconception that I have used the word “servile” in some rhetorical sense of “irksome” or “oppressive,” whereas I have attempted to use it only under the limits of my definition, viz., that labour is “servile” which is undertaken not in fulfilment of contract but under the compulsion of positive law and which attaches to the status of the labourer, and is performed for the benefit of others who are under no such compulsion.
2. The misconception that the advent of the servile state is put forward for a warning or a danger-sign: I am concerned in this book to say how and why we *are* approaching it; not whether we *should* approach it.
3. The misconception that I have misstated the aims and the convictions of socialists.

===Chinese===

“……这条河流虽然在此刻确实流向南方，但甚至在奔流出数哩之前，就正在向东偏转；它的主要干流将带你走向深山腹地一块更加冰冷、更加荒芜的盆地，你在那里将遭受四面群山的围困。”
这种解释并不意味着建议那位旅人走向寒冷的山谷。它并不包含对温暖平原的谴责。它只是把自然事实呈现给一个盲目追随一条看似通向他心之所向、实则通向他心之所恶之途径的人。
对于社会主义者而言正是如此。他的理想是一个所有公民在政治与经济上皆享有平等自由的社会；他们将通过选出官员来托管全部生产资料，从而享受这种自由。
资本主义的现实是一个社会被划分为两个阶级：一小撮人在经济上处于自由地位并占有生产资料；大众则在政治上自由，但在经济上不自由，被剥夺了生产资料。
社会主义者带着他对国家改造的热切渴望，来到了这样一个社会之中。他认为，若由国家接管某项工业或通过法案来调控其运作，他便是在迈向他的理想。
他没有看到的是：资本主义社会的肌体在吸纳这种社会主义冲动之时，并没有把它转变为社会主义，而是把它改造成了资本主义的补充物。生产资料依然握在资本家手中；资本家被赋予了更强大的国家保障，甚至国家以其全部警察力量和法律强制来保证无产阶级稳定地劳作，以换取最低限度的温饱和保障。社会主义者原本希望消灭剥削，其结果却是使剥削制度化并永久化。

===Original===

These aims and convictions are simple enough, and my point is not that they are either illusions or doubtful, but that in point of fact we are not heading towards them and that the effect of socialist doctrine upon capitalist society is to produce a third thing different from either of its two begetters﻿—to wit, the servile state.
Apart from these three main points I must, in view of certain less intelligent criticisms the book has provoked, mention one or two other matters.
Thus, my argument that slavery was slowly transformed and that the old pagan servile state slowly approached a distributive state under the influence of the Catholic Church is not a piece of special pleading put forward to please my coreligionists. It is a plain piece of historical fact which anyone can verify for himself, and which many do not regard as an advantage, but as a disadvantage inflicted upon humanity by the advent of this religion. Whether the servile institution be a good or a bad thing, it did, as a matter of fact, slowly disappear as Catholic civilisation developed; and it has, as a matter of fact, slowly begun to return where Catholic civilisation has receded.
Nor have I said that the goal of a completely free distributive state was ever reached. I have said that it was in process of formation when the disruption of our united European civilisation in the sixteenth century arrested its development and slowly produced, in this country especially, capitalism in its stead.
Again, examples of state regulation and of state or municipal economic enterprise increasing rapidly among us obviously do not affect my argument. Unless or until these are based upon a policy of confiscation they are no more an example of socialism than the explosion of gunpowder is an example of warfare. They are no more “socialistic efforts” or “beginnings” or “experiments in socialism” than fireworks at the Crystal Palace are “military” efforts or “beginnings” or “experiments in militarism.” Socialism would indeed involve such regulations and such municipal enterprise just as war involves the explosion of gunpowder; but they do not form its essence at all. Its essence consists in vesting in trust with the politicians what is now private property.

===Chinese===

我的论点并非认为社会主义理想本身是虚幻或可疑的，而是指出在事实层面上我们并没有朝向它们迈进，社会主义学说对资本主义社会的作用乃是产生出一种不同于其两造父体的第三种事物——亦即奴役国家。
除了这三个要点之外，鉴于本书所招致的某些缺乏见识的批评，我必须提及另外一两件事。
因此，关于奴隶制被缓慢改造、古老的异教奴役国家在天主教会（the Catholic Church）的影响下逐渐走向分散所有制国家（distributive state）的论证，绝非为了取悦我的同教信徒而提出的一家之言。这是一个任何人皆可亲自核实的清晰历史事实，且许多人并不将其视为一种益处，而是视为这一宗教降临施加给人类的祸患。不论奴役制度是善是恶，在事实上，随着天主教文明的发展，它确实慢慢消失了；而在事实上，凡是天主教文明发生倒退之处，它又慢慢开始卷土重来。
我也从未断言完全自由的分散所有制国家的目标曾经彻底实现过。我说的是它当时正处于形成过程之中，而十六世纪我们统一的欧洲（European）文明的分裂阻碍了它的发展，并逐渐产生出（尤其是在本国）资本主义（capitalism）以取代它。
此外，我们周围迅速增多的国家规制以及国家或市政经济事业的例子，显然丝毫不影响我的论证。除非或直至这些举措建立在没收政策的基础之上，否则它们算不上是社会主义的范例，正如火药的爆炸算不上是战争的范例一样。它们算不上“社会主义的努力”或“开端”或“社会主义试验”，正如水晶宫（the Crystal Palace）的焰火算不上“军事”努力或“开端”或“军国主义试验”一样。社会主义确实会涉及此类规制和市政事业，正如战争涉及火药的爆炸一样；但它们根本不构成社会主义的本质。其本质在于将如今属于私人的财产托付给政客代管。

===Original===

When municipal and state enterprise accompanied by municipal and state regulation is based upon loans instead of confiscation, nay, loans devised to *avoid* confiscation, it is a negation of socialism; and I have shown that attempts to mask the capitalist character of such operations by the machinery of sinking funds and the rest are logically worthless. You cannot “buy out” capitalism.
I need not point out what steps have been taken, even in the very short time since this book first appeared, in the direction which it is intended to explain. We already have wages boards in one great industry; we shall shortly have them in more. We already have the registration of the proletariat with name, address, movement from place to place, nature of illness when illness is incurred, supposed or real “malingering,” indulgence in this or that vice (such as drink), domestic habits, nature of employment, and all the rest of it very nearly complete, and *imposed by the wealthier classes who are the actual gatherers of the Poll Tax upon which this registration is based*. We have through the labour exchanges a system which will soon be equally complete and by which every member of the proletariat will ultimately be similarly registered as a worker, his tendencies to rebellion against capital known and their frequency set down, how far he is willing to serve capitalism, whether and when he has refused service, and if so where and why.
The reader will be interested to note amid the accidents and reactions of the years immediately before us the slow perfection of this system: registration and control of the proletariat, with its necessary and fatal approach towards the term of compulsory labour. But I think in justice to my book I should point out to that same reader the meaning of its concluding pages. No change in European society arrives at completion unless it is universal throughout Europe. Capitalism is not thus universal; it is developed in very different degrees in different parts of Europe; the advent of servitude is therefore a probability differing in degree with different portions of European society. It is evident that the example of economic freedom elsewhere may in the future transform, and will certainly limit, such sections of European life as are drifting towards the reestablishment of slavery.

===Chinese===

当伴随市政和国家规制的市政与国家事业是建立在借贷而非没收的基础之上——抑或更甚者，是建立在旨在*规避*没收的借贷基础之上时，它便是对社会主义的否定；而且我已经证明，试图通过偿债基金及类似机制来掩盖此类运作之资本主义性质的企图，在逻辑上是毫无价值的。你不可能“买赎”资本主义。
我无须指出，即使在本书初版问世以来的极短时间里，向着本书所试图阐明的方向已经迈出了何等步伐。我们已经在一个庞大工业行业中设立了工资委员会（wages boards）；不久之后我们将在更多行业中设立它。我们已经通过姓名、住址、迁徙轨迹、患病时的病况、推定或真实的“装病（malingering）”、对某种恶习（如酗酒）的沉溺、家庭习惯、就业性质等等，对无产阶级完成了几乎巨细靡遗的登记造册，且*这是由较为富有的阶级所强制推行的，而他们正是作为这项登记基础的人头税（Poll Tax）的实际征收者*。我们通过职业介绍所（labour exchanges）建立了一套不久之后将同样完备的体系：无产阶级的每一名成员最终都将以同样方式被登记为劳工，其反抗资本的倾向受到掌握并被记录下频次，以及他愿意为资本主义效力的程度、是否曾拒绝服役、若曾拒绝又在何处何因。
读者将饶有兴趣地在紧接我们面前的岁月的偶发事件与反动浪潮中，观察这套体系的缓慢完善：对无产阶级的登记与控制，连同其走向强制劳役之终局的必然且致命的趋近。但我认为，为了公正对待拙著，我应当向同一位读者指出本书末尾各页的深意。在欧洲社会中，任何变革除非普及全欧，否则便无法达致完满。资本主义并非普遍如此；它在欧洲不同地区的发展程度迥异；因此，奴役状态的降临在欧洲社会不同部分也是程度不一的可能性。显然，别处的经济自由典范在未来可能会改变、且必定会限制欧洲生活中那些趋向重建奴隶制的部分。

===Original===

But the tendency to the reestablishment of slavery as a necessary development of capitalism is patent wherever capitalism has power, and nowhere more than in this country.
H. Belloc.
Kings Land, Shipley,
Horsham, Sussex.

===Chinese===

然而，奴隶制的重建作为资本主义之必然发展的趋势，在资本主义握有权力的任何地方都是显而易见的，而在本国尤甚于任何地方。
H. 贝洛克（H. Belloc）
萨塞克斯郡，霍舍姆，希普利，国王领地（Kings Land, Shipley, Horsham, Sussex）

## VII Socialism Is the Easiest Apparent Solution of the Capitalist Crux / 第七章 社会主义是资本主义困境最显而易见的表面出路
===Original===

A contrast between the reformer making for distribution and the reformer making for socialism (or collectivism)﻿—The difficulties met by the first type﻿—He is working against the grain﻿—The second is working with the grain﻿—Collectivism a natural development of capitalism﻿—It appeals both to capitalist and proletarian﻿—None the less we shall see that the collectivist attempt is doomed to fail and to produce a thing very different from its object﻿—To wit, the servile state.
I say that the line of least resistance, if it be followed, leads a capitalist state to transform itself into a servile state.
I propose to show that this comes about from the fact that not a *distributive* but a *collectivist* solution is the easiest for a capitalist state to aim at, and that yet, in the very act of attempting *collectivism*, what results is not collectivism at all, but the servitude of the many, and the confirmation in their present privilege of the few; that is, the servile state.
---
Men to whom the institution of slavery is abhorrent propose for the remedy of capitalism one of two reforms.
Either they would put property into the hands of most citizens, so dividing land and capital that a determining number of families in the state were possessed of the means of production; or they would put those means of production into the hands of the political officers of the community, to be held in trust for the advantage of all.
The first solution may be called the attempted establishment of the **distributive state**. The second may be called the attempted establishment of the **collectivist state**.
Those who favour the first course are the conservatives or traditionalists. They are men who respect and would, if possible, preserve the old forms of Christian European life. They know that property was thus distributed throughout the state during the happiest periods of our past history; they also know that where it is properly distributed today, you have greater social sanity and ease than elsewhere. In general, those who would reestablish, if possible, the distributive state in the place of, and as a remedy for, the vices and unrest of capitalism, are men concerned with known realities, and having for their goal a condition of society which experience has tested and proved both stable and good.

===Chinese===

趋向分散所有制的改革者与趋向社会主义（或集体主义）的改革者之间的对照——第一类人所遭遇的困难——他是在逆势而动——第二类人则是在顺水推舟——集体主义乃是资本主义的自然发展——它对资本家和无产者皆有吸引力——然而我们仍将看到，集体主义的企图注定要归于失败，并产生出一种与其目标大相径庭的事物——即奴役国家。
我断言：顺应阻力最小的途径（若予依循），必将引领资本主义国家将其自身转变为奴役国家（the Servile State）。
我打算证明，这是源于这样一个事实：对资本主义国家而言，最容易追求的目标并非*分散所有制*的解决方案，而是*集体主义*的解决方案；然而，正是在试图推行*集体主义*的行为本身之中，其最终结果根本不是集体主义，而是大众沦为奴役、少数人巩固其现存特权；亦即奴役国家。
---
对奴隶制制度深恶痛绝的人们，针对资本主义的救治提出了两种改革方案之一。
他们要么将财产置于大多数公民手中，通过对土地和资本的分散，使国家中具有决定性数量的家庭拥有生产资料；要么将那些生产资料置于共同体的政治官员手中，由其为了所有人的利益代为托管。
第一种解决方案可以称为建立**分散所有制国家（distributive state）**的尝试。第二种则可称为建立**集体主义国家（collectivist state）**的尝试。
赞同第一种途径的人是保守派或传统主义者。他们尊重并渴望在可能的情况下保全欧洲基督教生活的古老形态。他们深知，在我们过去历史上最幸福的时期，财产在整个国家中正是以这种方式分布的；他们也深知，在今日财产分布得当之处，你所见到的社会健全与从容安定远甚于别处。一般而言，那些渴望在可能的情况下重建分散所有制国家以取代资本主义之弊害与骚乱并作为其救治之道的人，皆是关注已知现实的人，并将经验所检验和证明为既稳定又美好的社会形态作为其追求目标。

===Original===

They are then, of the two schools of reformers, the more *practical* in the sense that they deal more than do the collectivists (called also socialists) with things which either are or have been in actual existence. But they are less practical in another sense (as we shall see in a moment) from the fact that the stage of the disease with which they are dealing does not readily lend itself to such a reaction as they propose.
The collectivist, on the other hand, proposes to put land and capital into the hands of the political officers of the community, and this on the understanding that they shall hold such land and capital in trust for the advantage of the community. In making this proposal he is evidently dealing with a state of things hitherto imaginary, and his ideal is not one that has been tested by experience, nor one of which our race and history can furnish instances. In this sense, therefore, he is the *less* practical of the two reformers. His ideal cannot be discovered in any past, known, and recorded phase of our society. We cannot examine socialism in actual working, nor can we say (as we can say of well-divided property): “On such and such an occasion, in such and such a period of European history, collectivism was established and produced both stability and happiness in society.”
In this sense, therefore, the collectivist is far less practical than the reformer who desires well-distributed property.
On the other hand, there is a sense in which this socialist is more practical than that other type of reformer, from the fact that the stage of the disease into which we have fallen apparently admits of his remedy with less shock than it admits of a reaction towards well-divided property.
For example: the operation of buying out some great tract of private ownership today (as a railway or a harbour company) with public funds, continuing its administration by publicly paid officials and converting its revenue to public use, is a thing with which we are familiar and which seemingly might be indefinitely multiplied. Individual examples of such transformation of waterworks, gas, tramways, from a capitalist to a collectivist basis are common, and the change does not disturb any fundamental thing in our society.

===Chinese===

于是，在这两派改革者之中，他们在这种意义上更为*务实*：与集体主义者（亦称社会主义者）相比，他们更多地打交道于实际存在或曾经存在过的事物。然而在另一种意义上，他们却不够务实（正如我们片刻之后将看到的那样），因为他们所处理的疾病阶段并不容易接受他们所提出的这种逆转反应。
另一方面，集体主义者提议将土地和资本置于共同体的政治官员手中，其前提是这些官员应当为了共同体的利益托管此类土地和资本。在提出这一建议时，他显然是在打交道于一种迄今纯属想象的事态，他的理想并非某种经受过经验检验的理想，亦非我们的民族与历史能够提供先例的理想。因此，在这种意义上，他是两位改革者中*较不*务实的一位。他的理想无法在我们社会的任何过去、已知和有记录的阶段中被发现。我们无法检验实际运转中的社会主义，我们也不能（像我们谈论分散良好的财产制时那样）断言：“在欧洲历史上的某时某刻、某一时期，集体主义曾经确立，并为社会带来了稳定与幸福。”
因此，在这种意义上，集体主义者远不如渴望财产良好分散的改革者务实。
另一方面，在某种意义上，这位社会主义者却比另一类改革者更为务实，因为我们陷入其中的疾病阶段，在接受他的救治方案时表面上受到的震荡，要少于接受向分散良好财产制倒退所产生的震荡。
例如：今日利用公共资金赎买某一大片私有产业（如铁路或港口公司），继续由公帑雇用的官员进行管理并将其收益转归公用，这是一件我们完全熟悉且看似可以无限复制的事情。将自来水厂、燃气公司、有轨电车从资本主义基础转变为集体主义基础的个案比比皆是，这种变革丝毫不触动我们社会中的任何根本事物。

===Original===

When a private water company or tramway line is bought by some town and worked thereafter in the interests of the public, the transaction is effected without any perceptible friction, disturbs the life of no private citizen, and seems in every way normal to the society in which it takes place.
Upon the contrary, the attempt to create a large number of shareholders in such enterprises and artificially to substitute many partners, distributed throughout a great number of the population, in the place of the original few capitalist owners, would prove lengthy and at every step would arouse opposition, would create disturbance, would work at an expense of great friction, and would be imperilled by the power of the new and many owners to sell again to a few.
In a word, the man who desires to reestablish property as an institution normal to most citizens in the state is *working against the grain* of our existing capitalist society, while a man who desires to establish socialism﻿—that is collectivism﻿—is working *with* the grain of that society. The first is like a physician who should say to a man whose limbs were partially atrophied from disuse: “Do this and that, take such and such exercise, and you will recover the use of your limbs.” The second is like a physician who should say: “You cannot go on as you are. Your limbs are atrophied from lack of use. Your attempt to conduct yourself as though they were not is useless and painful; you had better make up your mind to be wheeled about in a fashion consonant to your disease.” The physician is the reformer, his patient the proletariat.
It is not the purpose of this book to show how and under what difficulties a condition of well-divided property might be restored and might take the place (even in England) of that capitalism which is now no longer either stable or tolerable; but for the purposes of contrast and to emphasise my argument I will proceed, before showing how the collectivist unconsciously makes for the servile state, to show what difficulties surround the distributive solution and why, therefore, the collectivist solution appeals so much more readily to men living under capitalism.
---
If I desire to substitute a number of small owners for a few large ones in some particular enterprise, how shall I set to work?
I might boldly confiscate and redistribute at a blow.

===Chinese===

当某座市镇买下一家私营自来水公司或有轨电车线路，并在日后为了公众利益进行运营时，该交易是在没有任何可察觉的摩擦中完成的，不扰乱任何私人公民的生活，且在其发生的社会看来在各方面都显得完全合情合理。
相反，若试图在此类企业中培育大量的小股东，并在绝大多数人口中人为地建立起许多合伙人，以取代原先少数资本家所有者，这一过程将被证明是冗长漫长的，且每走一步都会激起反对、制造动荡、付出巨大摩擦的代价，并且随时面临新出现的众多小所有者再次将股权转卖给少数人的致命危险。
简言之，渴望将财产重建为国家大多数公民之常态制度的人，是在*逆着*我们现有资本主义社会的*纹理而动*；而渴望建立社会主义——亦即集体主义——的人，则是在*顺着*该社会的*纹理行事*。前者犹如一位医生对一个因缺乏使用而四肢部分萎缩的人说：“做这个做那个，进行这样那样的锻炼，你就会恢复四肢的功能。”后者则犹如一位医生说：“你不能按现在的样子硬撑下去了。你的四肢因缺乏使用已经萎缩。你试图表现得好像它们未曾萎缩一样的举动是徒劳且痛苦的；你最好下定决心，按照契合你病况的方式坐轮椅代步。”医生即改革者，他的病人则是无产阶级。
本书的目的并非阐明如何以及在何种困难之下才能恢复良好分散的财产状态，并（即便在英格兰）取代如今既不稳定又不可忍受的资本主义；然而为了形成对比并凸显我的论点，在说明集体主义者如何下意识地迈向奴役国家之前，我将先行阐明围绕着分散所有制解决方案的重重困难，以及生活在资本主义之下的民众为何因此而远更容易为集体主义解决方案所吸引。
---
若我渴望在某个特定企业中以众多小所有者取代少数大所有者，我该如何着手展开工作？
我或许可以断然采取没收手段，在一击之下完成重新分配。

===Original===

But by what process should I choose the new owners? Even supposing that there was some machinery whereby the justice of the new distribution could be assured, how could I avoid the enormous and innumerable separate acts of injustice that would attach to general redistributions? To say “none shall own” and to confiscate is one thing; to say “all should own” and apportion ownership is another. Action of this kind would so disturb the whole network of economic relations as to bring ruin at once to the whole body politic, and particularly to the smaller interests indirectly affected. In a society such as ours a catastrophe falling upon the state from outside might indirectly do good by making such a redistribution possible. But no one working from within the state could provoke that catastrophe without ruining his own cause.
If, then, I proceed more slowly and more rationally and canalise the economic life of society so that small property shall gradually be built up within it, see against what forces of inertia and custom I have to work today in a capitalist society!
If I desire to benefit small savings at the expense of large, I must reverse the whole economy under which interest is paid upon deposits today. It is far easier to save £100 out of a revenue of £1,000 than to save £10 out of a revenue of £100. It is infinitely easier to save £10 out of a revenue of £100 than £5 out of a revenue of £50. To build up small property through thrift when once the mass have fallen into the proletarian trough is impossible unless you deliberately subsidise small savings, offering them a reward which, in competition, they could never obtain; and to do this the whole vast arrangement of credit must be worked backwards. Or, let the policy be pursued of penalising undertakings with few owners, of heavily taxing large blocks of shares and of subsidising with the produce small holders in proportion to the smallness of their holding. Here again you are met with the difficulty of a vast majority who cannot even bid for the smallest share.
One might multiply instances of the sort indefinitely, but the strongest force against the distribution of ownership in a society already permeated with capitalist modes of thought is still the moral one: Will men want to own?

===Chinese===

然而，我应当依循何种程序来挑选新的所有者？即便假定存在某种能够确保新分配之公正性的机制，我又该如何避免普遍重新分配所必然伴随的巨大且不可胜数的个别不公正行径？宣称“任何人都不得拥有财产”并予以没收是一回事；宣称“人人都应当拥有财产”并按份额分摊所有权则是另一回事。这种性质的举动将剧烈扰乱整个经济关系网络，以致立即给整个政治体带来毁灭，尤其是间接受其波及的较小利益主体。在像我们这样的社会中，从外部降临于国家的灾难或许可以通过使这种重新分配成为可能而间接促成益处。但从国家内部开展工作的人，若挑起这场浩劫，便绝不可能不毁灭他自身的追求。
那么，若我采取更为缓慢、更为理性的步骤，疏导社会的经济生活，以便在其内部逐步建立起小财产制，请看在今日的资本主义社会中，我必须克服何等巨大的惯性与习俗阻力！
若我想牺牲大额储蓄来扶持小额储蓄，我就必须彻底逆转今日对存款支付利息的全部经济机制。从1,000英镑（£1,000）的收入中省出100英镑（£100），远比从100英镑（£100）的收入中省出10英镑（£10）容易得多。从100英镑（£100）的收入中省出10英镑（£10），又比从50英镑（£50）的收入中省出5英镑（£5）容易得无可比拟。一旦大众跌入了无产阶级的深渊，试图通过勤俭积累小财产便绝无可能，除非你蓄意补贴小额储蓄，向其提供在自由竞争中绝不可能获得的丰厚报酬；而要做到这一点，庞大的信贷体制必须全盘倒转操作。或者，采取对少数所有者企业实施惩罚的政策，对大宗股份征收重税，并按持有份额之微小成比例地补贴小持有者。在此，你又将遭遇绝大多数人甚至连哪怕最微小的份额都无力出资竞购的困境。
人们可以无限列举此类例证，但在早已浸透了资本主义思维方式的社会中，阻碍所有权分散的最强大力量依然是道德层面的阻力：人们究竟还渴望拥有财产吗？

===Original===

Will officials, administrators, and lawmakers be able to shake off the power which under capitalism seems normal to the rich? If I approach, for instance, the works of one of our great trusts, purchase it with public money, bestow, even as a gift, the shares thereof to its workmen, can I count upon any tradition of property in their midst which will prevent their squandering the new wealth? Can I discover any relics of the cooperative instinct among such men? Could I get managers and organisers to take a group of poor men seriously or to serve them as they would serve rich men? Is not the whole psychology of a capitalist society divided between the proletarian mass which thinks in terms not of property but of “employment,” and the few owners who are alone familiar with the machinery of administration?
I have touched but very briefly and superficially upon this matter, because it needs no elaboration. Though it is evident that with a sufficient will and a sufficient social vitality property could be restored, it is evident that all efforts to restore it have in a capitalist society such as our own a note of oddity, of doubtful experiment, of being uncoordinated with other social things around them, which marks the heavy handicap under which any such attempt must proceed. It is like recommending elasticity to the aged.
On the other hand, the collectivist experiment is thoroughly suited (in appearance at least) to the capitalist society which it proposes to replace. It works with the existing machinery of capitalism, talks and thinks in the existing terms of capitalism, appeals to just those appetites which capitalism has aroused, and ridicules as fantastic and unheard-of just those things in society the memory of which capitalism has killed among men wherever the blight of it has spread.
So true is all this that the stupider kind of collectivist will often talk of a “capitalist phase” of society as the necessary precedent to a “collectivist phase.” A trust or monopoly is welcomed because it “furnishes a mode of transition from private to public ownership.” Collectivism promises employment to the great mass who think of production only in terms of employment. It promises to its workmen the security which a great and well-organised industrial capitalist unit (like one of our railways) can give through a system of pensions, regular promotion, etc.

===Chinese===

官员、行政管理者和立法者能否摆脱在资本主义下富人看似习以为常的支配权力？例如，若我走向我们某家大型托拉斯的工场，用公帑将其买下，甚至作为赠礼将其股份分给工人们，我能否指望在他们中间存在任何能够防止其挥霍这笔新财富的财产传统？我能否在这些人中间发现任何合作本能的残存痕迹？我能否让经理人和组织者严肃对待一群穷人，或者像为富人效劳那样为他们服务？资本主义社会的全部心理，难道不是分裂在不以财产而仅以“雇佣”为考量的无产阶级大众、与唯有其自身才熟悉行政运作机制的少数所有者之间吗？
我对这一问题仅做了极简略和浮于表面的触及，因为它无须详加赘述。尽管显而易见的是，若具备充分的意志与充沛的社会活力，财产制度是可以恢复的，但同样显而易见的是：在像我们这样的资本主义社会中，恢复财产的一切努力都带着一种古怪离奇、前途未卜的试验意味，带着与周遭其他社会事物格格不入的脱节感，这构成了任何此类尝试在推行中所背负的沉重掣肘。这犹如建议耄耋老者去追求身轻如燕。
另一方面，集体主义试验与它提议取代的资本主义社会则彻底契合（至少在表面上如此）。它利用资本主义的现有机制运作，以资本主义的现有话语进行言说和思考，迎合的正是资本主义所激起的那些欲望；而且，凡是资本主义的毒害所蔓延之处，它便对资本主义早已在人们心中扼杀其记忆的那些社会事物，冷嘲热讽为异想天开与闻所未闻。
这一切是如此真确，以至于较为愚钝的那类集体主义者经常将社会的“资本主义阶段”称作“集体主义阶段”的必然先决条件。托拉斯或垄断组织受到欢迎，乃是因为它“提供了一种从私人所有制向公共所有制过渡的形式”。集体主义向仅从雇佣角度看待生产的广大群众许诺了就业。它向其工人们许诺了一座庞大且组织优良的工业资本主义单位（如我们的某家铁路公司）通过退休金制度、定期晋升等所能提供的那种安全保障。

===Original===

, but that security vastly increased through the fact that it is the state and not a mere unit of the state which guarantees it. Collectivism would administer, would pay wages, would promote, would pension off, would fine﻿—and all the rest of it﻿—exactly as the capitalist state does today. The proletarian, when the collectivist (or socialist) state is put before him, perceives nothing in the picture save certain ameliorations of his present position. Who can imagine that if, say, two of our great industries, coal and railways, were handed over to the state tomorrow, the armies of men organised therein would find any change in the character of their lives, save in some increase of security and possibly in a very slight increase of earnings?
The whole scheme of collectivism presents, so far as the proletarian mass of a capitalist state is concerned, nothing unknown at all, but a promise of some increment in wages and a certainty of far greater ease of mind.
To that small minority of a capitalist society which owns the means of production, collectivism will of course appear as an enemy, but, even so, it is an enemy which they understand and an enemy with whom they can treat in terms common both to that enemy and to themselves. If, for instance, the state proposes to take over such and such a trust now paying 4 percent, and believes that under state management it will make the trust pay 5 percent, then the transference takes the form of a business proposition: the state is no harder to the capitalists taken over than was Mr. Yerkes to the Underground. Again, the state, having greater credit and longevity, can (it would seem)5 “buy out” any existing capitalist body upon favourable terms. Again, the discipline by which the state would enforce its rules upon the proletariat it employed would be the same rules as those by which the capitalist imposes discipline in his own interests today.
There is in the whole scheme which proposes to transform the capitalist into the collectivist state no element of reaction, the use of no term with which a capitalist society is not familiar, the appeal to no instinct, whether of cowardice, greed, apathy, or mechanical regulation, with which a capitalist community is not amply familiar.
In general, if modern capitalist England were made by magic a state of small owners, we should all suffer an enormous revolution.

===Chinese===

在未来达致该国家形态之时，它许诺所有利润与全部总收入将自然发放给整个共同体，而不再扣除任何资本家的利润。然而，那个整个共同体届时将会发现：这台机器已经在全速运转，且他们不得不去维系饲喂它。简言之，在资本主义国家的大众所涉及的范围内，集体主义对无产阶级而言根本不是任何未知的事物，而是某种工资增长的许诺，以及某种内心安定得多的确定性。
对于资本主义社会中拥有生产资料的极少数人而言，集体主义固然在表面上看似敌人，但即便如此，它也是一个他们完全能够理解的敌人，一个他们可以用彼此通用的商业术语与之谈判的敌人。例如，若国家提议接管目前派息4%的某个托拉斯，并相信在国家管理下可以使其达到5%的收益，那么这种产权转移便采取了一项商业交易的形式：国家对被接管资本家的苛刻程度，并不亚于约克斯先生（Mr. Yerkes）对待伦敦地下铁道公司（the Underground）。再者，国家拥有更强大的信用和更漫长的寿命，因此（看似）5能够以极为优厚的条件“买赎”任何现存的资本家团体。此外，国家用来对其所雇用的无产阶级推行其规章的纪律，与资本家今日为了自身利益所施加的纪律如出一辙。
在这项试图将资本主义国家转变为集体主义国家的整套方案中，不存在任何反动的要素，不使用任何资本主义社会所不熟悉的词汇，不诉诸任何资本主义共同体未曾广泛熟悉的本能——不论是怯懦、贪婪、冷漠，还是机械的规制。
一般而言，如果现代资本主义的英格兰被施了魔法一般转变为一个小所有者的国家，我们所有人都将经历一场翻天覆地的剧烈革命。

===Original===

We should marvel at the insolence of the poor, at the laziness of the contented, at the strange diversities of task, at the rebellious, vigorous personalities discernible upon every side. But if this modern capitalist England could, by a process sufficiently slow to allow for the readjustment of individual interests, be transformed into a collectivist state, the apparent change at the end of that transition would not be conspicuous to the most of us, and the transition itself should have met with no shocks that theory can discover. The insecure and hopeless margin below the regularly paid ranks of labour would have disappeared into isolated workplaces of a penal kind: we should hardly miss them. Many incomes now involving considerable duties to the state would have been replaced by incomes as large or larger, involving much the same duties and bearing only the newer name of salaries. The small shop-keeping class would find itself in part absorbed under public schemes at a salary, in part engaged in the old work of distribution at secure incomes; and such small owners as were left, of boats, of farms, even of machinery, would perhaps know the new state of things into which they had survived through nothing more novel than some increase in the irritating system of inspection and of onerous petty taxation: they are already fairly used to both.
This picture of the natural transition from capitalism to collectivism seems so obvious that many collectivists in a generation immediately past believed that nothing stood between them and the realisation of their ideal save the unintelligence of mankind. They had only to argue and expound patiently and systematically for the great transformation to become possible. They had only to continue arguing and expounding for it at last to be realised.
I say, “of the last generation.” Today that simple and superficial judgment is getting woefully disturbed. The most sincere and single-minded of collectivists cannot but note that the practical effect of their propaganda is not an approach towards the collectivist state at all, but towards something very different. It is becoming more and more evident that with every new reform﻿—and those reforms commonly promoted by particular socialists, and in a puzzled way blessed by socialists in general﻿—another state emerges more and more clearly.

===Chinese===

我们将会惊骇于穷人的傲慢无礼，惊骇于心满意足者的慵懒闲散，惊骇于各项劳作的千差万别，惊骇于四面八方皆可察觉的桀骜不驯且生机勃勃的个性。然而，如果现代资本主义的英格兰能够通过一个足够缓慢以容纳个体利益重新调整的进程转变为集体主义国家，那么在这一过渡终点时表面的变化对我们大多数人而言绝不会显眼刺目，而且这一过渡本身在理论所能发现的范围内绝不会遭遇任何震荡。在领受稳定工资的劳动队伍之下那部分朝不保夕且绝望的边缘人群，将消失在某种带有惩戒性质的孤立工场之中：我们几乎不会想念他们。如今承担着对国家重大职责的许多收入，将被同样丰厚乃至更为丰厚的收入所取代，承担着大致相同的职责，仅仅换上了一个更新的名称——薪俸。小店主阶层将发现自己一部分以领薪者的身份被吸纳进公共计划之中，另一部分则以有保障的收入从事以往的分配劳作；而那些幸存下来的小业主——小船只、小农场乃至小机器的所有者——或许只会通过某种日益令人恼火的巡查制度和沉重繁琐的琐碎税赋的增加，才能觉察到自己幸存其中的新事态：而他们对这两者早就相当习惯了。
这幅从资本主义向集体主义自然过渡的图景显得如此不言而喻，以至于紧接上一代人的许多集体主义者深信，除了人类的缺乏智识之外，没有任何东西阻碍在他们与其理想的实现之间。他们只需耐心且系统地论证和阐释，这场伟大的转变就能成为可能。他们只需继续论证和阐释，它最终便能付诸实现。
我说的是“上一代人”。今天，这种简单而肤浅的判断正在令人扼腕地遭受动摇。最真诚、最专一的集体主义者不能不注意到：他们的宣传在实践中所产生的效果，根本不是走向集体主义国家，而是走向某种截然不同的事物。日益明显的是，随着每一项新改革的推行——且这些改革通常是由具体的社会主义者所发起，并以某种令人困惑的方式得到广义社会主义者的首肯——另一种国家形态正越来越清晰地浮出水面。

===Original===

It is becoming increasingly certain that the attempted transformation of capitalism into collectivism is resulting not in collectivism at all, but in some third thing which the collectivist never dreamt of, or the capitalist either; and that third thing is the **servile state**: a state, that is, in which the mass of men shall be constrained *by law* to labour to the profit of a minority, but, as the price of such constraint, shall enjoy a security which the old capitalism did not give them.
Why is the apparently simple and direct action of collectivist reform diverted into so unexpected a channel? And in what new laws and institutions does modern England in particular and industrial society in general show that this new form of the state is upon us?
To these two questions I will attempt an answer in the two concluding divisions of this book.

===Chinese===

日益确凿无疑的是：将资本主义转变为集体主义的尝试，其结果根本不是集体主义，而是某种集体主义者从未梦想过、资本家也未曾梦想过的第三种事物；而那第三种事物便是**奴役国家（servile state）**：换言之，即这样一个国家：大众将被*法律强制*为少数人的利益而劳动，但作为这种强制的代价，他们将享有旧资本主义未曾给予他们的安全保障。
为什么集体主义改革看似简单而直接的行动会被引向如此始料未及的歧途？现代英格兰（以其具体表现）以及一般的工业社会，究竟是在何种新法律和新制度中表明这种全新的国家形态已然降临到了我们头上？
对于这两个问题，我将在本书最后两个篇章中尝试给出解答。

## V The Capitalist State in Proportion as It Grows Perfect Grows Unstable / 第五章 资本主义国家愈完善便愈不稳定
===Original===

It can of its nature be but a transitory phase lying between an earlier and a later stable state of society﻿—The two internal strains which render it unstable﻿—(a) The conflict between its social realities and its moral and legal basis﻿—(b) The insecurity and insufficiency to which it condemns free citizens﻿—The few possessors can grant or withhold livelihood from the many non-possessors﻿—Capitalism is so unstable that it dares not proceed to its own logical conclusion, but tends to restrict competition among owners, and insecurity and insufficiency among non-owners.
From the historical digression which I have introduced by way of illustrating my subject in the last two sections I now return to the general discussion of my thesis and to the logical process by which it may be established.
---
The capitalist state is unstable, and indeed more properly a transitory phase lying between two permanent and stable states of society.
In order to appreciate why this is so, let us recall the definition of the capitalist state:﻿—

> A society in which the ownership of the means of production is confined to a body of free citizens, not large enough to make up properly a general character of that society, while the rest are dispossessed of the means of production, and are therefore proletarian, we call “capitalist.”

===Chinese===

依其本质，它只能是处于较早与较晚两种稳定社会形态之间的过渡阶段——导致其不稳定的两种内在张力——(一) 其社会现实与其道德和法律基础之间的冲突——(二) 它将自由公民判处其中的朝不保夕与匮乏不均——少数占有者可以向多数未占有者赋予或拒予生计——资本主义是如此不稳定，以致它不敢走向其自身的逻辑终局，而是趋于限制所有者之间的竞争，并限制非所有者之中的朝不保夕与匮乏不均。
从我在前两章中为了阐释主题而引入的历史题外话，我现在回到对拙著论点的普遍探讨，以及用以确立该论点的逻辑进程。
---
资本主义国家是不稳定的，且更确切地说，它乃是处于两种持久而稳定的社会形态之间的一个过渡阶段。
为了领会何以如此，让我们重温资本主义国家的定义：——
> 生产资料的所有权局限于一部分自由公民、其人数之少不足以恰当地构成该社会的普遍特征，而其余的人则被剥夺了生产资料、因而是无产的社会，我们称之为“资本主义社会”。

===Original===

Note the several points of such a state of affairs. You have private ownership; but it is not private ownership distributed in many hands and thus familiar as an institution to society as a whole. Again, you have the great majority dispossessed but at the same time citizens, that is, men politically free to act, though economically impotent; again, though it is but an inference from our definition, it is a necessary inference that there will be under capitalism a conscious, direct, and planned *exploitation* of the majority (the free citizens who do not own) by the minority who are owners. For wealth must be produced: the whole of that community must live: and the possessors can make such terms with the non-possessors as shall make it certain that a portion of what the non-possessors have produced shall go to the possessors.
A society thus constituted cannot endure. It cannot endure because it is subject to two very severe strains: strains which increase in severity in proportion as that society becomes more thoroughly capitalist. The first of these strains arises from the divergence between the moral theories upon which the state reposes and the social facts which those moral theories attempt to govern. The second strain arises from the insecurity to which capitalism condemns the great mass of society, and the general character of anxiety and peril which it imposes upon all citizens, but in particular upon the majority, which consists, under capitalism, of dispossessed free men.
Of these two strains it is impossible to say which is the gravest. Either would be enough to destroy a social arrangement in which it was long present. The two combined make that destruction certain; and there is no longer any doubt that capitalist society must transform itself into some other and more stable arrangement. It is the object of these pages to discover what that stable arrangement will probably be.
---
We say that there is a moral strain already intolerably severe and growing more severe with every perfection of capitalism.
This moral strain comes from a contradiction between the realities of capitalist society and the moral base of our laws and traditions.
The moral base upon which our laws are still administered and our conventions raised presupposes a state composed of free citizens.

===Chinese===

请注意这种事态的若干要点。你拥有私有制；但它并非分散在众多人手中的私有制，从而并非对整个社会而言皆为熟知的制度。此外，绝大多数人被剥夺了财产，但同时他们又是公民，亦即在政治上拥有行动自由、但在经济上却无能为力的人；再者，尽管这只是从我们定义中所做出的推论，但这是一个必然的推论：在资本主义下，少数拥有财产的所有者必然会对多数人（不拥有财产的自由公民）实施自觉、直接且有计划的*剥削（exploitation）*。因为财富必须生产，那个共同体的全体成员必须生存；而占有者可以与非占有者订立条款，确保非占有者所生产的一部分财富归占有者所有。
如此构成的社会绝不可能长久维系。它之所以不能长久维系，是因为它承受着两种极其严峻的张力：随着该社会变得愈发彻底地资本主义化，这两种张力的严峻程度便成比例地与日俱增。第一种张力源于国家赖以立足的道德理论与那些道德理论试图管辖的社会事实之间的分歧。第二种张力源于资本主义将社会广大群众判处其中的朝不保夕的不安定状态，以及它强加于所有公民（尤其是由被剥夺财产的自由人所构成的多数人）身上的普遍焦虑与凶险特征。
在这两种张力之中，无法判定孰轻孰重。任何一种张力若长期存在，皆足以摧毁一种社会安排。两者结合起来，便使这种毁灭成为必然；毫无疑问，资本主义社会必然会将其自身转变为某种其他且更为稳定的社会形态。这几页篇幅的目的，便是探寻那种稳定的社会形态大概会是什么。
---
我们断言：已经存在着一种难以忍受且严峻的道德张力，且资本主义每完善一步，这种张力便愈发严峻。
这种道德张力来自资本主义社会的现实与我们法律和传统的道德基石之间的矛盾。
我们的法律在执行时以及我们的习俗在建立时所依凭的道德基石，预设了一个由自由公民构成的国家。

===Original===

Our law defends property as a normal institution with which all citizens are acquainted, and which all citizens respect. It punishes theft as an abnormal incident only occurring when, through evil motives, one free citizen acquires the property of another without his knowledge and against his will. It punishes fraud as another abnormal incident in which, from evil motives, one free citizen induces another to part with his property upon false representations. It enforces contract, the sole moral base of which is the freedom of the two contracting parties, and the power of either, if it so please him, not to enter into a contract which, once entered into, must be enforced. It gives to an owner the power to leave his property by will, under the conception that such ownership and such passage of property (to natural heirs as a rule, but exceptionally to any other whom the testator may point out) is the normal operation of a society generally familiar with such things, and finding them part of the domestic life lived by the mass of its citizens. It casts one citizen in damages if by any wilful action he has caused loss to another﻿—for it presupposes him able to pay.
The sanction upon which social life reposes is, in our moral theory, the legal punishment enforceable in our courts, and the basis presupposed for the security and material happiness of our citizens is the possession of goods which shall guarantee us from anxiety and permit us an independence of action in the midst of our fellowmen.
Now contrast all this, the moral theory upon which society is still perilously conducted, the moral theory to which capitalism itself turns for succour when it is attacked, contrast, I say, its formulae and its presuppositions with the social reality of a capitalist state such as is England today.
Property remains as an instinct perhaps with most of the citizens; as an experience and a reality it is unknown to nineteen out of twenty. One hundred forms of fraud, the necessary corollary of unrestrained competition between a few and of unrestrained avarice as the motive controlling production, are not or cannot be punished: petty forms of violence in theft and of cunning in fraud the laws can deal with, but they cannot deal with these alone.

===Chinese===

我们的法律将财产捍卫为一种所有公民皆熟知且所有公民皆尊重的常态制度。它惩处盗窃，将其视为一种异常事件，仅当出于邪恶意图、一个自由公民在另一个自由公民不知情且违背其意愿的情况下获取其财产时才会发生。它惩处欺诈，将其视为另一种异常事件，在其中出于邪恶意图，一个自由公民通过虚假陈述诱使另一个自由公民放弃其财产。它强制执行契约，而契约唯一的道德基石便是订约各方的自由，以及他们拒绝或提供契约规定之物的能力。它维系家庭，但家庭只有扎根于财产之中，才能得到充分且健康的发展。简言之，社会赖以立足的一切法律与道德概念，皆是对一种并不存在的事态的概念。

===Original===

Our legal machinery has become little more than an engine for protecting the few owners against the necessities, the demands, or the hatred of the mass of their dispossessed fellow-citizens.
