The vast bulk of so-called “free” contracts are today leonine contracts: arrangements which one man was free to take or to leave, but which the other man was not free to take or to leave, because the second had for his alternative starvation.
Most important of all, the fundamental social fact of our movement, far more important than any security afforded by law, or than any machinery which the state can put into action, is the fact that *livelihood* is at the will of the possessors. It can be granted by the possessors to the non-possessors, or it can be withheld. The real sanction in our society for the arrangements by which it is conducted is not punishment enforceable by the courts, but the withholding of livelihood from the dispossessed by the possessors. Most men now fear the loss of employment more than they fear legal punishment, and the discipline under which men are coerced in their modern forms of activity in England is the fear of dismissal. The true master of the Englishman today is not the Sovereign nor the officers of state, nor, save indirectly, the laws; his true master is the capitalist.
Of these main truths everyone is aware; and anyone who sets out to deny them does so today at the peril of his reputation either for honesty or for intelligence.
If it be asked why things have come to a head so late (capitalism having been in growth for so long), the answer is that England, even now the most completely capitalist state of the modern world, did not itself become a completely capitalist state until the present generation. Within the memory of men now living half England was agricultural, with relations domestic rather than competitive between the various human factors to production.
This moral strain, therefore, arising from the divergence between what our laws and moral phrases pretend, and what our society actually is, makes of that society an utterly unstable thing.
This spiritual thesis is of far greater gravity than the narrow materialism of a generation now passing might imagine.

===Chinese===

我们的法律机器已蜕变为几乎纯粹是用来保护少数所有者免受其被剥夺财产的同胞公民大众的穷困急需、索求乃至憎恨的工具。绝大多数所谓的“自由”契约，在今日皆是不平等的霸王契约（leonine contracts）：在这些安排中，一方可以自由选择接受或拒绝，而另一方却没有选择接受或拒绝的自由，因为后者的另一抉择便是活活饿死。
在这一切之中最为重要的是：我们这场运动的基本社会事实——远比法律所提供的任何保障、或国家所能付诸实施的任何机制重要得多——便是*生计（livelihood）*完全取决于占有者的意愿。生计可由占有者赋予非占有者，亦可予以拒给。在我们社会中维系其运行各项安排的真正制裁手段，并非法庭所能强制执行的刑罚，而是占有者对被剥夺财产者扣留生计。大多数人如今对丧失就业的恐惧远甚于对法律制裁的恐惧；而在现代英格兰，人们在各项活动中所被迫服从的纪律，便是对遭到解雇的恐惧。今日英国人真正的统治者既非君主，亦非国家官员，且除间接意义外亦非法理；他真正的统治者乃是资本家。
对于这些主要真理，人人心知肚明；在今日，任何企图否认它们的人，都是在拿自己的诚实或智识声誉冒险。
若要问为何事态在如此晚近之时才达到顶点（资本主义既然已经成长了如此之久），答案在于：即使是作为现代世界中最彻底之资本主义国家的英格兰，其自身直至当今这一代人才完全成为一个彻底的资本主义国家。在如今健在的人们的记忆之中，半个英格兰曾经是农业社会，各项生产要素之间的人际关系带有家庭式色彩而非纯粹竞争性。
因此，源于我们法律与道德词藻所伪称之物与我们社会实际状况之间分歧的这种道德张力，使该社会变成了一个极其不稳定的事物。
这一精神论题的严峻程度，远非正在逝去的那一代人的狭隘唯物主义所能想象。

===Original===

Spiritual conflict is more fruitful of instability in the state than conflict of any other kind, and there is acute spiritual conflict, conflict in every man’s conscience and ill-ease throughout the commonwealth when the realities of society are divorced from the moral base of its institutions.
---
The second strain which we have noted in capitalism, its second element of instability, consists in the fact that capitalism destroys security.
Experience is enough to save us any delay upon this main point of our matter. But even without experience we could reason with absolute certitude from the very nature of capitalism that its chief effect would be the destruction of security in human life.
Combine these two elements: the ownership of the means of production by a very few; the political freedom of owners and non-owners alike. There follows immediately from that combination a competitive market wherein the labour of the non-owner fetches just what it is worth, not as full productive power, but as productive power which will leave a surplus to the capitalist. It fetches nothing when the labourer cannot work, more in proportion to the pace at which he is driven; less in middle age than in youth; less in old age than in middle age; nothing in sickness; nothing in despair.
A man in a position to accumulate (the normal result of human labour), a man founded upon property in sufficient amount and in established form is no more productive in his nonproductive moments than is a proletarian; but his life is balanced and regulated by his reception of rent and interest as well as wages. Surplus values come to him, and are the flywheel balancing the extremes of his life and carrying him over his bad times. With a proletarian it cannot be so. The aspect whence capital looks at a human being whose labour it proposes to purchase cuts right across that normal aspect of human life from which we all regard our own affections, duties, and character. A man thinks of himself, of his chances and of his security along the line of his own individual existence from birth to death. Capital purchasing his labour (and not the man himself) purchases but a cross-section of his life, his moments of activity. For the rest, he must fend for himself; but to fend for yourself when you have nothing is to starve.

===Chinese===

精神层面的冲突在国家中孕育不稳定的能量，远甚于任何其他种类的冲突；而当社会的现实与其实体制度的道德基石发生脱节时，每个人的良知中便会出现剧烈的精神冲突，整个国家体中便弥漫着不安。
---
我们在资本主义中所注意到的第二种张力，其第二项不稳定因素，在于资本主义摧毁了安全保障。
经验足以让我们在这一核心论点上免于任何耽搁。但即便没有经验，我们仅凭资本主义的本质本身，就能以绝对确凿的把握推断出：它的主要后果必然是摧毁人类生活中的安全感。
将这两项要素结合起来：极少数人占有生产资料；所有者与非所有者皆享有平等的政治自由。从这种结合中立即衍生出一个竞争性市场：非所有者的劳动在其中仅能换取其身价——并非作为充分的生产能力，而是作为能够为资本家留下剩余的生产能力。当劳工无力工作时，它一文不值；与其被驱使劳作的节奏成比例，节奏越快则换取越多；中年时少于青年时；老年时少于中年时；患病时一无所获；绝望时一文不值。
一个处于能够积累财富地位的人（人类劳动的正常结果），一个立足于足额且稳固形式之财产上的人，在其不从事生产的时刻，并不比一个无产者更具生产力；但他的生活却通过获取地租、利息以及工资而得到平衡与调节。剩余价值流向他，充当着平衡其人生波动的飞轮，带领他度过逆境。对于无产者而言则绝非如此。资本意图购买其劳动的那个视角，完全横切过我们所有人看待自身情感、职责与品格的正常人生视角。一个人沿着自身从生到死的个体生存轨迹来思考自己、思考自己的机遇与安全。而购买其劳动的资本（并非购买其人本身）所购买的仅仅是他生命的一个横截面，仅仅是他活跃劳作的瞬间。其余的一切，他必须自谋生路；然而当你一无所有时，自谋生路便是活活饿死。

===Original===

As a matter of fact, where a few possess the means of production perfectly free political conditions are impossible. A perfect capitalist state cannot exist, though we have come nearer to it in modern England than other and more fortunate nations had thought possible. In the perfect capitalist state there would be no food available for the non-owner save when he was actually engaged in production, and that absurdity would, by quickly ending all human lives save those of the owners, put a term to the arrangement. If you left men completely free under a capitalist system, there would be so heavy a mortality from starvation as would dry up the sources of labour in a very short time.
Imagine the dispossessed to be ideally perfect cowards, the possessors to consider nothing whatsoever except the buying of their labour in the cheapest market﻿—and the system would break down from the death of children and of out-o’-works and of women. You would not have a state in mere decline such as ours is. You would have a state manifestly and patently perishing.
As a fact, of course, capitalism cannot proceed to its own logical extreme. So long as the political freedom of all citizens is granted [the freedom of the few possessors of food to grant or withhold it, of the many non-possessors to strike any bargain at all, lest they lack it]: to exercise such freedom fully is to starve the very young, the old, the impotent, and the despairing to death. Capitalism must keep alive, by non capitalist methods, great masses of the population who would otherwise starve to death; and that is what capitalism was careful to do to an increasing extent as it got a stronger and a stronger grip upon the English people. Elizabeth’s Poor Law at the beginning of the business, the Poor Law of 1834, coming at a moment when nearly half England had passed into the grip of capitalism, are original and primitive instances: there are today a hundred others.
---
Though this cause of insecurity﻿—the fact that the possessors have no direct incentive to keep men alive﻿—is logically the most obvious, and always the most enduring under a capitalist system, there is another cause more poignant in its effect upon human life. That other cause is the competitive anarchy in production which restricted ownership coupled with freedom involves.

===Chinese===

事实上，在少数人占有生产资料的地方，完全自由的政治条件是不可能的。一个纯粹完备的资本主义国家是不可能存在的，尽管我们在现代英格兰比其他更幸运的民族所认为可能的更接近这种状态。在完备的资本主义国家中，非所有者除非实际从事生产，否则根本得不到任何口粮；而这种荒谬事态由于会迅速终结除所有者之外的所有人的生命，从而将为这一社会安排划上句号。如果在资本主义制度下听任人们完全自由，那么饥馑所导致的死亡率将极其惊人，以致在极短的时间内就会使劳动力源泉彻底枯竭。
设想被剥夺财产者是一群理想中完美顺从的懦夫，而占有者除了在最廉价的市场购买其劳动之外什么都不考虑——那么这套体制就会因为儿童、失业者与妇女的死亡而彻底崩溃。你所拥有的将不再是一个像我们这样仅仅处于衰落之中的国家，而是一个显而易见正在走向灭亡的国家。
当然，在事实层面上，资本主义无法走向其自身的逻辑极端。只要承认所有公民的政治自由［少数粮食占有者赋予或拒给口粮的自由，多数未占有者为了免于饥馑而达成任何交易的自由］：若充分行使这种自由，就等于将幼童、老人、伤残者与绝望者活活饿死。资本主义必须依靠非资本主义的方法，维持那些否则将被饿死的大批人口的生存；而这正是资本主义随着它对英国人民的控制日益加深，而日益谨慎推行之事。伊丽莎白时期的《济贫法》（Elizabeth’s Poor Law）始其端，1834年的《济贫法》（the Poor Law of 1834）则出台于近半个英格兰落入资本主义掌控的时刻，皆是原始而典型的例证；在今日还有上百种其他例证。
---
尽管这种不安全感的原因——即占有者没有维持人类生存的直接动机这一事实——在逻辑上最为明显，且在资本主义制度下始终最为持久，但在其对人类生活的影响方面，还存在另一个更为尖锐的原因。那另一个原因，便是有限所有权与自由相结合所必然伴随的生产领域的竞争性无政府状态。

===Original===

Consider what is involved by the very process of production where the implements and the soil are in the hands of a few whose motive for causing the proletariat to produce is not the use of the wealth created but the enjoyment by those possessors of surplus value or “profit.”
If full political freedom be allowed to any two such possessors of implements and stores, each will actively watch his market, attempt to undersell the other, tend to overproduce at the end of some season of extra demand for his article, thus glut the market only to suffer a period of depression afterwards﻿—and so forth. Again, the capitalist, free, individual director of production, will miscalculate; sometimes he will fail, and his works will be shut down. Again, a mass of isolated, imperfectly instructed competing units cannot but direct their clashing efforts at an enormous waste, and that waste will fluctuate. Most commissions, most advertisements, most parades, are examples of this waste. If this waste of effort could be made a constant, the parasitical employment it afforded would be a constant too. But of its nature it is a most inconstant thing, and the employment it affords is therefore necessarily precarious. The concrete translation of this is the insecurity of the commercial traveller, the advertising agent, the insurance agent, and every form of touting and cozening which competitive capitalism carries with it.
Now here again, as in the case of the insecurity produced by age and sickness, capitalism cannot be pursued to its logical conclusion, and it is the element of freedom which suffers. Competition is, as a fact, restricted to an increasing extent by an understanding between the competitors, accompanied, especially in this country, by the ruin of the smaller competitor through secret conspiracies entered into by the larger men, and supported by the secret political forces of the state.3 In a word, capitalism, proving almost as unstable to the owners as to the non-owners, is tending towards stability by losing its essential character of political freedom. No better proof of the instability of capitalism as a system could be desired.
Take any one of the numerous trusts which now control English industry, and have made of modern England the type, quoted throughout the Continent, of artificial monopolies.

===Chinese===

促使无产阶级从事生产的动机，不是所创造财富的效用，而是占有者对剩余价值或“利润”的享用。
若允许任意两个此类工具和给养的占有者享有充分的政治自由，每个人都会积极监视市场，企图压低价格战胜对方，在其产品出现额外需求旺季末期趋于过度生产，从而使市场过剩，结果只能在日后承受萧条期——等等。再者，作为生产之自由、个体指挥者的资本家会出现误判；有时他会破产，他的工厂将关闭。此外，大批彼此孤立、信息不完备的竞争单位，其互相冲突的努力必然造成巨大浪费，且这种浪费会上下波动。绝大多数佣金、绝大多数广告、绝大多数排场，都是这种浪费的例证。若这种努力的浪费能够成为常态，那么它所提供的寄生性就业也将成为常态。但依其本质，这是一件最无常的事物，因而它所提供的就业必然是不稳定的。对此的具象写照，便是商业差旅推销员、广告代理人、保险经纪人以及竞争性资本主义所伴随的形形色色的拉客兜售与哄骗欺瞒的朝不保夕。
此时此刻，正如在衰老与疾病所引发的不安全感的情形中一样，资本主义无法被贯彻到其逻辑终局，而受损的恰恰是自由要素。在事实上，竞争日益受到竞争者之间默契协议的限制；尤其是本国，伴随着通过较大资本家订立的秘密勾结并由国家秘密政治力量所支持而导致的较小竞争者的毁灭。3 简言之，事实证明资本主义对于所有者而言几乎与对非所有者一样不稳定，它正通过丧失其政治自由的本质特征来趋向稳定。要想证明资本主义作为一种制度的不稳定性，再没有比这更好的证据了。
以如今控制着英国工业、并使现代英格兰成为全欧洲大陆所引述的人为垄断之典型的众多托拉斯中的任何一家为例。

===Original===

If the full formula of capitalism were accepted by our courts and our executive statesmen, anyone could start a rival business, undersell those trusts and shatter the comparative security they afford to industry within their field. The reason that no one does this is that political freedom is not, as a fact, protected here by the courts in commercial affairs. A man attempting to compete with one of our great English trusts would find himself at once undersold. He might, by all the spirit of European law for centuries, indict those who would ruin him, citing them for a conspiracy in restraint of trade; of this conspiracy he would find the judge and the politicians most heartily in support.
But it must always be remembered that these conspiracies in restraint of trade which are the mark of modern England are in themselves a mark of the transition from the true capitalist phase to another.
Under the essential conditions of capitalism﻿—under a perfect political freedom﻿—such conspiracies would be punished by the courts for what they are: to wit, a contravention of the fundamental doctrine of political liberty. For this doctrine, while it gives any man the right to make any contract he chooses with any labourer and offer the produce at such prices as he sees fit, also involves the protection of that liberty by the punishment of any conspiracy that may have monopoly for its object. If such perfect freedom is no longer attempted, if monopolies are permitted and fostered, it is because the unnatural strain to which freedom, coupled with restricted ownership, gives rise, the insecurity of its mere competition, the anarchy of its productive methods have at last proved intolerable.
I have already delayed more than was necessary in this section upon the causes which render a capitalist state essentially unstable.
I might have treated the matter empirically, taking for granted the observation which all my readers must have made, that capitalism is as a fact doomed, and that the capitalist state has already passed into its first phase of transition.
We are clearly no longer possessed of that absolutely political freedom which true capitalism essentially demands.

===Chinese===

倘若资本主义的完全信条被我们的法庭与执政政治家所完全接受，任何人都可以创办一家竞争性企业，以低于那些托拉斯的价格出售产品，并粉碎托拉斯在其领域内为工业所提供的相对安全感。没有人这样做的原因在于：在商业事务中，政治自由在事实上于此处并未受到法庭的保护。一个企图与我们英格兰大型托拉斯之一竞争的人，会发现自己立即被以倾销低价打垮。依凭数百年来欧洲法律的全部精神，他本可以起诉那些企图毁灭他的人，传讯他们犯有同谋限制贸易罪（conspiracy in restraint of trade）；然而对于这种同谋，他会发现法官与政客们最衷心地予以支持。
但必须永远牢记：这些作为现代英格兰印记的限制贸易的同谋，其本身便标志着从真正的资本主义阶段向另一阶段的过渡。
在资本主义的根本条件下——在完全的政治自由下——此类同谋将因其本来面目而受到法庭的惩处：换言之，即违反政治自由的根本学说。因为这一学说在赋予任何人以权利、使其可以随意与任何劳工订立任何契约并以其认为合适的任何价格出售产品的同时，也包含着通过惩处任何以垄断为目的的同谋来保护这一自由。若不再企图推行这种完全的自由，若垄断受到许可并受到扶植，乃是因为自由与有限所有权相结合所激起的不自然张力、其纯粹竞争的不安全感、其生产方式的无政府状态，最终被证明是难以忍受的。
在本章中，我在导致资本主义国家本质上不稳定的原因上耽搁的时间已经超过了必要。
我本可以经验主义地处理该问题，将所有读者必定皆已观察到的现象视为理所当然：即资本主义在事实上已注定灭亡，且资本主义国家已跨入了其过渡的第一阶段。
我们显然不再拥有真正的资本主义本质上所要求的那种绝对政治自由。

===Original===

The insecurity involved, coupled with the divorce between our traditional morals and the facts of society, have already introduced such novel features as the permission of conspiracy among both possessors and non-possessors, the compulsory provision of security through state action, and all these reforms, implicit or explicit, the tendency of which I am about to examine.

===Chinese===

所伴随的不安全感，加之我们的传统道德与社会现实之间的脱节，已经引入了诸如准许占有者与非占有者双方彼此勾结、通过国家行动强制提供安全保障等新颖特征，以及所有这些内隐或外显的改革——我即将对其趋势展开考察。

## VIII The Reformers and Reformed Are Alike Making for the Servile State / 第八章 改革者与被改革者都在共同趋向奴役国家
===Original===

There are two types of reformers working along the line of least resistance﻿—These are the socialist and the practical man﻿—The socialist again is of two kinds, the humanist and the statistician﻿—The humanist would like both to confiscate from the owners and to establish security and sufficiency for the non-owners﻿—He is allowed to do the second thing by establishing servile conditions﻿—He is forbidden to do the first﻿—The statistician is quite content so long as he can run and organise the poor﻿—Both are canalised towards the servile state and both are shepherded off their ideal collectivist state﻿—Meanwhile the great mass, the proletariat, upon whom the reformers are at work, though retaining the instinct of ownership, has lost any experience of it and is subject to private law much more than to the law of the courts﻿—This is exactly what happened in the past during the converse change from slavery to freedom﻿—Private law became stronger than public at the beginning of the Dark Ages﻿—The owners welcomed the changes which maintained them in ownership and yet increased the security of their revenue﻿—Today the non-owners will welcome whatever keeps them a wage-earning class but increases their wages and their security without insisting on the expropriation of the owners.
I propose in this section to show how the three interests which between them account for nearly the whole of the forces making for social change in modern England are all necessarily drifting towards the servile state.
Of these three interests the first two represent the reformers﻿—the third the people to be reformed.
These three interests are, first, the “socialist,” who is the theoretical reformer working along the line of least resistance; secondly, the “practical man” who as a “practical” reformer depends on his shortness of sight, and is therefore today a powerful factor; while the third is that great proletarian mass for whom the change is being effected, and on whom it is being imposed. What *they* are most likely to accept, the way in which *they* will react upon new institutions is the most important factor of all, for they are the material with and upon which the work is being done.

===Chinese===

沿着阻力最小路线开展工作的改革者有两类——即社会主义者与务实派——社会主义者又分为两类：人道主义者与统计学家——人道主义者既想没收所有者的财产，又想为非所有者确立安全与温饱——他被允许通过确立奴役条件来实现后一目的——他被禁止去实现前一目的——统计学家只要能管辖和组织穷人就心满意足——两者都被引向奴役国家，两者都被迫远离他们理想的集体主义国家——与此同时，改革者在其身上开展工作的大众，即无产阶级，尽管仍保留着所有权的本能，却已丧失了对所有权的任何经验，服从私法远甚于服从法庭之法——这恰恰是过去从奴隶制向自由转变的逆向过程中所发生的事情——在黑暗时代初期私法变得比公法更为强大——所有者欢迎那些既能保全其所有权又能增加其收益安全性的变革——今日非所有者将欢迎任何能维持其作为挣取工资阶级、却能增加其工资与安全感且无须坚持没收所有者财产的举措。
我打算在本章中阐明：在现代英格兰，共同构成了促成社会变革之几乎全部势力的三种利益集团，如何必然全都在漂向奴役国家。
在这三种利益集团中，前两者代表了改革者——第三者代表了有待被改革的人民。
这三种利益集团是：第一，“社会主义者”，即沿着阻力最小路线开展工作的理论改革者；第二，“务实派”，作为“务实”改革者，他依赖于自身的短视，因而在今日成为一支强大的力量；而第三种利益集团则是变革为其而推行、且被强加于其身上的庞大无产阶级大众。*他们*最有可能接受什么、*他们*将对新制度做出何种反应，乃是所有因素中最为重要的，因为他们正是这项工作所依凭且在其身上施加工作的材料。

===Original===

---
(1) Of the “socialist” reformer:
I say that men attempting to achieve collectivism or socialism as the remedy for the evils of the capitalist state find themselves drifting not towards a collectivist state at all, but towards a servile state.
The socialist movement, the first of the three factors in this drift, is itself made up of two kinds of men: there is (a) the man who regards the public ownership of the means of production (and the consequent compulsion of all citizens to work under the direction of the state) as the only feasible solution of our modern social ills. There is also (b) the man who loves the collectivist ideal in itself, who does not pursue it so much because it is a solution of modern capitalism, as because it is an ordered and regular form of society which appeals to him in itself. He loves to consider the ideal of a state in which land and capital shall be held by public officials who shall order other men about and so preserve them from the consequences of *their* vice, ignorance, and folly.
These types are perfectly distinct, in many respects antagonistic, and between them they cover the whole socialist movement.
Now imagine either of these men at issue with the existing state of capitalist society and attempting to transform it. Along what line of least resistance will either be led?
(a) The first type will begin by demanding the confiscation of the means of production from the hands of their present owners, and the vesting of them in the state. But wait a moment. That demand is an exceedingly hard thing to accomplish. The present owners have between them and confiscation a stony moral barrier. It is what *most* men would call the moral basis of property (the instinct that property is a *right*), and what *all* men would admit to be at least a deeply rooted tradition. Again, they have behind them the innumerable complexities of modern ownership.
To take a very simple case. Decree that all common lands enclosed since so late a date as 1760 shall revert to the public. There you have a very moderate case and a very defensible one. But conceive for a moment how many small freeholds, what a nexus of obligation and benefit spread over millions, what thousands of exchanges, what purchases made upon the difficult savings of small men such a measure would wreck!

===Chinese===

---
(一) 关于“社会主义”改革者：
我断言：企图推行集体主义或社会主义以作为资本主义国家之弊害的救治之道的人们，发现自己根本不是在漂向集体主义国家，而是在漂向奴役国家。
在这一漂移过程的三项因素中，社会主义运动作为第一项因素，其本身是由两类人构成的：有这样一类人(a)，他将生产资料的公有制（以及随之而来的强迫所有公民在国家指挥下劳动）视为救治我们现代社会弊病的唯一可行出路。此外还有一类人(b)，他喜爱集体主义理想本身；他追求该理想与其说是为了解决现代资本主义的矛盾，不如说是因为集体主义本身是一种有序且规整的社会形态，对其具有吸引力。他热衷于构想这样一个理想国家：由公职官员占有土地与资本，由这些官员调遣其他人，从而使其他人免受*他们自身*的恶习、愚昧与荒唐所带来的恶果。
这两种类型截然不同，在许多方面甚至彼此对立，两者合起来便涵盖了整个社会主义运动。
现在设想这两类人中的任何一位面临着资本主义社会的现存状态并企图改造它。任何一位改革者将被引向何种阻力最小的路线？
(a) 第一类人一开始将要求从现有所有者手中没收生产资料，并将其归属于国家。但且慢。那一要求是一件极其难以办到的事情。在现有所有者与没收之间，横亘着一道坚硬的道德屏障。这便是*绝大多数*人所称的财产的道德基石（即认定财产是一项*权利*的本能），也是*所有*人皆承认的至少是一项根深蒂固的传统。再者，在他们背后还存在着现代所有制错综复杂的千丝万缕的联系。
举一个极简单的例子。颁布法令宣布：自 1760 年如此晚近的年代以来所圈占的一切公地皆应归还公众。这是一个非常温和且完全站得住脚的主张。但设想片刻：这样一项举措将会摧毁多少小自有地，将瓦解遍及数百万人的何等庞大的义务与利益网络，将破灭多少笔交易，将摧毁多少依靠小人物艰难积攒的微薄储蓄所达成的购买！

===Original===

It is conceivable, for, in the moral sphere, society can do anything to society; but it would bring crashing down with it twenty times the wealth involved and all the secure credit of our community. In a word, the thing is, in the conversational use of that term, impossible. So your best type of socialist reformer is led to an expedient which I will here only mention﻿—as it must be separately considered at length later on account of its fundamental importance﻿—the expedient of “*buying out*” the present owner.
It is enough to say in this place that the attempt to “buy out” without confiscation is based upon an economic error. This I shall prove in its proper place. For the moment I assume it and pass on to the rest of my reformer’s action.
He does not confiscate, then; at the most he “buys out” (or attempts to “buy out”) certain sections of the means of production.
But this action by no means covers the whole of his motive. By definition the man is out to cure what he sees to be the great immediate evils of capitalist society. He is out to cure the destitution which it causes in great multitudes and the harrowing insecurity which it imposes upon all. He is out to substitute for capitalist society a society in which men shall all be fed, clothed, housed, and in which men shall not live in a perpetual jeopardy of their housing, clothing, and food.
Well, there is a way of achieving that without confiscation.
This reformer rightly thinks that the ownership of the means of production by a few has caused the evils which arouse his indignation and pity. But they have only been so caused on account of a combination of such limited ownership with universal freedom. The combination of the two is the very definition of the capitalist state. It is difficult indeed to dispossess the possessors. It is by no means so difficult (as we shall see again when we are dealing with the mass whom these changes will principally affect) to modify the factor of freedom.
You can say to the capitalist: “I desire to dispossess you, and meanwhile I am determined that your employees shall live tolerable lives.” The capitalist replies: “I refuse to be dispossessed, and it is, short of catastrophe, impossible to dispossess me. But if you will define the relation between my employees and myself, I will undertake particular responsibilities due to my position.

===Chinese===

这是可以设想的，因为在道德领域，社会可以对社会施加任何处置；但它将连带摧毁相当于所涉及财富二十倍的价值，摧毁我们共同体全部稳固的信用。简言之，在普通的口语表达中，该举措是“行不通”的。
因此，这位渴望没收的大善人发现自己寸步难行。在一切方向上，只要他稍稍触及资本主义的基础，他就面临着立即导致毁灭的威胁。资本主义的巨厦太庞大了，它与我们整个社会的物质生活纠缠得太深了，以至于任何直接击垮它的企图都将导致全体人民陷入饥馑与无政府状态。
于是，由于没收是不可行的，这位渴望行善的改革者被迫退后一步。他自忖：“如果我不能立刻把生产资料收归国有，我至少可以立刻改善受雇工人的命运。”
他放弃了夺取资本的企图，转而寻求对资本的使用施加限制。他放弃了剥夺所有者的方案，转而致力于为被剥夺所有权者争取某种法律保护。
这一转向具有决定性的意义。通过这一转向，改革者不知不觉地踏上了通向奴役国家的康庄大道。因为剥夺占有者是极其困难的；但（正如我们稍后在讨论将主要受这些变革影响的大众时所看到的那样）改变自由要素却绝非那么困难。
你可以对资本家说：“我渴望剥夺你的财产，与此同时我决心让你的雇员过上能够忍受的生活。”资本家回答道：“我拒绝被剥夺财产；若非发生灭顶灾难，剥夺我的财产是不可能的。但如果你愿意界定我和我的雇员之间的关系，我愿意承担由于我的地位而应尽的特殊责任。

===Original===

Subject the proletarian, as a proletarian, and because he is a proletarian, to special laws. Clothe me, the capitalist, as a capitalist, and because I am a capitalist, with special converse duties under those laws. I will faithfully see that they are obeyed; I will compel my employees to obey them, and I will undertake the new role imposed upon me by the state. Nay, I will go further, and I will say that such a novel arrangement will make my own profits perhaps larger and certainly more secure.”
This idealist social reformer, therefore, finds the current of his demand canalised. As to one part of it, confiscation, it is checked and barred; as to the other, securing human conditions for the proletariat, the gates are open. Half the river is dammed by a strong weir, but there is a sluice, and that sluice can be lifted. Once lifted, the whole force of the current will run through the opportunity so afforded it; there will it scour and deepen its channel; there will the main stream learn to run.
To drop the metaphor, all those things in the true socialist’s demand which are compatible with the servile state can certainly be achieved. The first steps towards them are already achieved. They are of such a nature that upon them can be based a further advance in the same direction, and the whole capitalist state can be rapidly and easily transformed into the servile state, satisfying in its transformation the more immediate claims and the more urgent demands of the social reformer whose ultimate objective indeed may be the public ownership of capital and land, but whose driving power is a burning pity for the poverty and peril of the masses.
When the transformation is complete there will be no ground left, nor any demand or necessity, for public ownership. The reformer only asked for it in order to secure security and sufficiency: he has obtained his demand.
Here are security and sufficiency achieved by another and much easier method, consonant with and proceeding from the capitalist phase immediately preceding it: there is no need to go further.

===Chinese===

让无产者作为无产者、且因为他是无产者，而服从特殊的法律。让我这个资本家作为资本家、且因为我是资本家，而在那些法律下承担特殊的对应义务。我将忠实地监督他们遵守法律；我将强迫我的雇员遵守法律，并将承担国家强加给我的新角色。不仅如此，我还要更进一步说：这种新颖的安排或许会使我的利润更大，且肯定会更稳固。”
因此，这位理想主义的社会改革者发现他的诉求潮流被疏导引流了。对于其诉求的一部分，即没收，大门被死死堵住；而对于另一部分，即确保无产阶级享有合乎人道的条件，大门却敞开着。河流的一半被坚固的滚水坝拦住了，但那里有一道水闸，且那道水闸是可以提起的。一旦提起，水流的全部力量将借着所赋予它的机会喷涌而出；它将在那里冲刷并加深河槽；主流将在那里奔腾流淌。
抛开隐喻不谈，真正社会主义者的诉求中凡是与奴役国家相容的内容，皆肯定可以实现。迈向它们的第一步已经完成了。它们具有这样的性质：可以在其基础之上沿着相同方向进一步推进，整个资本主义国家可以迅速而轻易地转变为奴役国家，在其转变中满足社会改革者更直接的主张与更紧迫的诉求——其最终目标固然可能是资本与土地的公有制，但其驱动力乃是对大众的贫困与危险的炽热同情。
当转变大功告成之时，公有制将不再保留任何立足之地，亦不再具有任何诉求或必要性。改革者原本要求公有制仅仅是为了换取安全与温饱：他已经得到了他的要求。
安全与温饱在此通过另一种容易得多的方法实现了，这种方法契合于紧接其前的资本主义阶段并从中脱胎而来：没有必要再往前走了。

===Original===

In this way the socialist whose motive is human good and not mere organisation is being shepherded in spite of himself *away* from his collectivist ideal and *towards* a society in which the possessors shall remain possessed, the dispossessed shall remain dispossessed, in which the mass of men shall still work for the advantage of a few, and in which those few shall still enjoy the surplus values produced by labour, but in which the special evils of insecurity and insufficiency, in the main the product of freedom, have been eliminated by the destruction of freedom.
At the end of the process you will have two kinds of men, the owners economically free, and controlling to their peace and to the guarantee of their livelihood the economically unfree non-owners. But that is the servile state.
---
(b) The second type of socialist reformer may be dealt with more briefly. In him the exploitation of man by man excites no indignation. Indeed, he is not of a type to which indignation or any other lively passion is familiar. Tables, statistics, an exact framework for life﻿—these afford him the food that satisfies his moral appetite; the occupation most congenial to him is the “running” of men: as a machine is run.
To such a man the collectivist ideal particularly appeals.
It is orderly in the extreme. All that human and organic complexity which is the colour of any vital society offends him by its infinite differentiation. He is disturbed by multitudinous things; and the prospect of a vast bureaucracy wherein the whole of life shall be scheduled and appointed to certain simple schemes deriving from the coordinate work of public clerks and marshalled by powerful heads of departments gives his small stomach a final satisfaction.
Now this man, like the other, would prefer to begin with public property in capital and land, and upon that basis to erect the formal scheme which so suits his peculiar temperament. (It need hardly be said that in his vision of a future society he conceives of himself as the head of at least a department and possibly of the whole state﻿—but that is by the way.) But while he would prefer to begin with a collectivist scheme ready-made, he finds in practice that he cannot do so.

===Chinese===

这样，动机在于造福人类而非单纯热衷组织的社会主义者，尽管违背其本意，却正被驱赶着*远离*其集体主义理想，而*迈向*这样一个社会：在其中占有者依然占有，被剥夺者依然被剥夺；在其中大众依然为少数人的利益而劳动，而那少数人依然享有劳动所生产的剩余价值；但在其中，主要作为自由之产物的不安全感与匮乏不均等特殊弊病，已经通过消灭自由而被根除了。
在这一进程的终点，你将拥有两类人：在经济上自由的所有者，以及受到所有者控制以维持和平并保障其生计的在经济上不自由的非所有者。但这正是奴役国家。
---
(b) 第二类社会主义改革者可以更简略地予以探讨。在他心中，人对人的剥削激不起任何义愤。事实上，他绝非义愤或任何其他鲜活激情所熟悉的那类人。图表、统计数据、生活的精准框架——这些为满足其道德胃口提供了食粮；最合乎其心意的职业便是“管辖”人类：犹如运转一台机器。
对这样的人而言，集体主义理想尤其具有吸引力。
它是极度井然有序的。构成任何充满活力的社会之鲜活色彩的全部人类与有机复杂性，因其无限的分化而触怒了他。他为纷繁芜杂的事物所扰；而一个庞大官僚机构的前景——在其中全部生活皆被编排并归入源于公职文员协调工作且由强力部门长官所统辖的某些简单计划之中——带给他狭小的格局以终极的满足。
现在，这个人同另一类人一样，更愿意从资本与土地的公有制开始，并在此基础之上建立起极其契合其特殊性情的形式化体系。（无须多言，在他对未来社会的设想中，他将自己构想为至少是一个部门的首长，甚至可能是整个国家的元首——但这不过是题外话。）然而，尽管他更愿意从一个现成的集体主义体系开始，他在实践中却发现自己无法做到这一点。

===Original===

He would have to confiscate just as the more hearty socialist would; and if that act is very difficult to the man burning at the sight of human wrongs, how much more difficult is it to a man impelled by no such motive force and directed by nothing more intense than a mechanical appetite for regulation?
He cannot confiscate or begin to confiscate. At the best he will “buy out” the capitalist.
Now, in his case, as in the case of the more human socialist, “buying out” is, as I shall show in its proper place, a system impossible of general application.
But all those other things for which such a man cares much more than he does for the socialisation of the means of production﻿—tabulation, detailed administration of men, the coordination of many efforts under one schedule, the elimination of all private power to react against his department, all these are immediately obtainable without disturbing the existing arrangement of society. With him, precisely as with the other socialist, what he desires can be reached without any dispossession of the few existing possessors. He has but to secure the registration of the proletariat; next to ensure that neither they in the exercise of their freedom, nor the employer in the exercise of his, can produce insufficiency or insecurity﻿—and he is content. Let laws exist which make the proper housing, feeding, clothing, and recreation of the proletarian mass be incumbent upon the possessing class, and the observance of such rules be imposed, by inspection and punishment, upon those whom he pretends to benefit, and all that he really cares for will be achieved.
To such a man the servile state is hardly a thing towards which he drifts, it is rather a tolerable alternative to his ideal collectivist state, which alternative he is quite prepared to accept and regards favourably. Already the greater part of such reformers who, a generation ago, would have called themselves “socialists” are now less concerned with any scheme for socialising capital and land than with innumerable schemes actually existing, some of them possessing already the force of laws, for regulating, “running,” and drilling the proletariat without trenching by an inch upon the privilege in implements, stores, and land enjoyed by the small capitalist class.
The so-called “socialist” of this type has not fallen into the servile state by a miscalculation.

===Chinese===

他必须像那位更热诚的社会主义者一样进行没收；而且，如果对于目睹人间不平而满腔怒火的人来说没收是一件极其困难的事，那么对于一个未受任何此类原动力驱使、仅凭对机械规制的胃口所支配的人来说，没收岂不是难上加难吗？
他无法进行没收，甚至无法着手没收。充其量，他将“买断赎买”资本家。
然而在他的情形中，正如在那位更具人情味的社会主义者的情形中一样，正如我将在相应章节中所证明的那样，“买断赎买”是一套不可能普遍适用的制度。
但是，这样一个人所挂念的远甚于生产资料公有化的所有其他事物——制表造册、对人员的巨细靡遗管理、在一张计划表下对众多努力的协调统筹、消除所有反抗其部门的私人力量——所有这一切皆可在丝毫不扰乱现有社会安排的情况下立刻获得。对他而言，正如对另一位社会主义者一样，他所渴望之物无须剥夺现存的少数占有者即可达到。他只需确保对无产阶级进行登记造册；接着确保无论是无产者行使其自由、还是雇主行使其自由，皆不能造成匮乏或不安全感——他就心满意足了。只要存在相关法律，规定占有阶级有责任为无产阶级大众提供恰当的住房、伙食、衣物与娱乐，并通过巡查和惩处，将对此类规章的遵守强加给那些他口口声声自称要造福的人身上，那么他真正挂念的一切便都实现了。
对这样的人而言，奴役国家几乎算不上是他被动漂向的事物，反倒成了对其理想集体主义国家的一种完全可以容忍的替代选择，他完全准备接受且赞赏这种替代选择。那些在上一代人本会自称为“社会主义者”的改革者，如今绝大多数早已不再关心任何将资本与土地公有化的方案，而是投身于实际存在的不可胜数的方案之中——其中某些方案已具有法律效力——旨在对无产阶级进行规制、“管辖”与操练，同时对那少数资本家阶级在工具、给养与土地上所享有的特权秋毫无犯。
这种类型的所谓“社会主义者”绝非因误判而跌入奴役国家的。

===Original===

He has fathered it; he welcomes its birth, he foresees his power over its future.
So much for the socialist movement, which a generation ago proposed to transform our capitalist society into one where the community should be the universal owner and all men equally economically free or unfree under its tutelage. Today their ideal has failed, and of the two sources whence their energy proceeded, the one is reluctantly, the other gladly, acquiescent in the advent of a society which is not socialist at all but servile.
---
(2) Of the *practical* reformer:
There is another type of reformer, one who prides himself on *not* being a socialist, and one of the greatest weight today. He also is making for the servile state. This second factor in the change is the “practical man”; and this fool, on account of his great numbers and determining influence in the details of legislation, must be carefully examined.
It is your “practical man” who says: “Whatever you theorists and doctrinaires may hold with regard to this proposal (which I support), though it may offend some abstract dogma of yours, yet *in practice* you must admit that it does good. If you had *practical* experience of the misery of the Jones’ family, or had done *practical* work yourself in Pudsey, you would have seen that a *practical* man,” etc.
It is not difficult to discern that the practical man in social reform is exactly the same animal as the practical man in every other department of human energy, and may be discovered suffering from the same twin disabilities which stamp the practical man where-ever found: these twin disabilities are an inability to define his own first principles and an inability to follow the consequences proceeding from his own action. Both these disabilities proceed from one simple and deplorable form of impotence, the inability to think.
Let us help the practical man in his weakness and do a little thinking for him.
As a social reformer he has of course (though he does not know it) first principles and dogmas like all the rest of us, and *his* first principles and dogmas are exactly the same as those which his intellectual superiors hold in the matter of social reform. The two things intolerable to him as a decent citizen (though a very stupid human being) are *insufficiency* and *insecurity*.

===Chinese===

他孕育了它；他欢迎它的诞生，他预见到了自己对其未来的支配权力。
关于社会主义运动就谈这么多：在上一代人中，它曾提议将我们的资本主义社会转变为共同体作为普遍所有者、且所有人在其监护下享有平等的经济自由或不自由的社会。今天，他们的理想已经失败了；在其能量所发源的两个源头中，一个勉强顺从、另一个欣然顺从于一个根本不是社会主义而是奴役性的社会的降临。
---
(二) 关于*务实派*改革者：
还有另一种类型的改革者，他以自己*不是*社会主义者而自豪，并在今日具有举足轻重的分量。他同样在走向奴役国家。变革中的这第二个因素便是“务实派（practical man）”；鉴于这个傻瓜人数众多且在立法细节中具有决定性影响，必须对其予以仔细剖析。
正是你的这位“务实派”会说：“无论你们这些理论家和教条主义者对这项（我所支持的）提案持何种态度，尽管它可能冒犯你们的某些抽象教条，但*在实践中*你们必须承认它带来了好处。如果你们对琼斯（Jones）一家的悲惨遭遇有*实际*经验，或者你们自己曾在普德西（Pudsey）做过*实际*工作，你们就会看到，一个*务实*的人……”等等。
不难看出，社会改革领域的务实派与人类活动其他任何部门的务实派完全是同一种动物，且可以发现他们患有在任何地方都可以见到的打在务实派身上的孪生残疾：这两大孪生残疾便是无力定义自身的首要原则，以及无力追踪自身行为所导致的后果。这两项残疾皆源于一种简单而可悲的无能——即无力思考。
让我们在务实派软弱之处施以援手，替他做一点思考。
作为一名社会改革者，他当然（尽管他自己并不知道）同我们其他人一样拥有首要原则和信条，且*他的*首要原则和信条与他的智识上司在社会改革问题上所持有的完全相同。作为一个正派公民（尽管是一个极其愚蠢的人），令他无法忍受的两件事是*匮乏不均*与*朝不保夕*。

===Original===

When he was “working” in the slums of Pudsey or raiding the proletarian Jones’s from the secure base of Toynbee Hall, what shocked the worthy man most was “unemployment” and “destitution”: that is, insecurity and insufficiency in flesh and blood.
Now, if the socialist who has thought out his case, whether as a mere organiser or as a man hungering and thirsting after justice, is led away from socialism and towards the servile state by the force of modern things in England, how much more easily do you not think the “practical man” will be conducted towards that same servile state, like any donkey to his grazing ground? To those dull and shortsighted eyes the immediate solution which even the beginnings of the servile state propose are what a declivity is to a piece of brainless matter. The piece of brainless matter rolls down the declivity, and the practical man lollops from capitalism to the servile state with the same inevitable ease. Jones has not got enough. If you give him something in charity, that something will be soon consumed, and then Jones will again not have enough. Jones has been seven weeks out of work. If you get him work “under our unorganised and wasteful system, etc.,” he may lose it just as he lost his first jobs. The slums of Pudsey, as the practical man knows by practical experience, are often unemployable. Then there are “the ravages of drink”: more fatal still the dreadful habit mankind has of forming families and breeding children. The worthy fellow notes that “as a practical matter of fact such men do not work unless you make them.”
He does not, because he cannot, coordinate all these things. He knows nothing of a society in which free men were once owners, nor of the cooperative and instinctive institutions for the protection of ownership which such a society spontaneously breeds. He “takes the world as he finds it”﻿—and the consequence is that whereas men of greater capacity may admit with different degrees of reluctance the general principles of the servile state, *he*, the practical man, positively gloats on every new detail in the building up of that form of society. And the destruction of freedom by inches (though he does not see it to be the destruction of freedom) is the one panacea so obvious that he marvels at the doctrinaires who resist or suspect the process.

===Chinese===

当他在普德西（Pudsey）的贫民窟“工作”，或突访无产者琼斯（Jones）那毫无隔热防寒的住处时，激怒他的是琼斯一家的孩子衣衫褴褛、琼斯一家缺煤少粮、琼斯一家没有工作，或者琼斯先生即使有工作，也时刻面临着下周可能丢掉工作的惊恐危险。
务实派渴望医治这些具体的溃疡。他不在乎什么抽象原则。他看到的是眼前具体的饥饿、疾病与寒冷。他高呼：“给他们面包！给他们衣服！给他们炉火！给他们一份稳定的工作！”
当有人向他指出：“为了给他们这些，你正在把他们登记造册，正在剥夺他们自由订约的权利，正在强迫他们接受雇主的管辖，正在建立一个奴役国家”时，务实派愤怒地斥责道：“教条！空谈！冷血的理论！当琼斯一家正在挨饿受冻时，你们却在谈论什么抽象的自由！”他不能理解，因为他无法将所有这些事情协调统筹起来。他看不到：正是他那些善意的、零碎的、“务实”的修修补补，正在一砖一瓦地构筑起奴役大厦的坚实墙体。

===Original===

It has been necessary to waste so much time on this deplorable individual because the circumstances of our generation give him a peculiar power. Under the conditions of modern exchange a man of that sort enjoys great advantages. He is to be found as he never was in any other society before our own, possessed of wealth, and political as never was any such citizen until our time. Of history with all its lessons; of the great schemes of philosophy and religion, of human nature itself he is blank.
The practical man left to himself would not produce the servile state. He would not produce anything but a welter of anarchic restrictions which would lead at last to some kind of revolt.
Unfortunately, he is not left to himself. He is but the ally or flanking party of great forces which he does nothing to oppose, and of particular men, able and prepared for the work of general change, who use him with gratitude and contempt. Were he not so numerous in modern England, and, under the extraordinary conditions of a capitalist state, so economically powerful, I would have neglected him in this analysis. As it is, we may console ourselves by remembering that the advent of the servile state, with its powerful organisation and necessity for lucid thought in those who govern, will certainly eliminate him.
---
Our reformers, then, both those who think and those who do not, both those who are conscious of the process and those who are unconscious of it, are making directly for the servile state.
---
(3) What of the third factor? What of the people about to be reformed? What of the millions upon whose carcasses the reformers are at work, and who are the subject of the great experiment? Do they tend, as material, to accept or to reject that transformation from free proletarianism to servitude which is the argument of this book?
The question is an important one to decide, for upon whether the material is suitable or unsuitable for the work to which it is subjected, depends the success of every experiment making for the servile state.
The mass of men in the capitalist state is proletarian. As a matter of definition, the actual number of the proletariat and the proportion that number bears to the total number of families in the state may vary, but must be sufficient to determine the general character of the state before we can call that state *capitalist*.

===Chinese===

在这种可悲的傻瓜身上浪费这么多时间是必要的，因为正是由于他在现代议会中的庞大数量以及他在中产阶级选民中的普遍存在，才使得将现代英格兰引向奴役国家的立法得以顺利通过。
资本家操纵着他；官僚牵引着他；而他自以为在行善积德。
于是，理论社会主义者与短视务实派这两大改革势力，在事实层面上皆顺应了同一历史潮流。
现在让我们转向这幅图景中第三个、也是最重要的因素：被改革的人民大众。
---
(三) 关于无产阶级大众：
无论改革者有何种理想或缺乏何种理想，他们所操作的材料乃是人民。
现代资本主义国家的大众是无产阶级。作为资本主义本质的产物，这一阶级在政治上是自由的，但在经济上被剥夺了生产资料。
他们对这种事态作何反应？他们将如何面对走向奴役国家的趋势？
在任何资本主义国家中，无产阶级对社会变革的态度皆取决于三项基本因素：第一，他们对财产与经济自由的记忆或传统；第二，个体逃脱无产阶级境况的投机机会；第三，也是最重要的，对安全与温饱的本能渴望。

===Original===

But, as we have seen, the capitalist state is not a stable, and therefore not a permanent, condition of society. It has proved ephemeral; and upon that very account the proletariat in any capitalist state retains to a greater or less degree some memories of a state of society in which its ancestors were possessors of property and economically free.
The strength of this memory or tradition is the first element we have to bear in mind in our problem, when we examine how far a particular proletariat, such as the English proletariat today, is ready to accept the servile state, which would condemn it to a perpetual loss of property and of all the free habit which property engenders.
Next be it noted that under conditions of freedom the capitalist class may be entered by the more cunning or the more fortunate of the proletariat class. Recruitment of the kind was originally sufficiently common in the first development of capitalism to be a standing feature in society and to impress the imagination of the general. Such recruitment is still possible. The proportion which it bears to the whole proletariat, the chance which each member of the proletariat may think he has of escaping from his proletarian condition in a particular phase of capitalism such as is ours today, is the second factor in the problem.
The third factor, and by far the greatest of all, is the appetite of the dispossessed for that security and sufficiency of which capitalism, with its essential condition of freedom, has deprived them.
Now let us consider the interplay of these three factors in the English proletariat as we actually know it at this moment. That proletariat is certainly the great mass of the state: it covers about nineteen-twentieths of the population﻿—if we exclude Ireland, where, as I shall point out in my concluding pages, the reaction against capitalism, and therefore against its development towards a servile state, is already successful.
---
As to the first factor, it has changed very rapidly within the memory of men now living. The traditional rights of property are still strong in the minds of the English poor. All the moral connotations of that right are familiar to them. They are familiar with the conception of theft as a wrong; they are tenacious of any scraps of property which they may acquire.

===Chinese===

但是，正如我们所见，资本主义国家并非一种稳定的、因而亦非一种永久性的社会状态。事实证明它是短暂易逝的；正是由于这个缘故，任何资本主义国家的无产阶级都在或多或少的程度上保留着某种社会状态的记忆：在那种社会状态中，他们的祖先是财产的占有者且在经济上享有自由。
当我们考察某一特定无产阶级（如今日的英国无产阶级）在多大程度上准备接受奴役国家（这将把他们判处为永久丧失财产以及财产所孕育的全部自由习惯）时，这种记忆或传统的强弱是我们必须铭记的第一个要素。
其次应当看到：在自由的条件下，无产阶级中较为精明或较为幸运的人可以跨入资本家阶级。在资本主义初期发展中，这种补充机制曾十分普遍，以致成为社会的一个常设特征并深刻铭刻在大众的想象之中。此类跨越依然可能。它在整个无产阶级中所占的比例，以及在像我们今日这样的特定资本主义阶段中无产阶级的每一成员自认为逃脱其无产境况所拥有的机会，乃是该问题中的第二个因素。
第三个因素，也是迄今为止最重大的因素，便是被剥夺财产者对资本主义（伴随其本质的自由条件）所剥夺了他们的那种安全与温饱的渴求。
现在让我们来考察这三个因素在我们此时此刻实际了解的英国无产阶级之中的相互作用。那个无产阶级无疑构成了国家的主体大众：它覆盖了大约二十分之十九的人口——如果我们把爱尔兰排除在外的话；在爱尔兰，正如我将在结语各页中所指出的那样，针对资本主义、因而亦针对其走向奴役国家之演进的反抗，已经取得了成功。
---
关于第一个因素，在当今健在之人的记忆中，它已经发生了极其迅速的变化。财产的传统权利在英国穷人的心目中依然强烈。该权利的全部道德内涵对他们而言皆是熟悉的。他们熟悉将盗窃视为过错的观念；他们坚决捍卫自己所能获得的任何零星财产。

===Original===

They could all explain what is meant by ownership, by legacy, by exchange, and by gift, and even by contract. There is not one but could put himself in the position, mentally, of an owner.
But the actual experience of ownership, and the effect which that experience has upon character and upon one’s view of the state is a very different matter. Within the memory of people still living a sufficient number of Englishmen were owning (as small freeholders, small masters, etc.) to give to the institution of property coupled with freedom a very vivid effect upon the popular mind. More than this, there was a living tradition proceeding from the lips of men who could still bear living testimony to the relics of a better state of things. I have myself spoken, when I was a boy, to old labourers in the neighbourhood of Oxford who had risked their skins in armed protest against the enclosure of certain commons, and who had of course suffered imprisonment by a wealthy judge as the reward of their courage; and I have myself spoken in Lancashire to old men who could retrace for me, either from their personal experience the last phases of small ownership in the textile trade, or, from what their fathers had told them, the conditions of a time when small and well-divided ownership in cottage looms was actually common.
All that has passed. The last chapter of its passage has been singularly rapid. Roughly speaking, it is the generation brought up under the Education Acts of the last forty years which has grown up definitely and hopelessly proletarian. The present instinct, use, and meaning of property is lost to it: and this has had two very powerful effects, each strongly inclining our modern wage-earners to ignore the old barriers which lay between a condition of servitude and a condition of freedom. The first effect is this: that property is no longer what they seek, nor what they think obtainable for themselves. The second effect is that they regard the possessors of property as a class apart, whom they always must ultimately obey, often envy, and sometimes hate; whose moral right to so singular a position most of them would hesitate to concede, and many of them would now strongly deny, but whose position they, at any rate, accept as a known and permanent social fact, the origins of which they have forgotten, and the foundations of which they believe to be immemorial.

===Chinese===

他们人人都能解释所有权、遗赠、交换、赠与甚至契约的含义。没有一个人在心理上不能将自己代入所有者的位置。
然而，对所有权的实际经验，以及那种经验对人的性格和对国家观所产生的深刻影响，则完全是另一回事。在当今健在之人的记忆中，曾有足够数量的英国人拥有财产（作为小自有农、小作坊主等），从而赋予了与自由相结合的财产制度在大众心灵中极其生动的印记。不仅如此，当时还存在着一种活生生的传统，它出自那些仍能对更好事态之遗迹做出鲜活见证的人们的口中。我自己在孩提时代就曾与牛津周边的老劳工交谈过，他们曾在武装抗议圈占某些公地的斗争中甘冒生命危险，并理所当然地作为其勇气的奖赏而遭受了一位富有法官的牢狱之灾；我也曾在兰开夏郡与老人们交谈过，他们或是根据自身的亲身经历为我重现纺织业中小所有制的最后阶段，或是根据其父辈的讲述，为我重现家庭织机中小而分散良好的所有制切实普及的时代的状况。
所有这一切皆已逝去。其消逝的最后一章异常迅速。大体而言，正是在过去四十年《教育法》（Education Acts）下抚育成长起来的一代人，已经彻底且毫无希望地沦为了无产者。对他们而言，财产当下的本能、使用与意义皆已丧失：这产生了两种极其强大的后果，两者皆强烈促使我们现代的工资挣取者无视横亘在奴役状态与自由状态之间的旧有藩篱。第一种后果是：财产不再是他们所寻求之物，亦非他们认为自己所能企及之物。第二种后果是：他们将财产的占有者视为一个截然分开的阶级，他们自始至终必须服从这一阶级，常常嫉妒、有时甚至憎恨他们；对占有者享有如此特殊地位的道德权利，他们大多数人将犹豫不决不敢承认，许多人如今更会强烈否认，但无论如何，他们将这一地位作为已知的持久社会事实予以接受——他们早已遗忘了这一事实的起源，且认定其根基自古有之。

===Original===

To sum up: The attitude of the proletariat in England today (the attitude of the overwhelming majority, that is, of English families) towards property and towards that freedom which is alone obtainable through property is no longer an attitude of experience or of expectation. They think of themselves as wage-earners. To increase the weekly stipend of the wage-earner is an object which they vividly appreciate and pursue. To make him cease to be a wage earner is an object that would seem to them entirely outside the realities of life.
What of the second factor, the gambling chance which the capitalist system, with its necessary condition of freedom, of the legal power to bargain fully, and so forth, permits to the proletarian of escaping from his proletariat surroundings?
Of this gambling chance and the effect it has upon men’s minds we may say that, while it has not disappeared, it has very greatly lost in force during the last forty years. One often meets men who tell one, whether they are speaking in defence of or against the capitalist system, that it still blinds the proletarian to any common consciousness of class, because the proletarian still has the example before him of members of his class, whom he has known, rising (usually by various forms of villainy) to the position of capitalist. But when one goes down among the working men themselves, one discovers that the hope of such a change in the mind of any individual worker is now exceedingly remote. Millions of men in great groups of industry, notably in the transport industry and in the mines, have quite given up such an expectation. Tiny as the chance ever was, exaggerated as the hopes in a lottery always are, that tiny chance has fallen in the general opinion of the workers to be negligible, and that hope which a lottery breeds is extinguished. The proletarian now regards himself as definitely proletarian, nor destined within human likelihood to be anything but proletarian.

===Chinese===

总而言之：今日英格兰无产阶级（亦即绝大多数英格兰家庭）对于财产以及唯有通过财产方能获得的自由的态度，已不再是一种具备亲身经验或抱持切实行指望的态度。他们将自己纯粹视作工资挣取者。增加工资挣取者的每周薪酬，是一个他们极其真切地领会并全力追求的目标。而让他不再作为一名工资挣取者，在他们看来则是一件完全脱离生活实际的空想。
至于第二个因素——资本主义制度（连同其本质的自由条件、完全订立契约的法定权力等等）赋予无产者逃脱其无产阶级环境的博彩式投机机会，情况又如何呢？
关于这种博彩式投机机会及其在人们心灵中所产生的效果，我们可以说：尽管它并未完全绝迹，但在过去四十年里，它的力量已经大打折扣。人们经常遇到这样的人，不论他们是在为资本主义制度辩护还是反对资本主义，他们都会告诉你：资本主义依然通过蒙蔽无产者而阻碍了阶级共同意识的形成，因为无产者眼前依然树立着榜样——那些他们所熟知的本阶级成员（通常通过形形色色的卑劣勾当）跃升到了资本家的地位。然而，当你深入工人们自身中间时，你会发现：在任何单个工人的头脑中，对这种转变的指望如今已极其渺茫。大工业部门的数百万劳工，尤其是交通运输业和采矿业中的工人们，已经完全放弃了这种指望。这种机会以往无论多么微小，彩票所催生的希望无论多么夸大，在工人们的普遍看法中，那点微小的机会如今已沦落为微不足道，而彩票所孕育的希望也早已彻底熄灭。无产者如今将自己明确界定为无产者，且在人间常理所及的范围内注定只能永远是无产者。

===Original===

---
These two factors, then, the memory of an older condition of economic freedom, and the effect of a hope individuals might entertain of escaping from the wage-earning class, the two factors which might act most strongly *against* the acceptation of the servile state by that class, have so fallen in value that they offer but little opposition to the third factor in the situation which is making so strongly *for* the servile state, and which consists in the necessity all men acutely feel for sufficiency and for security. It is this third factor alone which need be seriously considered today, when we ask ourselves how far the material upon which social reform is working, that is, the masses of the people, may be ready to accept the change.
The thing may be put in many ways. I will put it in what I believe to be the most conclusive of all.
If you were to approach those millions of families now living at a wage, with the proposal for a contract of service for life, guaranteeing them employment at what each regarded as his usual full wage, how many would refuse?
Such a contract would, of course, involve a loss of freedom: a life-contract of the kind is, to be accurate, no contract at all. It is the negation of contract and the acceptation of status. It would lay the man that undertook it under an obligation of forced labour, coterminous and coincident with his power to labour. It would be a permanent renunciation of his right (if such a right exists) to the surplus values created by his labour. If we ask ourselves how many men, or rather how many families, would prefer freedom (with its accompaniments of certain insecurity and possible insufficiency) to such a life-contract, no one can deny that the answer is: “Very few would refuse it.” That is the key to the whole matter.
What proportion would refuse it no one can determine; but I say that even as a voluntary offer, and not as a compulsory obligation, a contract of this sort which would for the future destroy contract and re-erect status of a servile sort would be thought a boon by the mass of the proletariat today.
Now take the truth from another aspect﻿—by considering it thus from one point of view and from another we can appreciate it best﻿—of what are the mass of men now most afraid in a capitalist state? Not of the punishments that can be inflicted by a court of law, but of “the sack.”

===Chinese===

---
因此，这两个因素——对较古老经济自由状态的记忆，以及个体可能抱有的逃离工资挣取阶级的希望——这两个本可能最有力地*反对*该阶级接受奴役国家的因素，其分量已大为贬损，以致它们对局势中正如此有力地*促成*奴役国家的第三个因素几乎构不成任何阻碍；而这第三个因素便在于人人皆切身体会到的对温饱与安全的必需。正是这第三个因素，在今日当我们自问社会改革所作用的材料（即人民大众）在多大程度上准备好接受这种变革时，唯有它才需要予以严肃考量。
这一问题可以用多种方式表述。我将以我认为最无可辩驳的方式予以表述。
如果你走向如今依靠工资生活的数百万家庭，提出一份终身雇用契约，向他们保证提供每个人所认为的常规全额工资的就业保障，请问会有多少家庭予以拒绝？
这种契约当然会涉及自由的丧失：严格而言，这种终身契约根本不是契约。它是对契约的否定，是对身份的接纳。它将使承担该契约的人背负强制劳动的义务，只要他具备劳动能力就必须服劳役。这将意味着永久放弃对其劳动所创造的剩余价值的权利（如果存在这种权利的话）。如果我们自问，有多少人，或者更确切地说有多少家庭，会偏爱自由（连同其伴随的必然不安全与可能匮乏）而拒绝这样一份终身契约？没有人能够否认，答案是：“极少有人会拒绝。”这便是整个问题的关键所在。
究竟有多大比例的人会拒绝，没有人能够确定；但我敢断言，哪怕作为一项自愿的要约而非强制性义务，这样一份在未来摧毁契约并重建奴役性身份的契约，在今日广大无产阶级大众看来将被视为天赐的恩惠。
现在从另一个侧面来看待这一真理——通过从不同角度进行考察，我们能最好地把握它——在资本主义国家中，广大群众如今最害怕什么？绝非法庭所能施加的刑罚，而是“被炒鱿鱼（the sack）”。

===Original===

You may ask a man why he does not resist such and such a legal infamy; why he permits himself to be the victim of fines and deductions from which the Truck Acts specifically protect him; why he cannot assert his opinion in this or that matter; why he has accepted, without a blow, such and such an insult.
Some generations ago a man challenged to tell you why he forswore his manhood in any particular regard would have answered you that it was because he feared punishment at the hands of the law; today he will tell you that it is because he fears unemployment.
Private law has for the second time in our long European story overcome public law, and the sanctions which the capitalist can call to the aid of his private rule, by the action of his private will, are stronger than those which the public courts can impose.
In the seventeenth century a man feared to go to Mass lest the judges should punish him. Today a man fears to speak in favour of some social theory which he holds to be just and true lest his master should punish him. To deny the rule of public powers once involved public punishments which most men dreaded, though some stood out. To deny the rule of private powers involves today a private punishment against the threat of which very few indeed dare to stand out.
Look at the matter from yet another aspect. A law is passed (let us suppose) which increases the total revenue of a wage-earner, or guarantees him against the insecurity of his position in some small degree. The administration of that law requires, upon the one hand, a close inquisition into the man’s circumstances by public officials, and, upon the other hand, the administration of its benefits by that particular capitalist or group of capitalists whom the wage-earner serves to enrich. Do the servile conditions attaching to this material benefit prevent a proletarian in England today from preferring the benefit to freedom? It is notorious that they do not.
No matter from what angle you approach the business, the truth is always the same. That great mass of wage-earners upon which our society now reposes understands as a present good all that will increase even to some small amount their present revenue and all that may guarantee them against those perils of insecurity to which they are perpetually subject.

===Chinese===

你或许会问一个人，他为什么不反抗这样或那样的法律劣行；为什么容许自己沦为罚款和扣款的牺牲品，而《实物工资法》（Truck Acts）明确保护他免遭这些侵害；为什么他不能在此事或彼事上坚持自己的见解；为什么他未经抗争就忍受了如此这般的侮辱。
几代人之前，若质问一个人为何在某个具体方面背弃其男子气概，他会回答你说那是因为他害怕遭受法律之手的惩罚；今天他会告诉你，那是因为他害怕失业。
私法在我们漫长的欧洲历史上第二次压倒了公法；而资本家通过其私人意志的行使为其私人统治所能调动的制裁力量，比公立法院所能施加的更为强大。
在十七世纪，一个人害怕去参加天主教弥撒（Mass），生怕法官惩罚他。今天，一个人害怕公开支持他认为公正且真实的某种社会理论，生怕他的主人惩罚他。违抗公共权力的统治曾经伴随着多数人所畏惧的公开惩罚，尽管有些人挺身抗争。违抗私人权力的统治在今日伴随着私人惩罚，在这一威胁面前，真正敢于挺身抗争的人寥寥无几。
从又一个侧面来看待这个问题。假定通过了一项法律，增加了工资挣取者的总收入，或在微小程度上保障其免受地位不安全之苦。该法律的实施一方面要求公职官员对该人的境况进行严密审查；另一方面，其福利的实施则通过该工资挣取者为其效力谋利的具体资本家或资本家集团来进行。附着于这种物质福利之上的奴役条件，难道会阻碍今日英格兰的无产者宁要福利而不要自由吗？众所周知，它们绝不会阻碍。
无论你从哪个角度来审视这件事，真理始终如一。我们社会如今赖以立足的庞大工资挣取大众，把凡是能在哪怕微小程度上增加其现有收入、凡是能保障他们免受其时刻面临的不安全威胁的一切事物，都理解为切实的善好。

===Original===

They understand and welcome a good of this kind, and they are perfectly willing to pay for that good the corresponding price of control and enregimentation, exercised in gradually increasing degree by those who are their paymasters.
---
It would be easy by substituting superficial for fundamental things, or even by proposing certain terms and phrases to be used in the place of terms and phrases now current﻿—it would be easy, I say, by such methods to ridicule or to oppose the prime truths which I am here submitting. They none the less remain truths.
Substitute for the term “employee” in one of our new laws the term “serf,” even do so mild a thing as to substitute the traditional term “master” for the word “employer,” and the blunt words might breed revolt. Impose of a sudden the full conditions of a servile state upon modern England, and it would certainly breed revolt. But my point is that when the foundations of the thing have to be laid and the first great steps taken, there is no revolt; on the contrary, there is acquiescence and for the most part gratitude upon the part of the poor. After the long terrors imposed upon them through a freedom unaccompanied by property, they see, at the expense of losing a mere legal freedom, the very real prospect of *having enough* and *not losing it*.
All forces, then, are making for the servile state in this the final phase of our evil capitalist society in England. The generous reformer is canalised towards it; the ungenerous one finds it a very mirror of his ideal; the herd of “practical” men meet at every stage in its inception the “practical” steps which they expected and demanded; while that proletarian mass upon whom the experiment is being tried have lost the tradition of property and of freedom which might resist the change, and are most powerfully inclined to its acceptance by the positive benefits which it confers.
It may be objected that however true all this may be, no one can, upon such theoretical grounds, regard the servile state as something really approaching us. We need not believe in its advent (we shall be told) until we see the first effects of its action.
To this I answer that the first effects of its action are already apparent. The servile state is, in industrial England today, no longer a menace but something in actual existence. It is in process of construction.

===Chinese===

他们理解并欢迎此类善好，且他们完全愿意为此类善好支付相应的代价——即接受由其发薪人以逐渐递增的程度所实施的控制与整编管束。
---
通过以表面事物取代根本事物，甚至通过提议使用某些词语和短语来取代现行流通的词语和短语——我说，通过此类方法很容易嘲讽或反对我在此提出的根本真理。然而，它们依然是真理。
在我们的一部新法律中，若以“农奴”一词取代“雇员”一词，甚至做一件温和的事——以传统的“主人”一词取代“雇主”一词，这些直白的字眼便可能滋生反抗。若在现代英格兰骤然推行奴役国家的全部条件，肯定会激起反抗。但我的论点在于：当必须奠定该事物的基石并迈出最初的伟大步伐时，并没有反抗；恰恰相反，在穷人一方存在着顺从，且在绝大多数情况下甚至满怀感激。在经历了伴随无财产之自由所强加给他们的漫长恐怖之后，以丧失单纯的法律自由为代价，他们看到了*享有温饱*且*不再失去它*的极其真实的指望。
因此，在英格兰我们邪恶的资本主义社会这最后的阶段中，一切势力皆在趋向奴役国家。慷慨的改革者被引向它；狭隘吝啬的改革者在其中看到了自身理想的逼真倒影；“务实派”这群从众之徒在其开端的每一个阶段都碰到了他们所预期和要求的“务实”步骤；而在其身上进行试验的无产阶级大众，则已经丧失了本可抵抗这种变革的财产与自由传统，并因它所赋予的切实现实利益而极具倾向性地予以接纳。
或许有人会反驳说：无论这一切多么真确，任何人都不能根据此类理论依据将奴役国家视为切实迫近我们的事物。我们（人们将告诉我们）在亲眼目睹其行动的最初后果之前，无须相信它的降临。
对此我回答道：其行动的最初后果早已显露无遗。在今日工业化的英格兰，奴役国家已不再是一种威胁，而是某种切实存在的事物。它正在建设的过程之中。

===Original===

The first main lines of it are already plotted out; the cornerstone of it is already laid.
To see the truth of this I will next consider servile laws and projects of law, the first of which we already suffer, while the last will pass from project to positive statute in due process of time.

===Chinese===

它的最初主要轮廓已被勾勒就绪；它的奠基石已然安放落成。
为看清这一真理，我接下来将考察奴役性法律与法律草案：其中的第一批我们已然在忍受，而最后一批则将在适当时机从草案演进为正式法令。

### Appendix on “Buying-Out” / 附录：关于“买断赎买”
===Original===

An appendix showing that the collectivist proposal to “buyout” the capitalist in lieu of expropriating him is vain.
There is an impression abroad among those who propose to expropriate the capitalist class for the benefit of the state, but who appreciate the difficulties in the way of direct confiscation, that by spreading the process over a sufficient number of years and pursuing it after a certain fashion bearing all the outward appearances of a purchase, the expropriation could be effected without the consequences and attendant difficulties of direct confiscation. In other words, there is an impression that the state could “buyout” the capitalist class without their knowing it, and that in a sort of painless way this class can be slowly conjured out of existence.
The impression is held in a confused fashion by most of those who cherish it, and will not bear a clear analysis.
It is impossible by any jugglery to “buyout” the universality of the means of production without confiscation.
To prove this, consider a concrete case which puts the problem in the simplest terms:﻿—
A community of twenty-two families lives upon the produce of two farms, the property of only two families out of that twenty-two.
The remaining twenty families are proletarian. The two families, with their ploughs, stores, land, etc., are capitalist.
The labour of the twenty proletarian families applied to the land and capital of these two capitalist families produces 300 measures of wheat, of which 200 measures, or 10 measures each, form the annual support of the twenty proletarian families; the remaining 100 measures are the surplus value retained as rent, interest, and profit by the two capitalist families, each of which has thus a yearly income of 50 measures.
The state proposes to produce, after a certain length of time, a condition of affairs such that the surplus values shall no longer go to the two capitalist families, but shall be distributed to the advantage of the whole community, while it, the state, shall itself become the unembarrassed owner of both farms.
Now capital is accumulated with the object of a certain return as the reward of accumulation. Instead of spending his money, a man saves it with the object of retaining as the result of that saving a certain yearly revenue. The measure of this does not fall in a particular society at a particular time below a certain level.

===Chinese===

附录：旨在证明集体主义者提议“买断赎买”资本家以取代直接剥夺其财产的企图乃是徒劳的。
在那些提议为了国家利益剥夺资本家阶级、却又深知直接没收之道所面临的困难的人们中间，流传着一种印象：认为通过将这一进程分散在足够漫长的年份中、并以某种带有购买全部外表的形式予以推行，便可在不引发直接没收之后果及伴随困难的情况下实现剥夺。换言之，流传着这样一种印象：国家可以在资本家阶级不知不觉之中将其“买断赎买”，且能以某种无痛的方式慢慢将这一阶级从存在中变走。
绝大多数抱持这种印象的人是以一种混乱的方式看待它的，它经不起明晰的剖析。
通过任何手法在不实施没收的情况下“买断赎买”生产资料的普遍性，是绝不可能做到的。
为证明这一点，设想一个将问题置于最简单条件下的具体案例：——
一个由二十二个家庭构成的共同体，依靠两个农场的产出生活，这两个农场仅属于那二十二个家庭中的两个家庭所有。
其余二十个家庭皆为无产者。那两个家庭连同其犁具、给养、土地等，则是资本家。
二十个无产家庭的劳动施加于这两个资本家家庭的土地和资本之上，共生产出 300 量度的小麦；其中 200 量度（每个家庭 10 量度）构成二十个无产家庭的年度生计；其余 100 量度则是作为地租、利息和利润由两个资本家家庭保留的剩余价值，每个资本家家庭因而享有 50 量度的年收入。
国家提议在经过一段时间之后促成这样一种事态：剩余价值不再归两个资本家家庭所有，而是为了整个共同体的利益进行分配，而国家自身则成为两个农场的无负担所有者。
现在，资本的积累是为了获得一定的报酬作为积累的奖赏。一个人不把钱花掉，而是存起来，目的是通过这种节约保留一定的年收益。在特定社会的特定时期，这一收益的衡量尺度不会跌落至某一水平之下。

===Original===

In other words, if a man cannot get a certain minimum reward for his accumulation, he will not accumulate but spend.
What is called in economics “the law of diminishing returns” acts so that continual additions to capital, other things being equal (that is, the methods of production remaining the same), do not provide a corresponding increase of revenue. A thousand measures of capital applied to a particular area of natural forces will produce, for instance, 40 measures yearly, or 4 percent; but 2000 measures applied in the same fashion will not produce 80 measures. They will produce more than the thousand measures did, but not more in proportion; not double. They will produce, say, 60 measures, or 3 percent, upon the capital. The action of this universal principle automatically checks the accumulation of capital when it has reached such a point that the proportionate return is the least which a man will accept. If it falls below that he will spend rather than accumulate. The limit of this minimum in any particular society at any particular time gives the measure to what we call “the effective desire of accumulation.” Thus in England today it is a little over 3 percent. The minimum which limits the accumulation of capital is a minimum return of about one-thirtieth yearly upon such capital, and this we may call for shortness the “E.D.A.” of our society at the present time.
When, therefore, the capitalist estimates the full value of his possessions, he counts them in “so many years’ purchase.”6 And that means that he is willing to take in a lump sum down for his possessions so many times the yearly revenue which he at present enjoys. If his E.D.A. is one-thirtieth, he will take a lump sum representing thirty times his annual revenue.
So far so good. Let us suppose the two capitalists in our example to have an E.D.A. of one-thirtieth. They will sell to the state if the state can put up thirty times their surplus or “income,” i.e. 3000 measures of wheat.
Now, of course, the state can do nothing of the kind. The accumulations of wheat being already in the hands of the capitalists, and those accumulations amounting to much less than 3000 measures of wheat, the thing appears to be a deadlock.
But it is not a deadlock if the capitalist is a fool.

===Chinese===

换言之，如果一个人不能因其积累而获得某一最低限度的报酬，他就不会积累，而是会花掉。
经济学中所谓的“报酬递减规律”发挥着作用，使得在其他条件不变（即生产方式保持不变）的情况下，持续追加资本并不会带来成比例的收益增长。例如，施加于特定自然力领域的 1000 量度资本，每年将生产 40 量度，即 4% ；但以相同方式施加的 2000 量度资本，却不会生产 80 量度。它们生产的将多于那 1000 量度，但并非成比例地增加；并未翻倍。它们将生产，假定说， 60 量度，即相当于资本的 3% 。这一普遍原则的作用，在资本积累达到其边际收益是人们所能接受的最低限度时，便自动遏制了资本的进一步积累。若收益低于那一水平，人们宁愿消费也不愿积累。在任何特定社会的任何特定时期，这一最低限度的边界给出了我们所称的“有效积累欲望（effective desire of accumulation）”的衡量尺度。因此在今日的英格兰，它略高于 3% 。限制资本积累的最低限度，乃是对此类资本每年约三十分之一的最低收益；为简便起见，我们可以将其称为我们社会当下的“有效积累欲望（E.D.A.）”。
因此，当资本家估算其财产的全部价值时，他是以“若干年买价（years’ purchase）”6来计算的。这意味着他愿意按其目前享有的年收益的若干倍一次性全额收取现款来出让其财产。若其有效积累欲望为三十分之一，他将接受相当于其年收益三十倍的总额。
到此为止一切尚好。让我们假定我们示例中的两个资本家的有效积累欲望为三十分之一。如果国家能拿出其剩余或“收入”的三十倍，亦即 3000 量度的小麦，他们就会卖给国家。
当然，国家绝不可能拿出此类物品。小麦的积累早已握在资本家手中，且那些积累的总量远远少于 3000 量度的小麦，事情看似陷入了僵局。
但如果资本家是个傻瓜，事情就不会陷入僵局。

===Original===

The state can go to the capitalists and say: “Hand me over your farms, and against them I will give you guarantee that you shall be paid *rather more than* 100 measures of wheat a year for the thirty years. In fact, I will pay you half as much again until these extra payments amount to a purchase of your original stock.”
Out of what does this extra amount come? Out of the state’s power to tax.
The state can levy a tax upon the profits of both capitalists A and B, and pay them the extra with their own money.
In so simple an example it is evident that this “ringing of the changes” would be spotted by the victims, and that they would bring against it precisely the same forces which they would bring against the much simpler and more straightforward process of immediate confiscation.
But it is argued that in a complex state, where you are dealing with myriads of individual capitalists and thousands of particular forms of profit, the process can be masked.
There are two ways in which the state can mask its action (according to this policy). It can buy out first one small area of land and capital out of the general taxation and then another, and then another, until the whole has been transferred; or it can tax with peculiar severity certain trades which the rest who are left immune will abandon to their ruin, and with the general taxation plus this special taxation buy out those unfortunate trades which will, of course, have sunk heavily in value under the attack.
The second of these tricks will soon be apparent in any society, however complex; for after one unpopular trade had been selected for attack the trying on of the same methods in another less unpopular field will at once rouse suspicion.7
The first method, however, might have some chance of success, at least for a long time after it was begun, in a highly complex and numerous society were it not for a certain check which comes in of itself. That check is the fact that the capitalist only takes *more than* his old yearly revenue with the object of reinvesting the surplus.
I have a thousand pounds in Brighton railway stock, yielding me 3 percent: £30 a year. The government asks me to exchange my bit of paper against another bit of paper guaranteeing the payment of £50 a year, that is, an extra rate a year, for so many years as will represent over and above the regular interest paid a purchase of my stock.

===Chinese===

国家可以走向资本家并对他们说：“把你们的农场交给我，我将向你们提供以我的全部权力为担保的保证凭证，每年付给你们50量度的小麦，连续支付三十年。”
资本家若是个傻瓜就会接受这一提议。他将交出农场，并在三十年里每年继续获得50量度小麦。三十年结束时，国家将成为农场的所有者，而资本家将一无所获。
但资本家不是傻瓜。他深知：如果他在三十年中每年仅仅拿到50量度小麦并将其消费掉，那么在三十年结束时他就将陷入赤贫。在以往，他在拿到50量度小麦的同时还保留着农场，且农场每年会继续源源不断地产出50量度小麦，直至永远。
因此，除非国家每年支付的数额不仅能弥补其原有收入、而且能包含一笔额外的偿债基金——这笔基金按当前利率投资后足以在期限结束时重新恢复其原有资本——否则资本家绝不会同意这种交易。
换言之，国家必须每年付给他们70量度或80量度的小麦，而不是50量度。
但国家从何处弄到这额外的20量度或30量度小麦呢？
它无法从农场的剩余中弄到，因为全部剩余只有100量度，而资本家原有的收入就占了100量度。
国家必须通过向整个共同体征税来弄到这笔款项。
让我以我自身的经历为例。我在布莱顿铁路公司（Brighton railway）拥有1,000英镑（£1,000）的股票，给我带来3%的收益：即每年30英镑（£30）。政府要求我用我的这张纸片换取另一张纸片，保证在若干年里每年付给我50英镑（£50），亦即每年付给我一笔额外利息，其年限足以代表在支付常规利息之外购买我的股票。

===Original===

The government’s bit of paper promises to pay to the holder £50 a year for, say, thirty-eight years. I am delighted to make the exchange, not because I am such a fool as to enjoy the prospect of my property being extinguished at the end of thirty-eight years, but because I hope to be able to reinvest the extra £20 every year in something else that will bring me in 3 percent. Thus, at the end of the thirty-eight years I shall (or my heirs) be better off than I was at the beginning of the transaction, and I shall have enjoyed during its maturing my old £30 a year all the same.
The state can purchase thus on a small scale by subsidising purchase out of the general taxation. It can, therefore, play this trick over a small area and for a short time with success. But the moment this area passes a very narrow limit the “market for investment” is found to be restricted, capital automatically takes alarm, the state can no longer offer its paper guarantees save at an enhanced price. If it tries to turn the position by further raising taxation to what capital regards as “confiscatory” rates, there will be opposed to its action just the same forces as would be opposed to frank and open expropriation.
The matter is one of plain arithmetic, and all the confusion introduced by the complex mechanism of “finance” can no more change the fundamental and arithmetical principles involved than can the accumulation of triangles in an ordnance survey reduce the internal angles of the largest triangle to less than 180 degrees.8 In fine: *if you desire to confiscate, you must confiscate*.
You cannot outflank the enemy, as financiers in the city and sharpers on the racecourse outflank the simpler of mankind, nor can you conduct the general process of expropriation upon a muddleheaded hope that somehow or other something will come out of nothing in the end.
There are, indeed, two ways in which the state could expropriate without meeting the resistance that must be present against any attempt at confiscation. But the first of these ways is precarious, the second insufficient.
They are as follows:﻿—
(1) The state can promise the capitalist a larger yearly revenue than he is getting in the expectation that it, the state, can manage the business better than the capitalist, or that some future expansion will come to its aid.

===Chinese===

政府的这张纸片许诺在，假定说，三十八年里每年付给持有者50英镑（£50）。我欣喜若狂地进行了交换，这并非因为我是个愚蠢到会乐于看到自己的财产在三十八年后化为乌有的傻瓜，而是因为我指望每年能够把额外的20英镑（£20）重新投资于能够带给我3%收益的其他事业之中。于是，在三十八年结束时，我（或我的继承人）将比该交易开始时更为富有，且在此期间我照样享有了我原先的每年30英镑（£30）。
国家可以通过用一般税收补贴购买的方式，在此类小规模上进行赎买。因此，它可以在狭小的领域内且在短暂的时间里成功玩弄这种把戏。但一旦该领域越过极其狭窄的边界，“投资市场”便被发现受到限制，资本便自动警觉起来，国家除了以更高的价格外再也无法提供其纸面担保。如果国家企图通过进一步提高税率至资本所认定的“没收性”税率来打破僵局，其行动所遭遇的阻力，恰恰与公开直白的没收所遭遇的阻力一模一样。
这是一个纯粹的算术问题；“金融”的复杂机制所引入的一切混乱，根本无法改变所涉及的根本算术原理，正如大三角测量中三角形的累加绝不可能使最大三角形的内角和减少到小于180度一样。8 归根结底：*若你想没收，你就必须没收*。
你不可能像城里的金融家和赛马场上的骗子包抄愚笨平民那样去包抄敌人，你也不可能寄托于某种糊里糊涂的指望——指望最终能以某种方式无中生有——来推进普遍的没收进程。
诚然，国家有两种途径可以在不遭遇任何直接没收企图所必然引发的抵抗的情况下实现剥夺。但这第一种途径是靠不住的，第二种途径则是杯水车薪的。
它们如下所示：——
(1) 国家可以向资本家许诺一笔比他目前所获更大的年收益，其前提是指望国家能够比资本家更好地经营该业务，或者指望某种未来的扩张会施以援手。

===Original===

In other words, if the state makes a bigger profit out of the thing than the capitalist, it can buy out the capitalist just as a private individual with a similar business proposition can buy him out.
But the converse of this is that if the state has calculated badly, or has bad luck, it would find itself *endowing* the capitalists of the future instead of gradually extinguishing them.
In this fashion the state could have “socialised” without confiscation the railways of this country if it had taken them over fifty years ago, promising the then owners more than they were then obtaining. But if it had socialised the hansom cab in the nineties, it would now be supporting in perpetuity that worthy but extinct type the cab-owner (and his children forever) at the expense of the community.
(2) The second way in which the state can expropriate without confiscation is by annuity. It can say to such capitalists as have no heirs or care little for their fate if they have: “You have only got so much time to live and to enjoy your £30, will you take £50 until you die?” Upon the bargain being accepted the state will, in process of time, though not immediately upon the death of the annuitant, become an unembarrassed owner of what had been the annuitant’s share in the means of production. But the area over which this method can be exercised is a very small one. It is not of itself a sufficient instrument for the expropriation of any considerable field.
I need hardly add that as a matter of fact the so-called “socialist” and confiscatory measures of our time have nothing to do with the problem here discussed. The state is indeed confiscating, that is, it is taxing in many cases in such a fashion as to impoverish the taxpayer and is lessening his capital rather than shearing his income. But it is not putting the proceeds into the means of production. It is either using them for immediate consumption in the shape of new official salaries or handing them over to another set of capitalists.9
But these practical considerations of the way in which sham socialist experiments are working belong rather to my next section, in which I shall deal with the actual beginnings of the servile state in our midst.

===Chinese===

换言之，如果国家从该业务中获得的利润大于资本家，它就可以买断资本家，正如一个拥有类似商业方案的私人可以将其买断一样。
但其反面在于：如果国家计算失误，或者运气不佳，它就会发现自己是在*资助*未来的资本家，而不是逐渐消灭他们。
以这种方式，如果国家在五十年前接管本国的铁路，向当时的所有者许诺比他们当时所获更多的收益，国家本可以在不实施没收的情况下将铁路“社会化”。但如果国家在九十年代将双轮双座轻便马车（hansom cab）社会化，那么国家如今就不得不以共同体的代价永久供养马车主（以及他的子子孙孙）这一值得尊敬却已绝迹的类型。
(2) 国家在不实施没收的情况下实现剥夺的第二种途径是年金制（annuity）。国家可以对那些没有继承人、或者即使有继承人也对其命运漠不关心的资本家说：“你只有这么长的寿命来生存并享用你的30英镑（£30），你愿意接受每年50英镑（£50）直到你去世吗？”一旦该交易被接受，在时间推移中，尽管不是在年金领取者死亡时立即实现，国家将成为年金领取者原先在生产资料中所占份额的无负担所有者。然而，该方法所能适用的领域极其狭小。它本身绝不是剥夺任何广阔领域的充分工具。
我几乎无须补充：事实上，我们时代所谓的“社会主义”和没收性措施与此处所讨论的问题毫无关系。国家确实在实施没收——也就是说，国家在许多情形下的征税方式使得纳税人陷入贫困，且是在剥夺其资本而非仅仅剪取其收入。但国家并没有将这笔收益投入到生产资料之中。它要么将其以新设立的官员薪俸形式用于直接消费，要么将其转交给了另一群资本家。9
但是，关于虚假的社会主义试验如何运作的这些实践考量，毋宁归属于我的下一章；在下一章中，我将探讨奴役国家在我们中间的切实开端。

## IX The Servile State Has Begun / 第九章 奴役国家已经开始
===Original===

The manifestation of the servile state in law or proposals of law will fall into two sorts﻿—(a) Laws or proposals of law compelling the proletariat to work﻿—(b) Financial operations riveting the grip of capitalists more strongly upon society﻿—As to (a), we find it **already** at work in measures such as the Insurance Act and proposals such as compulsory arbitration, the enforcement of trades union bargains and the erection of “labour colonies,” etc., for the “unemployable”﻿—As to the second, we find that so-called “municipal” or “socialist” experiments in acquiring the means of production have **already** increased and are continually increasing the dependence of society upon the capitalist.
In this last division of my book I deal with the actual appearance of the servile state in certain laws and proposals now familiar to the industrial society of modern England. These are the patent objects, “laws and projects of laws,” which lend stuff to my argument, and show that it is based not upon a mere deduction, but upon an observation of things.
Two forms of this proof are evident: first, the laws and proposals which subject the *proletariat* to servile conditions; next, the fact that the *capitalist*, so far from being expropriated by modern “socialist” experiments, is being confirmed in his power.
I take these in their order, and I begin by asking in what statutes or proposals the servile state first appeared among us.
A false conception of our subject might lead one to find the origins of the servile state in the restrictions imposed upon certain forms of manufacture, and the corresponding duties laid upon the capitalist in the interest of his workmen. The Factory Laws, as they are in this country, would seem to offer upon this superficial and erroneous view a starting point. They do nothing of the kind; and the view *is* superficial and erroneous because it neglects the fundamentals of the case. What distinguishes the servile state is not the interference of law with the action of any citizen even in connection with industrial matters. Such interference may or may not indicate the presence of a servile status. It in no way indicates the presence of that status when it forbids a particular kind of human action to be undertaken by the citizen as a citizen.

===Chinese===

奴役国家在法律或法律草案中的显现分为两类——(a) 强迫无产阶级劳动的法律或法律草案——(b) 使资本家对社会的控制更加牢固的财政运作——关于(a)，我们发现它已经在诸如《保险法》等措施以及诸如强制仲裁、强制执行工会协议、为“无法就业者”建立“劳动教养营”等提案中开始发挥作用——关于第二类，我们发现所谓的“市政”或“社会主义”获取生产资料的试验已经并且正在不断加深社会对资本家的依赖
在本书的这最后一个篇章中，我将探讨奴役国家在现代英格兰工业社会如今所熟悉的某些法律与提案中的切实呈现。这些便是为我的论证提供论据的显明事实，即“法律与法律草案”，并表明我的论证并非基于单纯的推演，而是基于对事物的切实观察
这方面的证明形式显而易见有两种：首先是将无产阶级置于奴役状态的法律与提案；其次是资本家非但未被现代“社会主义”试验所剥夺，反而在其权力上得到了巩固这一事实
我按顺序论述这两者，并从追问奴役国家最初在何种法令或提案中出现在我们中间开始
对我们主题的错误理解可能会引导人们在对某些制造形式所施加的限制、以及为了工人的利益强加给资本家的相应责任中，去寻找奴役国家的起源。根据这种表面且错误的看法，本国的《工厂法》（Factory Laws）似乎提供了一个起点。它们根本没有提供任何此类起点；而该看法之所以是表面且错误的，是因为它忽略了事情的根本所在。区分奴役国家的标志，并非法律对任何公民行为的干预，哪怕这种干预与工业事务相关。此类干预可能预示着、也可能不预示着奴役身份的存在。当法律禁止公民作为公民从事某种特定的人类行为时，它决不标志着该身份的存在

===Original===

The legislator says, for instance, “You may pluck roses; but as I notice that you sometimes scratch yourself, I will put you in prison unless you cut them with scissors at least 122 millimetres long, and I will appoint one thousand inspectors to go round the country seeing whether the law is observed. My brother-in-law shall be at the head of the department at £2,000 a year.”
We are all familiar with that type of legislation. We are all familiar with the arguments for and against it in any particular case. We may regard it as onerous, futile, or beneficent, or in any other light, according to our various temperaments.
